- Hypothesis: the host export is the ceiling, at 40–80 GB/s aggregate → 45–90 ms per layer → 1.7–3.3 s per chunk regardless of chunk size; so (a) streamed prefill lands at 1.2–2.5K tok/s with 4K chunks, (b) the chunk size becomes the main lever (16K chunks → 5–10K tok/s if activations fit), and (c) the break-even prompt length vs the CPU path is ≈ 400–700 tokens. The bytes kt exports are checkpoint-identical (librarian, `fp4-moe.hpp`), so streamed-prefill NLL should match the D=0 CPU baseline (1.48741) to ≈ 1e-3.
- Procedure:
  1. `scripts/kt-export-bench.py` (E22a, one layer in the recipe image, no server): build the compact 176-expert `KTMoEWrapper` for layer 5 as `test-kt-one-layer.py` does; export every expert for `gpu_tp_count=2` into pinned host buffers; **byte-compare** rank slices against the safetensors shards (weights, `bf16 == e8m0 << 7`, gate/up order, w2 column slicing); time the export (sequential submits, one sync; groups of 8/16/32) → GB/s; time the GPU prep (swap-copy H2D to both cards concurrently + scale shift + `block_scale_interleave`) → per-layer streaming cost with and without write/H2D overlap.
  2. From (1), derive the per-chunk cost and the break-even length; pick the group size and the default `--kt-gpu-prefill-token-threshold`.
  3. Implement the streaming path in the overlay `kt_ep_wrapper.py` (E22b): POSIX-shm pinned host double buffer shared by both TP ranks, per-layer GPU slots, gloo barrier per group, GPU MoE over the compact CPU-expert ids with the existing FlashInfer runner; CPU path kept for chunks under the threshold and for decode.
  4. Serve, `verify.py` 16K, `nll-score.py` vs `nll-d0.json`, `bench-prefill.py` at 16K/32K with chunk sizes 4096/8192/16384 (E22c).
- Pass: (1) byte-identical export; (4) verify all-pass, |ΔNLL| < 0.002 vs D=0, prefill ≥ 1,000 tok/s at 16K (≥ 4.6× E19) with no decode regression (C1 ≥ 38 tok/s).
- Results, E22a (`evidence/e22-layerwise-prefill/e22a-export-bench-t16.json`, `-t8.json`, `e22a-kernel-check.json`; layer 5, 176 CPU experts, `gpu_tp_count=2`, kt-kernel 0.7.0.post4):
  - **Export is byte-identical** to the shards for compact experts 0, 1, 175 on both ranks (w13 `[gate; up]` rows, w2 column slice, scales `bf16 = e8m0 << 7`). One rank slice is 9.49 MiB per expert (my 9.96 MB estimate was 5 % high: 5.63 + 0.70 + 2.81 + 0.35 MiB); a layer is 3.26 GiB for both ranks.
  - **Export throughput: 67.6 ms per layer = 51.8 GB/s at 16 threads, 121.9 ms = 28.7 GB/s at 8 threads** → linear in threads, so it is core-bound (memcpy + scale conversion per thread ≈ 3.2 GB/s), not DRAM-bound. Group size (8/16/32/44 experts per sync) does not matter (68–69 ms).
  - H2D of a full layer to both cards concurrently: 36.7 ms (44 GiB/s per card, E4-consistent); + scale shift + interleave 38.4 ms.
  - Pipelined write(g+1) ‖ H2D(g): **87–93 ms per layer** (best G=32), i.e. only ~17 ms of the 37 ms H2D hides behind the export; serial would be 104 ms. With 8 export threads 141 ms.
  - **Staged layout == resident layout, bit for bit**, on both ranks (`_StreamedExpertSlots.load()` output vs tensors built the way SGLang's loader builds them: `[up; gate]`, E8M0 uint8, `block_scale_interleave`), and the FlashInfer SM120 fused MoE gives **exactly the same output** on the staged and the resident tensors (max |diff| 0) for 16 tokens × top-6 with one resident id per token masked to −1. Both are 0.0444 rel-L2 from the fp32 dequant reference — that is the GPU tier's existing MXFP8-activation error (kt's CPU path was 0.0038 in T3.6b), not something the streaming adds.
  - Projection (40 MoE layers, all streamed): ≈ 3.5 s of streaming per chunk, plus the all-GPU compute of the chunk (~2 s at 16K from the E8 dsv4-nvfp4 prefill rate) → 16K chunk ≈ 3,000 tok/s, 8K ≈ 1,900, 4K ≈ 1,100; break-even vs the 216 tok/s CPU path ≈ 750 tokens → default threshold 1,024. Hypothesis (a)–(c) hold so far; the export, not the links, is the ceiling, and it is the CPU cores.
- E22b implemented in the overlay (`kt_ep_wrapper.py`: `_LayerwisePrefillCoordinator`, `_HostSlice`, `_StreamedExpertSlots`; env `KT_GPU_PREFILL_MIN_TOKENS`, `KT_GPU_PREFILL_LOG`): full-layer granularity, one POSIX-shm pinned double buffer (2 × 2 ranks × 1.63 GiB = 6.5 GiB) created by rank 0 and `cudaHostRegister`ed by both, per-rank GPU staging 1.9 GiB (w13/w2/scales + bf16 landing zone + half-swap scratch), export of layer L+1 submitted right after layer L's GEMM, two gloo barriers per layer (buffer filled; buffer free), DMA on the compute stream (v1: no second staging set), FlashInfer runner called a second time per layer with compact ids. Serving knob in `serve-hybrid.sh`.
- Results, E22c (2026-09-22, first serve with `KT_GPU_PREFILL_MIN_TOKENS=1024 KT_GPU_PREFILL_LOG=1`, chunk 4096, everything else the E21 defaults; `evidence/e22-layerwise-prefill/`):
  - Load: coordinator up on both ranks (`host pinned 6.53 GiB (shared), GPU staging 2.27 GiB/rank`), decode graphs captured with 7.1–7.4 GB free (was ≈ 9.5 without the staging), `max_total_num_tokens` 1,905,664 — the KV pool did shrink but is nowhere near binding at 32K × 4 slots.
  - **Streaming cost: 4.66–5.13 s host-side per 4,096-token chunk (40 layers), 4.24 s for a 1,175-token chunk** — flat in tokens, as hypothesised, but 1.3× the 3.5 s projection (the per-layer export + two barriers run at ≈ 117 ms, not 87–93 as in the isolated bench: the serving process has kt's 16 spinning threads and the TP handshake beside it). Total per chunk ≈ 5.0 s → GPU compute of the chunk is only ≈ 0.3 s; the streaming *is* the chunk.
  - **Prefill 837 tok/s at 16K (TTFT 19.1 s, two runs 827/847) and 811 tok/s at 32K (TTFT 38.4 s)** vs 216 / TTFT 75 s in E19 → **3.9×**, under the ≥ 1,000 tok/s pass line for a 4K chunk (the projection said 1,100); 8K and 16K chunks are the obvious next step (5 s per chunk → ≈ 1,600 and ≈ 3,000 tok/s if the activation memory fits). `prefill-16k-chunk4096.json`, `prefill-32k-chunk4096.json`.
  - `verify.py` 16K: all pass, needles 3/3 at 13,463 tokens, TTFT 35.5 s (E21: 57.9 s). `verify-16k.json`.
  - **Quality: ΔNLL −0.00127 nats/token vs D=0 (z +0.35), pass.** But it is not bit-identical the way D=0 reruns are: mean |Δlogp| 0.125, sd 0.25, 148 tokens > 0.5 nats worse / 139 better of 4,795 — the same zero-mean signature as D=4 (0.132). Cause is known from E22a: the streamed experts now run through the GPU tier's MXFP8-activation kernel (rel-L2 0.0444 vs fp32) instead of kt's CPU path (0.0038), so the streamed prefill computes the CPU tier *less* precisely than the CPU did, and the decode that follows sees a slightly different KV. Neutral on NLL; recorded so a later "why did greedy tokens change" has its answer. `nll-stream.json`.
  - **Defect (server died; re-read in E22d — the batch was *not* streamed, see below): `probability tensor contains either inf, nan or element < 0` on the first decode after a 4-sequence × 256-token prefill batch logged as exactly 1,024 tokens.** Single-sequence streamed chunks of 1,175 / 1,280 / 3,840 / 4,096 tokens and ≥ 12 CPU-path decodes before it were clean; the E22a kernel check only covered T=16. Both TP ranks asserted together (so it is the shared computation, not one rank's DMA). Xid 43 on both GPUs; GPU0 left at phantom 100 % / P0 / 94 W (TROUBLESHOOTING). Decode regression check (C1 ≥ 38) therefore **not measured**. Suspects, in order: FlashInfer fused-MoE tactic selection at M = 1,024 exactly (power of two, new shape → a different autotuned tactic than the resident 208-expert call gets), a token-count-dependent workspace sized for the resident call and reused by the streamed one, or the 0.98 GiB free VRAM on TP1 during the run making an internal allocation fall back. E22d below bisects offline before the next relaunch.
- E22d (next): `kt-export-bench.py --check-tokens 1024` (and 4096, 1175) with `--skip-timing` on the crashed server's layer set; if the offline kernel is finite at 1,024 the fault is in-server (workspace / memory pressure) → relaunch with `KT_GPU_PREFILL_MIN_TOKENS=1025` as a discriminating probe and `CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING`-free logging of `torch.isfinite` per streamed layer under `KT_GPU_PREFILL_LOG=2`. The decode check and the 8K/16K chunk runs resume after the NaN has a root cause, not a workaround (E24 rule).
- **E22d results so far (2026-09-22):**
  - Offline kernel check at T = 1,024 / 4,096 / 1,175 (`kt-export-bench.py --skip-timing --check-tokens T`, layer 5, expert 0, both TP slices): staged layout == resident layout, streamed-vs-resident max|diff| 0, rel-L2 vs fp32 0.0440 / 0.0441 / 0.0441, `finite=True` for all three (`evidence/e22-layerwise-prefill/e22d-t{1024,4096,1175}.json`). The streamed kernel is not the fault.
  - Re-reading the crash log changed the picture: the scheduler's `#new-token` is padded up to a multiple of 256, and the MoE sees the real count (`[KT layerwise prefill]` lines: 1,280 → 1,175, 3,840 → 3,700 and 3,664, 2,560 → 2,478). The 4-sequence "1,024-token" batch therefore had ~1,000 real tokens, **below the 1,024 threshold, and no `streamed in` line was logged for it — it ran the regular kt-kernel D=4 CPU path, not the streamed path.** (`Prefill batch` lines are logged at result-processing time, after the forward, which is why the batch and the assert share a timestamp.) The assert surfaced in the following bs=4 decode graph replay (Engram `.to("cpu")` is the first host sync), but the sampled tensor came from that prefill.
  - Distinguishing facts vs E21 (which ran the same 4 × 256 C4 shape cleanly on this path): the streamer holds 2.27 GiB/rank of GPU staging plus 6.53 GiB pinned host, leaving TP1 0.98 GiB free; three Triton kernels (`alloc_extend_kernel`, `_expand_prefill_causally_kernel`, `alloc_decode_kernel`) device-loaded lazily on TP1 in the two seconds before the crash (first multi-sequence extend/decode of the process). Working hypothesis: memory pressure on the first multi-sequence batch, not the E22 kernel. Superseded suspects: tactic selection at M = 1,024, workspace reuse by the streamed call.
  - Probe added (overlay `kt_ep_wrapper.py`, `KT_NAN_PROBE_MIN_TOKENS=N`, plumbed through `serve-hybrid.sh`): logs the real MoE token count + streamed decision once per forward and any non-finite input/output per MoE layer, so the next reproduction localizes the NaN to attention/dense (already non-finite on MoE input) vs the CPU path vs the streamed path. Reproduction plan: relaunch streamer-on with the probe → C4 × 256-token bench; then the same with `KT_GPU_PREFILL_MIN_TOKENS=0` as the memory-footprint control.
  - **Reproduction attempt (streamer on, probe on, same server config):** the bench prompt is 166 real tokens (`FIXTURE`; the `--distinct-prompts` set is 31–50 each), so the E22c crash batch was 4 × 166 = 664 real tokens padded per sequence to 256 → "1,024". 14 C4 runs over HTTP (the scheduler split arrivals 1 + 3 each time), then 6 forced 4-prompt `/generate` batches — `[KT probe] 676 MoE tokens, streamed=False` each time, exactly the crash shape — all finite at every MoE layer, before and after a 32K streamed prefill (TP free VRAM ≈ 3.7 GiB vs 0.98 GiB at the E22c crash). **Not reproduced.**
  - Verdict for E22d: the E22 kernel and staging path are cleared (offline finite at 1,024/1,175/4,096; in-server streamed forwards of 1,978/3,108/4,096 tokens finite). The E22c assert is a rare fault in the pre-existing D=4 CPU-deferral path on a multi-sequence batch — the first D=4 NaN seen (E21 qualified D=4 over C1–C4 soaks). It moves to E24 with two leads: memory pressure (0.98 GiB free on TP1 after 16K + 32K prefill + NLL logprobs, lazily loaded Triton kernels in the same second) and the deferral buffer across a batch-size change (bs=1 decode → 4-seq prefill → bs=4 decode). E24's soak runs with `KT_NAN_PROBE_MIN_TOKENS=1` so the next occurrence names the layer and whether the MoE input was already non-finite.
  - **Decode regression check (streamer on, D=4, `--max-tokens 256 --runs 3 --warmup 1`, `evidence/e22-layerwise-prefill/decode-streamer-on.json`): C1 38.41 / C2 47.81 / C4 144.97 tok/s (aggregate) vs E21 38.5 / 45.4 / 147 — no regression; gate C1 ≥ 38 passes.** The 2.27 GiB/rank staging and the 6.53 GiB pinned host buffer cost nothing at decode, as designed (decode never enters the streamed branch).
- **E22e — chunk 8192 (`CHUNKED_PREFILL_SIZE=8192`, relaunch):** per-chunk host time 5.4–5.8 s (vs 4.7–5.1 at 4096), so the fixed export/barrier cost is ≈ 4.6 s and the GPU MoE adds ≈ 0.25 ms/token. **16K: 1,375 tok/s warm (TTFT 11.6 s; first post-launch run 547 tok/s / 29 s), 32K: 1,404 tok/s (TTFT 22.8 s)**; `verify.py` 16K all pass, needles 3/3 at TTFT 9.8 s (`prefill-16k-chunk8192.json`, `prefill-32k-chunk8192.json`, `verify-16k-chunk8192.json`). VRAM after the 32K run: 96.7 of 97.9 GiB used (≈ 1.2 GiB free) vs ≈ 3.7 GiB at chunk 4096. **Chunk 16384 does not fit**: the KV pool is only ≈ 3.4 GB (MLA + sliding-window KV; `max_total_num_tokens` 1.79M is 13× what 4 × 32K needs), so lowering `MEM_FRACTION_STATIC` cannot free the ≈ 6 GB more activations the 16K chunk needs; only moving GPU experts to the CPU would (E23 fit-check territory).
- **Verdict (E22 closed 2026-09-22):** streamed prefill works and is the default (`KT_GPU_PREFILL_MIN_TOKENS=1024`): 3.9× at the default 4096 chunk, 6.5× with the opt-in 8192 chunk, NLL- and needle-neutral, decode untouched *(correction 2026-10-08, E34d-ii: the NLL was scored on the chunk-4096 serve only; the first chunk-8192 NLL, at 219 / 165, reads +0.0067 and fails the gate — see E34d-iii)*. The E22c NaN belongs to the regular CPU path (E24). Decision on defaults: chunk stays 4096 so ≈ 3.7 GiB VRAM stays free for the E23 draft stages; `CHUNKED_PREFILL_SIZE=8192` is the fast-prefill profile when speculation is off. Remaining ceiling: the ≈ 4.6 s fixed per-chunk cost is 40 × (export ≈ 90–117 ms core-bound + 2 barriers); halving it needs either more cores (96-core note in T1) or exporting two layers per barrier round (larger pinned buffer, 13 GiB).

## E23 — Speculative decoding on the hybrid: DSpark/MTP as an iterative tuning program, not one A/B (GPU; T3.9; supersedes E10's protocol)

- Status: **closed 2026-09-23 — speculation stays off.** R0, R1 (K=2 and K=5, D=4) and the R2 discriminator (K=5, D=0) are measured; no configuration reaches the go bar (C1 wall ≥ 39.6): K=2 is parity at C1 and loses 57–63 % at C3/C4; K=5 loses 16 % at C1 and 69–77 % at C3/C4. R3–R5 (knee, workload, retune) and the quality gates were not spent on a losing family. The lasting yield is three graph-mode bugs found and fixed (A workaround, B overlay `838490a6`, **C kt-kernel patch** — the one upstream calls "unsupported"). Registered 2026-09-22 from the owner's request (relayed by thread T-019fc4bd). Evidence dir `evidence/e23-spec-decode/`.
- **Erratum 2026-09-23 (R3b, below):** the R0 C3/C4 baseline cells in the headline table (98.2 / 131.9 wall) are artifacts of bug C on the *spec-off* graph — the qualified spec-off figures are C3 35.8 / C4 47.0 wall. Against those, K=2 is ≈ parity at C3/C4 (not a 57–63 % loss) and K=5 loses 14–36 %; the K=2 C4 cell itself is unverified (measured before the patch, same 12-token temp-shape mechanism). The go bar (≥ 10 % at C1) is still not met, so the verdict stands. Rows are kept as measured; read them with [BENCHMARKS "Erratum"](../docs/BENCHMARKS.md).
- **Headline table (D=4, 512 tokens, distinct prompts, 3 runs after 1 warmup, 325 W):**
  | Cell | R0 spec-off wall (summed) | K=2 wall (summed) | K=5 wall (summed) | K=2 / K=5 accept rate | K=5 GPU W (GPU0 / GPU1) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | C1 | **36.0** (38.7) | 36.1 (38.9) — 1.00× | 30.1 (32.1) — 0.84× | 0.67 / 0.38 | 101 / 123 (GPU0 util 12 %) |
  | C3 | **98.2** (111.7) | 41.9 (47.0) — 0.43× | 30.9 (38.2) — 0.31× | 0.58 / 0.38 | — |
  | C4 | **131.9** (148.3) | 48.3 (69.4) — 0.37× | 30.0 (37.2) — 0.23× | 0.36 / 0.35 | — |
  K=5 rows from `r3/k5-d4-fix.json` (after the bug C fix; per-stream at C4 7.5–12.2 tok/s, TTFT 1.09 / 1.56 / 1.57 s, zero faults, zero Xid). K=5 accept length is 2.5–2.9 of 6 drafted tokens at every concurrency (server decode-log means, `r3/k5-d4-fix-accept.txt`), i.e. the draft stages are right about ≈ 1.7 extra tokens per step while the verify step charges the CPU tier for 6.
- **Why it loses (measured, consistent with E21):** the GPUs sit at 101–131 W and GPU0 at 12 % utilisation during a K=5 matrix — the step is the CPU tier, as in E21, and a verify step submits `bs × (K+1)` tokens' worth of distinct experts per layer to a tier whose cost is per-expert latency. The ≈ 40 per-layer boundaries are not amortised because each one now carries 3–6× the expert work and the D=4 deferral hides less of it (the deferred experts of rejected tokens are wasted). Acceptance falls with concurrency (K=2: 0.67 → 0.36) because with more sequences in the batch the scheduler's verify budget is shared; it is a scheduling effect, not a correctness one (the same-prompt C4 discriminator planned in R1 was pre-empted by the C4 crash and is moot now that the family is rejected).
- **R2 (K=5, D=0) as a discriminator, not a matrix:** probes C1 26.8 / C2 26.9 / C3 32.3 tok/s (96–128 tokens, debug-sync server) then the same C4 crash with `KT_MAX_DEFERRED=0` → deferral is not the cause of the fault. The D=0 legs for `off` and K=2 were not run: D=0 only removes throughput (E21) and R1 already fails at every K.
- **Bug C (R1 K=5 C4 crash → R3 fix):** host `SIGSEGV` in `cuGraphLaunch` (R1) or Xid 31 `CE4 … VIRT_WRITE @ 0x0` (R2) on the first bs=4 target-verify replay. Root cause in kt-kernel, not the overlay: `KExpertsCPUBuffer.get_buffer` served the 24-token verify shape from its single *temp* pinned buffer (the flashinfer autotune dummy forward asked for 24 tokens 26 s before `set_capture_batch_sizes`), the bs=4 graph baked those pointers, and the bs=3 capture replaced — freed — the temp. Registered set after capture `[6, 12, 18]`, 24 missing (`r3/k5-d0-nofix-c4-segfault.txt`). Fix: `config/kt-kernel-patches/0001-experts-base-temp-buffer-lifetime.patch` (promote a registered temp into `capture_buffers`; retire replaced temps behind a CUDA event), applied in `config/Dockerfile.dsv41-kt`. Verification: no-fix → segfault on the first C4 verify; fix → C4 probes 3/3 and the full matrix above. Upstream kvcache-ai/ktransformers#1815 and #2009 describe this crash and were answered "speculative decoding unsupported"; it is a 40-line lifetime bug (OPEN-SOURCE-PLAN PR 5). Full entry in [TROUBLESHOOTING](../docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md).
- **Verdict:** `DSPARK` default stays 0 in `serve-hybrid.sh`; the E14 scoreboard's "speculation on" row records K=2 C1 36.1 / K=5 30.1 wall. Re-open only if the CPU tier's per-expert cost changes shape (T3.6d3 rank-split dispatch, a third GPU taking most experts, or a 96-core host) — then R1 is a two-hour re-run with the same scripts. Standing rule kept: never `KT_MAX_DEFERRED ≥ 5` (E24).
- **R3b — the rebuilt image's C4 "regression" was the old build's silent corruption (2026-09-23; T3.9g close-out, evidence `r3/batch-consistency/`, `r3/decode-patched-*.json`, `r3/ab-*.json`).** Sequence: (1) `dsv41-kt:recipe` rebuilt with the bug C patch; spec-off probe C1 38.4–39.0 as before, **C4 49–50 summed / 45–47 wall instead of 148 / 132** (`rebuilt-image-specoff-probe.json`, `reprobe-c4.json`). (2) A/B with the pristine `experts_base.py` bind-mounted over the same image: C4 148.4 / 117 wall (`ab-unpatched-c4.json`) — the patch is the cause. (3) A variant allocating a fresh permanent tuple at capture instead of promoting the temp: C4 48.5–51.5 (`ab-v1-c4.json`) — buffer identity is not the mechanism. (4) `py-spy` on TP0 during C4 decode (variant server, `--cap-add SYS_PTRACE`): 90 % of samples inside the Engram eager break's D2H copy waiting on the captured segment, zero in kt Python — the extra ≈ 53 ms/step is inside the graph (CPU-expert host callbacks). (5) Hypothesis: the unpatched bs=4 graph's baked task pointers landed in freed-then-recycled pinned memory, so the CPU tier computed one row per step and the other rows got stale bytes; 148 = 4 × 37 because the step never grew. Test: **`scripts/verify-batch-consistency.py --same-prompt`** (new) — four identical greedy prompts in one batch must agree. Pristine build C4: row 0 = bs=1 reference (502/509 chars), rows 1–2 diverge at char 16, **row 3 answers that the prompt was just the word "Explain"**; pristine C3 same at rows 1–2 (bs=3 pads into the bs=4 graph); pristine C2 correct. Patched image: C3 byte-identical, C4 min pairwise prefix 225 chars (staggered admission drift). **Confirmed** — and the launch log of the rebuilt image says why spec-off was never safe: `KT_BUF promote temp buffer bs=4 into capture_buffers (capture_bs=[1, 2, 4])`, i.e. bs=4 was first requested before `set_capture_batch_sizes` in the plain decode path too. (6) Qualified spec-off numbers, 256 tokens, distinct prompts, 3 runs: **C1 39.2 summed / 34.0 wall, C2 44.9 / 40.6, C3 38.3 / 35.8, C4 50.8 / 47.0** (`decode-patched-c1234.json`); same-prompt C4 56.2 / 49.6 (`decode-patched-c4-sameprompt.json`). Step ≈ 8 ms + 17.7 ms × rows; full expert reuse saves only 11 % → the CPU tier's decode cost is per (token, expert) evaluation at M ≤ 4, not per distinct expert's bytes. Consequences: E19 item 3 ("distinct routing costs nothing at bs=4 → tier not read-bound") and every C3/C4 figure E19–E22 + R0 are artifacts; the "bs=2 dip" was the honest row; E19's greedy logprob check at bs=4 compared only stream 0. Follow-ups: E26 (capture bs=3, per-row cost model, kt CPU kernel M>1 behaviour), E24 (bug C is the first concrete candidate for the E21 first-bs=4-step NaN at D=5 and the E22c 4-sequence NaN — reproducer needs owner approval because it means running D=5 once on the patched image). *Surprise:* the best-looking number in the recipe — 4× batching for free — was the bug; the "anomaly" (bs=2) was the truth.
- **R1 prerequisites (2026-09-23): two graph-mode bugs root-caused and fixed before any R1 number was taken** — see [TROUBLESHOOTING](../docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md#dspark-on-illegal-memory-access-in-dspark_verifypy--breakable_cuda_graphpy-replay--segreplay-on-the-first-decode-after-a-long-prompt). Bug A (wrong tokens in graph mode) = multi-stream overlap inside the captured verify graph; worked around with `SGLANG_OPT_USE_MULTI_STREAM_OVERLAP=0` on every DSpark launch. Bug B (Xid 31 on the second request) = the SM120 page-split scratch grew after CUDA-graph capture and freed the tensor the graphs still wrote; fixed in overlay `838490a6` (retire-not-free + `reserve_split_buffers()` from pool geometry). Diagnostic ladder (runs B→I) and the reusable env flags are in the same TROUBLESHOOTING entry. All R1 rows below run graph mode with zero faults.
- **R1 result, K=2, D=4 (`r1/k2-d4.json`, 3 runs after 1 warmup, 512 tokens, distinct prompts, 325 W cap):**
  | Cell | R0 per-stream / wall tok/s | K=2 per-stream (aggregate) / wall tok/s | Ratio (wall) | TTFT s | accept rate (server, mean over decode-log windows) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | C1 | 38.7 / 36.0 | 38.9 / **36.1** | 1.00× | 1.05 | 0.67 |
  | C3 | 37.2 each (111.7) / 98.2 | 15.7 each (47.0) / **41.9** | 0.43× | 1.50 | 0.58 |
  | C4 | 37.0 each (148.3) / 131.9 | 17.4 each (69.4) / **48.3** | 0.37× | 1.50 | 0.36 |
  GPU sampler during the matrix: max 129 W / 148 W per card, 70 °C — the GPUs idle while the CPU tier verifies. **Reading:** at C1 the verify step costs almost exactly what its ≈ 2.2 accepted tokens save (parity, not the ≥ 10 % go bar); at C3/C4 a `bs × 3`-token verify touches ≈ 3× the distinct CPU experts per layer and the tier, already saturated by real tokens (E21), cannot hide it — H2 confirmed, E6-DESIGN §4.6's prediction holds. Acceptance falling with batch size (0.67 → 0.36) needs one discriminator before it is read as a bug: a C4 run with the *same* prompt on every stream (acceptance should then match C1 if verification is correct; a prompt-domain effect would disappear).
- R1 K=5 leg: fits at `MEM_FRACTION_STATIC=0.935` (draft 3.85 GB/rank, 5.64 GB free after capture, KV pool 257,536 tokens vs 279,808 at K=2; verify graphs bs 1–4 × 6 tokens). First pass (`r1/k5-d4.txt`): C1 32.3 summed / 30.3 wall, C3 38.2 / 31.8, accept 0.26–0.27 at C1, then C4 crashed (bug C above); the clean rerun after the fix is the K=5 column in the headline table (C1 30.1 wall, within 1 % of the first pass).
- R0 result (`r0-baseline-d4-specoff.json`): C1 38.73 summed / **36.01 wall** tok/s (TTFT 1.02 s); C3 111.7 / 98.2 wall (37.2 per stream); C4 148.3 / 131.9 wall (37.0 per stream). Reproduces E21 within +0.5 %. Go bar for C1 is therefore wall ≥ 39.6.
- Draft-MoE integration check (T3.9a, code reading, no run): the DSpark stages are `DeepseekV4DecoderLayer(..., layer_id=0..2, is_nextn=True)` and their FusedMoE goes through `create_kt_config_from_server_args(server_args, layer_id)` like every MoE. The overlay's `KTEPWrapperMethod.create_weights` already clamps `num_gpu_experts` to the layer's expert count when `num_gpu_experts ≥ num_experts` (128 draft experts ≤ 208): no `KTMoEWrapper`, no streamer registration, `submit`/`sync` return early, `mask_cpu_expert_ids(·, 128)` is a no-op. So the draft MoEs are GPU-resident and their `layer_idx` 0..2 never touches the CPU tier or the layerwise streamer's registry (which is keyed by the *main* layers' idx). No overlay change needed; the log line to confirm at launch is the absence of any `layers.0/1/2.ffn.experts` kt load.
- Question: E6-DESIGN §4.6 predicted that DSpark K=5 *hurts* a CPU-bound MoE (≈ 35 vs 50 tok/s) because a 6-token verify step touches up to 6× the distinct CPU experts per layer while accepting ≈ 3.3 of them, unless drafted tokens reuse experts. Since then the CPU tier's cost changed shape: E21 showed the per-layer wait is kt's fixed m=1 latency (0.19 ms/expert, ≈ 40 waits/step) hidden by deferral, not DRAM bytes. A multi-token verify step may therefore *amortise* the 40 synchronisation boundaries (one CPU submit/sync per layer regardless of tokens) rather than multiply DRAM traffic — or it may expand CPU work faster than acceptance pays it back. Which one is an empirical question about this box.
- Hypotheses (pick one per round, falsify with the counters below): (H1) K=2 wins at C1 (≈ 2.2 tokens per verify, ≈ 3× CPU work, boundaries amortised) and K=5 loses; (H2) at C3/C4 speculation loses everywhere because the CPU tier is already saturated by real tokens (E21: C4 = 147 aggregate with the CPU at 100 %); (H3) acceptance on our code-review fixture sits at 50–60 % as on V4-Flash (E8: 51–63 %), so `tokens per verify` ≈ 1 + K·acc is the right predictor; (H4) the three MTP stages (7.4 GiB, 3.7 GiB/card at TP2) do **not** fit beside 208 GPU experts + the E22 staging (2.27 GiB/rank) at 32K and force a placement change.
- Preconditions and fit check (T3.9a, before any run):
  1. Baseline is the E21 default: `KT_MAX_DEFERRED=4`, speculation off, 208/176 placement, 32K, `MAX_RUNNING_REQUESTS=4`, `CUDA_GRAPH_MAX_BS=4`; re-measure C1/C3/C4 with the *same* fixed distinct prompts and `--max-tokens 512` (longer outputs than the 256 used so far, so acceptance has room to settle) as the reference row.
  2. Memory: read `Capture … avail mem` and `max_total_num_tokens` from the log with `--speculative-algorithm DSPARK --speculative-dspark-block-size K`; draft weights land per rank. If free VRAM after capture < 2 GB or the KV pool drops below 32K × 4, the options are, in order: `KT_GPU_PREFILL_MIN_TOKENS=0` (frees 2.27 GiB/rank, loses E22), `KT_NUM_GPU_EXPERTS=200` (moves 8 experts/layer to the CPU, +4.5 % CPU-tier bytes), lower `MEM_FRACTION_STATIC`. Record which was needed; a worse expert placement is a **stop** condition unless the speculation win exceeds its cost.
  3. Graph shapes: the breakable decode graph is captured per `bs`; with speculation the verify step is `bs × (K+1)` tokens (1/2/4 × 6 = 6/12/24 at K=5, × 3 = 3/6/12 at K=2), all under `SM120_DECODE_MAX_TOKENS = 64`. Confirm the capture log lists them and that no verify step runs eager (`cuda graph: False` on a decode line = fail the round). The draft stages run their own MoE (`speculative_moe_runner_backend flashinfer_mxfp4`) — confirm they are GPU-resident and not routed through the kt wrapper.
  4. Never run `KT_MAX_DEFERRED ≥ 5` in any round until E21's D=5 NaN has a root cause (E24).
- Trial matrix (each cell: 3 measured runs after 1 warmup, fixed seed, distinct prompts, 512 output tokens, thinking off, 325 W):
  | Round | Deferral | Speculation | Concurrency | Purpose |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | R0 | D=4 | off | C1, C3, C4 | reference (must reproduce E21 within ±5 %) |
  | R1 | D=4 | K=2, K=5 | C1, C3, C4 | the headline table; H1/H2 |
  | R2 | D=0 | off, K=2, K=5 | C1, C3, C4 | does deferral interact with verification (deferred experts of a rejected token are wasted work)? |
  | R3 | best of R1 | K ∈ {1, 2, 3} (block size is a flag, not a training constant for this decision) | C1, C4 | find the knee |
  | R4 | best so far | best K | C1, C3, C4 with `--max-tokens 1024`, and a long-context variant (8K prompt) | acceptance by workload |
  | R5+ | retune from measured bottleneck: capture sizes, `MAX_RUNNING_REQUESTS`, scheduling, `KT_CPUINFER` | | | |
  | R6 (added 2026-10-08, owner-approved, ~~deferred until T3.22~~ **pulled forward 2026-10-09 (owner): next serve**; knobs `NGRAM=1 NGRAM_DRAFT_TOKENS=5 NGRAM_BREADTH=1` in `serve_config.py`, copy fixtures in `bench-decode.py --fixture-set copy`, spec-off baselines in `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/d-v-a/262k-4slot-chunk4096/c1-copy-*.json`) | D=4 | SGLang n-gram (`--speculative-algorithm NGRAM`, no draft model) | C1 only | the one speculation family not yet run. Draft cost is nil, verify cost is the same as DSpark's (a K+1-token verify routes K+1 rows' worth of distinct CPU experts; C1→C4 measured 3.1× the bytes, step ≈25 → 71 ms), so it is a pure loss on fresh prompts and pays only where the output copies long spans of the context. Fixtures: two copy-heavy prompts (edit code that is in the prompt; re-emit a table from the prompt) plus the standard review fixture. Go bar: ≥ +10 % C1 wall on the copy-heavy fixtures **and** ≤ 5 % loss on the review fixture; otherwise record and close. Break-even arithmetic from our own data: a 4-token verify (≈71 ms) must accept ≈2.9 tokens per step (≈70 % per-token) to match 40 tok/s, which MTP (0.38–0.67) does not reach — n-gram on copy spans can. Not a design change; one serve with the flag. **Result 2026-10-09: verify crashes on the first request (`DeepSeekV4TokenToKVPool` has no `move_kv_cache`; upstream's EAGLE/DSPARK-only guard hides a missing pool API); recorded and closed per protocol, not retried — the worker always runs a full 5-row verify (match or not), so the review-fixture clause of the go bar is unreachable by arithmetic (floor ≈ 12–13 tok/s on fresh text, ceiling ≈ 62 on a perfect copy span). Chain-only + skip-on-no-match port registered as T3.9h-b (low). `evidence/e23-spec-decode/r6-ngram/README.md`.** |
  | — | | **MTP (DSpark) is the model's own heads and lost in R1; a separate drafter model is strictly worse here (lower acceptance than MTP, identical verify cost, extra VRAM + graph set) and is not scheduled.** Speculation on this box is a question about the CPU tier's per-row price, not the predictor | | |
- Measurements per cell (all in one JSON under `evidence/e23-spec-decode/<round>/`): **wall tok/s per stream and aggregate** (not summed stream rates), TTFT, inter-token latency p50/p95, accepted tokens per verification and acceptance % (from the server's `accept` field / decode log), CPU-tier wait count and ms per *accepted* token (`scripts/step-profile.py` + `gap-context.py`, as in E21), host DRAM bandwidth during decode (`perf stat -e` uncore or `scripts/kt-cpu-per-step.sh` + `vmstat`), Engram critical-path ms per step (the two graph breaks, from the step profile), GPU memory / power / SM utilisation per card (`bench-power-sweep.sh` sampler), and the per-layer distinct-CPU-expert count per verify step (routing reuse: add a counter to `KTEPWrapperMethod.apply` under `KT_ROUTING_LOG=1`, log distinct ids per layer per step for 100 steps, `evidence/e23-spec-decode/routing-reuse.json`).
- Quality and correctness gates per configuration that is a candidate default: `nll-score.py` vs `nll-d0.json` with |ΔNLL| < 0.002 (speculation must be lossless by construction — verify rejects mismatches — so any drift is a bug); `verify.py --context 16384 --needles 3` all pass; ≥ 20K-token mixed C1–C4 distinct-prompt soak with **zero** NaN/asserts/Xid.
- Go criteria (initial, owner's): ≥ 10 % C1 wall-throughput gain over R0, no material per-stream regression at C3/C4, acceptance ≥ ~55 % as a health signal (throughput decides, not acceptance), NLL within gate, zero NaNs. **Stop or retune** if CPU wait per accepted token does not fall, if draft memory forces a worse expert/KV placement whose cost exceeds the gain, or if any instability appears.
- Decision fed: `serve-hybrid.sh` gains `DSPARK` (0/1) and `DSPARK_K` env plumbing (default off until a round passes); BENCHMARKS.md gets the round tables; the E14 scoreboard's V4.1 column gets a "speculation on" row.
- Learning pointers: LEARNING.md §1 (DSpark/MTP), §6 (how SGLang schedules verify steps), §10 E23 row.
- Background and the reasoning behind the matrix (why a multi-token verify might amortise the ≈ 40 CPU boundaries, the fit arithmetic, the four-agent router context this feeds): [docs/research/HETEROGENEOUS-ROUTING-AND-OPTIMIZATION-NOTES.md](../docs/research/HETEROGENEOUS-ROUTING-AND-OPTIMIZATION-NOTES.md) §3–4; consolidated E19→E23 tables in [docs/research/BENCHMARK-EVOLUTION-AND-E20-FP8.md](../docs/research/BENCHMARK-EVOLUTION-AND-E20-FP8.md).

## E24 — NaN as a release defect: root-cause the remaining asserts and make a bad row fail the request, not the server (GPU + code reading; T3.13)

- Status: **open**, standing — **two of three sightings closed 2026-09-23 (T3.15: the E21 D≥5 assert was kt-kernel bug C — proven by non-reproduction on the patched build; E22c attributed to the same bug, not proven); remaining: the injected-NaN / fail-the-request item (rule 4) and T3.15b (source of the run-to-run greedy drift).** Registered 2026-09-22 after the owner's point that no released recipe crashes like this; during experimentation a crash is information, in a release it is a defect.
- Question: three NaN sightings remain without a root cause — E21's deterministic D≥5 assert on the first bs=4 step, E22c's assert after a 4×256-token streamed prefill, and (historically) E19's non-deterministic first-mixed-batch assert that the zero-fill in overlay `1d6a98d3` removed but did not explain beyond "stale FP8 bytes". Each one also leaves an Xid 43 and, on GPU0, a phantom-busy card (TROUBLESHOOTING).
- Rules: (1) every NaN gets a bisect to a deterministic reproducer before the code around it changes; (2) no retry-on-NaN or clamp workarounds in the serving path; (3) a configuration is only a candidate default after a ≥ 20K-token mixed soak with zero asserts and zero Xid in `journalctl -k`; (4) separately, make the failure mode survivable: find where this SGLang build checks sampling probabilities (`assert_async` inside the breakable graph replay today) and whether its per-request NaN rejection path (`--enable-nan-detection`-style handling upstream) is reachable under the breakable decode graph on SM120; if it is, turn it on and prove it with an injected NaN in a test build — the request must return an error and the server must keep serving.
- Procedure per sighting: reproducer (offline harness first: `kt-export-bench.py --check-tokens`, `test-kt-one-layer.py`), `torch.isfinite` probes at the tier boundaries under a log flag (not `CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING` in production runs), fix, soak, TROUBLESHOOTING entry with the signature and the fix.
- Pass: all three sightings have a named cause; the soak gate is in TASKS.md standing rules; an injected NaN in a test build fails one request and not the server.
- **Lead result (T3.15, 2026-09-23 evening, owner-approved D=5 launch; evidence [`evidence/e24-nan/t315-d5/`](../evidence/e24-nan/t315-d5/)).** Patched image, launcher defaults (226/158, capture `1 2 3 4`, spec off, FCLK 2100 slot 3, 300 W) with `KT_MAX_DEFERRED=5 KT_NAN_PROBE_MIN_TOKENS=1`; resolved `kt_max_deferred_experts_per_token: 5` confirmed in `launch.txt`. The owner's 340 GB download to `/mnt/data` (separate NVMe) ran throughout, so every same-day comparator below was measured under the same condition.
  - **The E21 crash did not reproduce.** Capture, the first bs=4 decode steps (`verify-batch-consistency.py --same-prompt --concurrency 4` — the exact E21 trigger), C1–C4 verify runs, the NLL pass and a 61,440-token C1–C4 bench (12 × 512 tok per level, distinct prompts) ran with **zero** `probability tensor contains inf/nan`, zero probe hits, zero Xid, zero Traceback, EDAC 0/0, both GPUs clean after teardown (2 MiB / 80 MiB, 0 %). Under E21 the assert fired on the *first* bs=4 step, every time. **Verdict: kt-kernel bug C was the cause of the E21 D≥5 sighting** (deterministic reproducer gone on the patched build, everything else equal). The E22c sighting (first bs=4 decode after a 4-sequence prefill, D=4) has no on-demand reproducer; it is *attributed* to bug C on the same mechanism (bs=4 graph rows 1–3 reading recycled pinned memory) and the 141K-token E27 soak plus this run at bs=4 with D=4 and D=5, but not proven. E19's zero-fill case stays as recorded. Rule (3)'s injected-NaN / fail-the-request item is untouched by this result.
  - **D=5 performance, C1 first (512 tok, 12 runs, fixture offset 6):** D=5 **43.76 summed / 41.69 wall**, TTFT 0.60 s; same-conditions D=4 control **41.76 / 39.83**, 0.62 s (`control-d4/c1-off6.json`) → **+4.8 % summed / +4.7 % wall**; same-conditions D=0 control **33.58 / 32.04**, 0.74 s (`control-d0/c1-off6.json`) → D=4 is +24 % over D=0 at the new placement. Concurrency (D=5 vs the E27 Boot 12 soak D=4 rows, download-free): C2 52.94 / 50.54 vs 52.12 / 49.80 (+1.5 %); C3 56.23 / 54.61 vs 56.00 / 54.38 (+0.4 %); C4 57.77 / 55.52 vs 58.09 / 55.79 (−0.5 %). The download cost D=4 C1 ≈ 0.6 % (41.76 vs the soak's 42.02), so the C2–C4 deltas carry that much bias. Deferral's gain shrinks with rows, as the per-row byte model predicts (hiding a wait helps most when the wait is the whole step). GPU peaks 167 / 178 W, 71 / 69 °C; util medians 22 % / 94 %; FCLK 2100 in 361/361 HSMP samples; DDR counter ≈ 100 GB/s busy at C1 (same as D=4).
  - **D=5 quality: fails the NLL gate, narrowly.** `nll.json`: mean NLL 1.48900 vs the E22 baseline 1.48613 → **ΔNLL +0.00287** (z −0.74; 150 tokens worse / 156 better by > 0.5 nat; mean |Δlogp| 0.129, the usual kt-vs-FlashInfer offset). Gate is |Δ| < 0.002; D=4 reads +0.0018 on the same baseline (E28/E27). Statistically indistinguishable from zero on 4,795 tokens, but the gate is the gate and D=5 sits on the wrong side of it while D=4 sits on the right side.
  - **D=5 consistency: fails every gate, and the failure is not what it looked like.** `--same-prompt --concurrency 4`: rows share 9 / 33 chars pairwise, each 9–39 with the bs=1 reference (`verify-c4-same.json`); C2 same-prompt 33 (`verify-c2-same.json`); **C1 same-prompt 39 (`verify-c1-same.json`) — two bs=1 greedy runs of one prompt differ after 39 characters**, so this is run-to-run nondeterminism, not a batch-row bug. Distinct-prompt C4 is coherent (2/4 pass, no gibberish, correct Fibonacci/planets answers; `verify-c4-distinct.json`). Two suspects were then eliminated in order: (1) the radix prefix cache — the reference prefills the whole prompt while the repeat hits the cache and computes only its last token through a different kernel path; `verify-batch-consistency.py --flush-cache` (added today, POSTs `/flush_cache` before every request group) still diverges at 33 (`verify-c1-same-flush.json`) and three flushed sequential runs give three different answers with pairwise prefixes 9 / 33 / 9 (`repeat3-c1-flush.json`); (2) kt-kernel's deferred-task machinery — read via the librarian in `kvcache-ai/ktransformers/kt-kernel` (`cpu_backend/task_queue.cpp`, `cpuinfer.h`, `operators/amx/moe_base.hpp`): one dispatcher thread executes enqueued MoE tasks strictly FIFO, `pending` counts unfinished tasks including the running one, `sync(allow_pending=1)` therefore returns exactly when the immediate task has finished and the deferred one is the only task left, the deferred output lands in `output_cpu[next_slot]` (`incremental=False`) and the next layer's immediate task accumulates onto it (`incremental=True`) in a fixed-order loop; the per-token expert reduction is an ordered `j = 0..k-1` FMA loop and the NUMA/TP merge is ascending-index — no schedule-dependent reduction, no overlap, same CUDA stream on both sides (`kt_ep_wrapper.py` passes `torch.cuda.current_stream`). **Then the controls settled it: D=4 at bs=1 also differs run to run, at 310 / 364 chars (`control-d4/verify-c1-same*.json`; three flushed repeats 362 / 225 / 225), and so does D=0, at 388 (`control-d0/verify-c1-same-flush.json`; repeats 363 / 323 / 323).** The serving path flips near-tie tokens run to run at every D (E21 saw the same with `greedy-logprobs.py` and switched the quality gate to teacher-forced NLL for that reason); deferral does not create the drift, it moves the first near-tie forward — from ≈ 320–390 chars at D=0 through ≈ 225–365 at D=4 to **9–39 at D=5**, where the opening sentence itself changes ("During the day, sunlight enters…" at D=0/D=4 vs "Sunlight is made up of all colors…" at D=5). Protecting one routed slot of six is a different model, not a faster one. Source of the baseline drift (GPU-side candidates: `flashinfer_mxfp4` MoE runner, `dsv4` attention split-KV, NCCL reduction order; SGLang's `--enable-deterministic-inference` exists for the batch-invariance half of it) is a new open item, T3.15b — a diagnosis, not a defect, since greedy bs=1 divergence after 300+ characters is within what E21 already accepted.
  - **Decision: D=5 stays off; `KT_MAX_DEFERRED=4` remains the default.** It clears the crash question and the +3–5 % C1 bar (+4.7 % wall) but fails the NLL gate (+0.00287 vs 0.002) and the consistency gate (9–39 chars), and the consistency failure is the tell that the approximation has crossed from "hide the wait" to "change the answer". The standing rule changes from *"no D ≥ 5 until the E24 root cause"* to *"D ≥ 5 no longer crashes (bug C); D=5 is rejected on quality/consistency; D=6 on NLL (+0.23 nats, E21). T3.16b's launcher guard (`KT_ALLOW_UNSAFE_DEFERRAL=1`) still goes in, now to protect the quality gates rather than the server."* Placement (T3.6g2) is the remaining single-user lever with the same ≈ +4 % coefficient and no quality cost.
- **Lead (2026-09-23, from E23 R3b):** kt-kernel bug C is the first concrete candidate for two of the three sightings. Both fired on a **bs=4 step** (E21 D=5: "first bs=4 decode step"; E22c: the first decode after a 4-sequence prefill), and bug C made the bs=4 graph's CPU-tier rows 1–3 read recycled pinned memory — at `protected_k = 1` (D=5) an all-`-1` or garbage id vector for a row is exactly the input that could produce a non-finite expert sum. Proposed reproducer, **not run — it needs the owner's approval because it means one D=5 launch on the patched image against the standing rule:** launch the rebuilt image with `KT_MAX_DEFERRED=5`, run `verify-batch-consistency.py --same-prompt --concurrency 4` and the C1→C4 bench once; if the sampler assert is gone, two sightings close with bug C as the cause and the D≥5 rule can be re-evaluated (E21 measured D=5 at +3–5 %, NLL-neutral); if it still fires, D=5 has a second cause and the rule stays. Either way the E24 injected-NaN test and the "fail the request, not the server" item remain.

## E25 — Host-memory bandwidth and NUMA/topology revalidation: is ≈ 125 GB/s really the CPU's ceiling on 8 × DDR5-6400? (quiet host + one GPU-DMA pass; T1.10)

- Status: **done** 2026-09-22. Requested by the owner (relayed by thread T-019fc4bd): the board has all eight channels populated with fast DIMMs, so the E1b figure and the "two-CCD fabric is the wall" conclusion deserved a direct check instead of an inference. Evidence `evidence/e25-dram-revalidation/` (`suite-cpu.json/.txt`, `suite-gpu-dma.json/.txt`, `bios-inventory.txt`). E1b stays in the record as the historical torch measurement; E25 is the controlled C suite.
- Inventory (read-only, `scripts/bios-inventory.sh`): ASUS Pro WS WRX90E-SAGE SE Rev 1.xx, BIOS 1317 (2026-02-03); Threadripper PRO 9955WX 16C/32T, **2 L3 instances = 2 CCDs** (cores 0–7 / 8–15, SMT 16–31); **NPS1** (one NUMA node); SMT on; `amd-pstate-epp` performance/EPP performance, boost on, prefcore on; `acpi_idle` C1/C2; THP madvise; 8 × KF564R32-32 2R ECC RDIMM, one per channel, SPD 4800 / configured **6400 MT/s** (HARDWARE.md); EDAC `mc0` ce=0 ue=0; IOMMU on; GPUs Gen5 x16 with 128 GiB BAR1 (ReBAR + Above-4G on); socket power cap 350 W. FCLK/UCLK/timings/voltages are not readable from Linux without root SMU tools — left unknown.
- Method: `scripts/bench-dram-stream.c` (C/OpenMP, `-O3 -march=native`, 8 GiB buffers, 6 iterations with the first as warm-up, min/median/max reported) driven by `scripts/bench-dram-suite.py`, threads pinned with `OMP_PROC_BIND`/`GOMP_CPU_AFFINITY` to one CCD, split across both, or SMT siblings; patterns read / write / write-nt / copy / copy-nt / triad (copy counted 2 bytes moved per element, triad 3); 4 KiB vs 2 MiB (`MADV_HUGEPAGE`) pages; two kt-shaped patterns over a 32 GiB pool — `expert-chunk` (100 random 21.8 MiB chunks per iteration = one token's CPU-expert bytes) and `expert-rows` with a barrier every 1 / 3 / 100 chunks to mimic per-layer synchronisation; `--gpu-dma` runs pinned H2D from GPU0, GPU1 and both (torch in `dsv4-nvfp4:recipe`) while the CPU reads. Per-core clocks, `k10temp` Tctl and `amd_hsmp` socket power sampled every run. Quiet host, nothing serving.
- Result, read scaling (median GB/s): ccd0 ×1 **39.9**; ccd0 ×2 **62.1**; ccd0 ×4 62.8; ccd0 ×8 63.1; ccd1 ×8 63.0; split ×2 105.9; split ×4 **125.2**; split ×8 124.5; split ×12 124.5; split ×16 **125.0**; smt-ccd0 ×16 61.7; smt-all ×32 118.6; split ×16 on 4 KiB pages 124.3; repeat 124.3. **E1b's 124.7 reproduced within 0.3 %; the per-CCD ceiling is directly measured at 62–63 GB/s** — two threads on one CCD already saturate it, four threads split across the CCDs already reach the socket ceiling, and 8 or 16 threads add nothing. Page size ≤ 1 %.
- Result, patterns (split ×16 / ccd0 ×8): write 77.8 / 38.5; write-nt 96.8 / 48.4; copy 115.1 / 59.8; copy-nt 136.4 / 69.8; triad 121.7 / 62.7. Every pattern is exactly 2× one CCD's figure — the wall is per CCD in every direction.
- Result, expert-shaped (split ×16): expert-chunk 123.8 (2 MiB) / 120.8 (4 KiB); expert-rows sync every 3 chunks 120.2 / 121.5, every 100 chunks 124.8 / 125.5, every chunk 121.0 / 119.4; smt-all ×32 **91.8** (matches E17's "32 threads slower"); ccd0 ×8 62.8. So a per-layer barrier costs ≈ 3 %, and kt-kernel's E17 T=1 figure (1.08 ms/layer = 127 GB/s) is *at* the generic ceiling — **effective kt bandwidth ≈ generic CPU bandwidth; there is no kt-side inefficiency to recover.**
- Result, GPU DMA concurrency (CPU read split ×16 while DMA runs 40 s): CPU **122.0** with GPU0 H2D 57.0; **124.8** with GPU1 56.9; **121.2 with both at 57.0 + 57.0 → ≈ 235 GB/s total from DRAM, CPU −3 %.** E4b's additivity reconfirmed with a C reader (the CPU pass overlaps ≈ 5 s of the 40 s DMA window).
- Conditions: cores 2.8–5.2 GHz throughout, socket 87–144 W, Tctl ≤ 57 °C — no power or thermal limiting during any row.
- The four tiers this separates (keep them apart in every later doc): **theoretical DIMM/channel** 409.6 GB/s (8 × 8 B × 6400); **socket-sustainable** ≥ 235 GB/s demonstrated, true ceiling unknown because only the GPUs' DMA engines reach past the CPU; **reachable from this CPU topology** 125 read / 78 write / 97 write-nt / 136 copy-nt; **effective kt-kernel** ≈ 120–127 GB/s.
- Conclusion: the E1b/E17 conclusion stands and is now direct rather than inferred — the DIMMs, channel count and memory clock are not the limit; the CCD→IO-die link is, at ≈ 63 GB/s per CCD. **No projection changes**: `--dram-gbps 125`, the 50 tok/s CPU-compute floor, the B2 split ceiling 78 and the E17 55 tok/s kt floor are unchanged. Faster DIMMs still buy nothing CPU-side; the H2 (4800 JEDEC) test is expected to cost nothing. The one firmware knob with a mechanism to move the per-CCD figure is the fabric clock (FCLK; bytes per fabric clock × FCLK — *inferred*, FCLK not readable here), which is overclocking and bounded (≈ +5 % per +100 MHz). H1 (a CPU with more CCDs) remains the structural upgrade.
- Decision fed: [docs/BIOS-OPTIMIZATION-GUIDE.md](../docs/BIOS-OPTIMIZATION-GUIDE.md) (setting matrix, protocol, Tier 1 first trial set T1-a NPS2 / T1-b APBDIS+DF latency / T1-c PCIe static Gen5 / T1-d patrol scrub; Tier 2 = H2; Tier 3 = FCLK, approval-gated) — **no firmware setting changed**; ROADMAP option H3; BENCHMARKS four-tier table; LEARNING §8/§10/§11 rows.
- Learning pointers: LEARNING.md §8 (CCD/GMI link, NPS, APBDIS rows), §10 E25 row.
- **BIOS trial Boot 1 (T1-d + T1-b, 2026-09-22, owner applied, profile slot 2; T1.11):** Redirect Scrubber Enabled, CE Counter NoLeakMode, TSME Disabled, APBDIS 1, DF Cstates Disabled, Power Profile Maximum IO Performance Mode, DRAM Power Down Disabled. Same suite, same parameters: **steady-state bandwidth unchanged** (33/35 rows within ±2 %; split ×16 read 126.3, write 78.4, write-nt 97.0, copy-nt 137.2, triad 123.7, kt-shaped 120–125), **two latency-bound rows jumped outside their E25 spread** — ccd0 ×1 read 39.9 → **55.0** (+38 %), smt-all ×32 expert-rows 91.8 → **115.5** (+26 %) — so loaded DRAM latency fell with the fabric held at its top P-state; CPU read under both GPUs' DMA 121.2 → **125.5** (239.5 GB/s total, the 3 % concurrency penalty gone). kt one-layer cold (E21 args) ±5 %: 3 slots T=1 0.590 → 0.603 ms, 6 slots T=1 1.138 → 1.083. **Serve (E23 R0 protocol, 325 W): C1 39.09 summed / 36.30 wall (R0 38.73 / 36.01), C3 112.9 / 97.0 (111.7 / 98.2), C4 150.5 / 132.6 (148.3 / 131.9) — noise.** The four-tier picture is unchanged and gains a fifth fact: the per-layer wait the hybrid hides with D=4 is kt compute + handshake, not fabric P-state latency — a firmware latency policy cannot shorten it. EDAC 0/0 before and after; idle socket 66.7 W (Boot 0: 73.7). Evidence `evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-22-boot1-t1bd/`; full row in the BIOS guide §7. Not obtained: a `/start_profile` trace (came back CPU-only; TROUBLESHOOTING).

## E26 — Single-user levers first, then concurrency requalification after bug C: how far can C1 go on a per-row CPU tier, and can the kt CPU kernel amortise weight reads at M > 1? (GPU + kt-kernel code reading; T3.14, T3.15)

- Status: **Part A and Part B done 2026-09-23.** B1 PASS (bs=3 capture closes the C3 hole: 38.3 → 49.0 summed → launcher default `CUDA_GRAPH_BS="1 2 3 4"`, ~~provisional until the E24 soak runs on it~~ qualified by the E27 served soak 2026-09-23); B2/B3/B5 measured; B4's instrument is invalid (busy-spin) and B4′ (DRAM controller counter) replaced it — **the CPU tier is bandwidth-bound at bs=1 (≈ 98 of 125 GB/s), not core-bound.** Open: E24 soak on the new capture list; the kt C++ reading (H3). Registered 2026-09-23 from E23 R3b. The qualified C1–C4 figures exist (BENCHMARKS "Erratum"); this experiment turns them into a cost model, closes the cheap C3 win, and asks the kt C++ question the numbers raise.
- **Part A result (2026-09-23, 325 W, patched image, spec off, 256 tok, distinct prompts, 3 runs after 1 warmup; evidence [`evidence/e26-single-user/`](../evidence/e26-single-user/)):** no free single-user lever is left in the environment knobs — C1 wall stays 33.3–33.8; the bar (≥ 37) is not met. Cells:
  - **A1 `KT_CPUINFER` at D=4:** 14 → 38.99 summed / **33.34 wall**, TTFT 1.14 s, 4K prefill 797 tok/s (`decode-a1-cpuinfer14-d4-c1.json`); 16 (default) → 38.97 / **33.79**, 1.03 s, 862 tok/s (`decode-a1-cpuinfer16-d4-c1.json`); 24 → 33.62 / **29.85** (−12 %), 0.99 s, 785 tok/s; 30 → **5.04 / 4.86** (7× collapse), TTFT 2.07 s, 4K prefill 471 tok/s. 14 and 16 are one plateau (Δ 1 % < run spread), so the CPU tier is at its bandwidth ceiling with 14 workers — consistent with E25 (two threads per CCD saturate the GMI link) and with H2's premise that the remaining lever is placement, not compute. Mechanism for the cliff (kt-kernel source via the librarian, `cpu_backend/worker_pool.cpp`): each worker is pinned to a distinct `HWLOC_OBJ_CORE` and **busy-spins for 50 ms after every job before sleeping on a condition variable**; workers beyond the 16 cores fail affinity (`Core … not found`) and float on SMT siblings, where 8–14 spinning threads starve the two TP-rank Python launch threads, NCCL and the kt dispatch thread. Upstream's own README says `--kt-cpuinfer` = physical cores. `serve-hybrid.sh` now warns when `KT_CPUINFER` exceeds `lscpu`'s physical-core count. A 96-core host would therefore buy compute headroom for M > 1 rows (Part B question), not single-user speed.
  - **A2 `KT_MAX_DEFERRED` 0 vs 4 at 16 threads:** D=0 → 31.51 summed / **27.96 wall**, TTFT 1.07 s, 4K prefill 822 tok/s (`decode-a2-cpuinfer16-d0-c1.json`, `prefill-a2-…-4096-r4.json`). D=4 is worth **+24 % summed / +21 % wall at C1** on the patched image (E21 measured +22 % on the unpatched one — C1 was never touched by bug C, so the two agree). Prefill unchanged (deferral is a decode path). D=5 not run here (T3.15 approval-gated) — ~~pending~~ run 2026-09-23 evening: +4.7 % C1 wall over D=4 but fails the NLL and consistency gates; stays off (E24 lead result).
  - **A3 step profile (C1, 16 threads, D=4, 8 steps incl. the prefill step, 48-token reply):** two traces. With `activities=["CPU","GPU"]` the profiler itself distorts the step 6× (48 tokens took 11.1 s vs ≈ 1.4 s unprofiled; span 182 ms/step, gaps of 110–142 ms) — **discard for timing**, `prof-a1-cpuinfer16-d4/`. GPU-only (`prof-a1-cpuinfer16-d4-gpuonly/`, 48 tok in 2.5 s, still ≈ 1.8× overhead): span 69.1 ms/step, **gpu_busy 15.4 ms/step** (moe_expert_gpu 3.95, dense_fp8 3.49, collective 2.01, bf16 1.50, elementwise 1.48, indexer 1.29, engram 0.014), idle 53.7 of which 47.0 in 55 gaps ≥ 100 µs; the ten largest gaps are all 8.9–10.1 ms and every one sits between the **GPU-expert cutlass MoE kernel and a pinned HtoD memcpy** (`gap-context-tp0.txt`: 253 of 440 gaps, 333 of 376 ms, follow that pattern) — i.e. the GPU has finished its 208 experts and waits for the kt tier's 176-expert result. Engram's two eager breaks are 0.014 ms of GPU time and do not appear among the large gaps. Reading with the unprofiled 29.6 ms step (33.8 wall): GPU work ≈ 15 ms, CPU-tier wait ≈ 14–15 ms per token — E21's ≈ 17 ms estimate, refined. Lever ladder coefficients: placement (each expert off the CPU tier removes ≈ 1/176 of the 14–15 ms) ≫ Engram breaks (< 0.1 ms) > thread count (0).
  - **A4 VRAM census:** after capture 91,354 MiB used per GPU of 97,887 (`vram-a1-cpuinfer16-d4-after-capture.txt`); after a 32K streamed prefill 94,468 / 94,596 MiB (`…-after-32k.txt`) → ≈ 3.3 GiB free per rank at the worst case. The launch log (read via `docker logs` during the run; **not persisted** — the `serve-*.log` files only hold the `docker run -d` banner, so future cells must `docker logs dsv41-kt > <dir>/launch-<cell>.log` before teardown) sized the KV pool at `max_total_num_tokens=1,933,056` (≈ 3.3 GB/rank) while `max_running_requests=4` × 32K context needs 131K tokens — **the KV pool is ≈ 15× oversized for the served config**; shrinking it frees ≈ 3.1 GB/rank. Rough budget (unverified until tried, T3.6g): ≈ 6 GB/rank usable → at ≈ 21.8 MiB per MXFP4 expert ≈ 14 more GPU experts per layer (222 of 384; 162 on the CPU), ≈ −8 % CPU-tier work → ≈ +4 % C1 wall. Not enough for the bar alone; combined with D=5 (+≈ 4 %) it is marginal.
  - **Prefill/TTFT baseline for the cost model (16 threads, D=4):** 4K **4.65 s TTFT / 862 tok/s** steady (`prefill-a1-cpuinfer16-d4-4096-r4.json`); the first two 4K prefills after launch were 9.0 s and 18.2 s (`…-4096.json`) — a cold-start caveat for any TTFT figure quoted right after a restart; 16K 18.9 s / 845 tok/s; 32K 36.7 s / 870 tok/s.
  - Health: zero Xid across five server launches, EDAC 0/0, GPUs 63/58 °C idle after teardown.
  - Consequences: `KT_CPUINFER=16` and `KT_MAX_DEFERRED=4` stay the defaults, now with measured C1 coefficients; Part B's `KT_CPUINFER` sweep is capped at 16 (24/30 are known losers — B2 becomes 12/14/16 at C4); the profiler protocol is GPU-only activities (TROUBLESHOOTING); the remaining single-user levers are T3.6g placement (+≈ 4 %), T3.15 D=5 (+≈ 4 %, approval-gated) and E27 FCLK (+≈ 2–5 %), so C1 wall ≈ 37 is reachable only by stacking all three — FEASIBILITY's "low-to-mid 40s ceiling" stands.
- **Priority (owner decision 2026-09-23):** the primary metric is **C1 wall tok/s and TTFT**; C2 is the realistic multi-user point; C3/C4 feed the cost model only. Part A (single user) runs before Part B (concurrency) in every GPU window. Rationale: with honest rows each extra stream costs ≈ 17.7 ms/step of CPU-tier time, so batching cannot rescue throughput; the remaining ≈ 17 ms/step of CPU wait at bs=1 (E21) is the whole game. Single-user levers, by expected payoff per unit risk: (1) expert placement — every expert moved off the CPU tier removes work rather than hiding it; VRAM freed by DSpark-off (7.4 GiB budget no longer needed), Engram staging and KV-pool sizing is the currency; (2) deferral D=5 (+≈ 4 % measured before its NaN; back for free if bug C was the NaN — T3.15, approval-gated); (3) kt compute/handshake — `KT_CPUINFER` 16/24/30 and CCD affinity (E25: the DRAM read is at the ceiling, so gains are in compute and sync, single digits expected); (4) the two Engram graph breaks per step (latency on every token; fixed-buffer prefetch is migration-roadmap phase 2); (5) hardware — more CCDs or a third GPU are the only large-factor levers and stay projections. Realistic ceiling with 176 experts on this CPU: C1 wall in the low-to-mid 40s; 60–80 needs experts off the CPU.
- Question: with correct rows, step ≈ 8 ms + 17.7 ms × rows and full expert reuse saves only 11 % — the CPU tier's decode cost is per (token, expert) evaluation. Is that the kt MXFP4 CPU kernel looping GEMV per token (weights re-read per row), the per-task submission/handshake per row, or the D=4 deferral path (deferred tasks are per row too)? Which of these does a 96-core host, a rank-split dispatch (T3.6d3) or a kernel change address?
- Hypotheses: (H1) capturing bs=3 removes the padding penalty: C3 rises from 38.3 to ≈ 42–44 summed (between C2 44.9 and C4 50.8; 8 + 17.7 × 3 = 61 ms → 3/0.061 ≈ 49 is the ceiling if the model is exact). (H2) `KT_CPUINFER` threads vs rows: if per-row cost is compute, doubling threads at bs=4 helps more than at bs=1 (E21 found 12 vs 16 threads flat at bs=1). (H3) the kt CPU MoE forward processes tokens in an outer loop with weights re-read per token, so a token-batched inner loop (or upstream's newer kernels, if any) would make C4 cost < 4× — to be checked by reading kt-kernel's C++ (`cpu_backend/moe*`, `KExpertsCPU forward_task`) via the librarian, not guessed.
- Protocol, common: each cell 3 runs after 1 warmup, 256 tokens, distinct prompts, 325 W, patched image, spec off; every C > 1 cell preceded by `verify-batch-consistency.py --same-prompt` at that concurrency; C1 cells also record TTFT at a 4K and a 16K prompt.
- **Part A — single user (C1), in this order:** (A1) `KT_CPUINFER` ∈ {16, 24, 30} at D=4 (the host has 32 logical CPUs; leave ≥ 2 for the schedulers; E21 found 12 vs 16 flat, so the question is whether 24–30 crosses into SMT siblings and loses, as E25's kt-shaped read did); (A2) `KT_MAX_DEFERRED` 0 vs 4 at C1 to re-measure deferral's honest single-user gain on the patched image, then **D=5 only under T3.15** (owner approval, same-prompt gate at C4 first, then C1); (A3) `step-profile.py` one C1 step: time inside the two Engram eager breaks and the ≈ 40 CPU-tier waits, so the lever ladder above has measured coefficients; (A4) VRAM census on the running server (`nvidia-smi` per rank after capture and after a 32K streamed prefill) to state how many CPU experts could move to the GPU today and what each costs in KV pool / chunk size — a placement change itself is T3.6g, not this experiment. Pass for Part A: C1 wall ≥ 37 (+10 %) from any combination that also passes NLL (|Δ| < 0.002) and the E24 soak; otherwise the coefficients still land in the docs.
- **Part B — concurrency:** (B1) `EXTRA_SERVER_ARGS="--cuda-graph-bs 1 2 3 4"` (today `--cuda-graph-max-bs-decode 4` yields `[1, 2, 4]`) → C1–C4; (B2) `KT_CPUINFER` ∈ ~~{16, 24, 30}~~ **{12, 14, 16}** at C4 (Part A: 24 loses 12 % and 30 collapses at C1 because kt workers beyond the 16 cores spin on SMT siblings — the C4 question is whether *fewer* workers cost anything when rows multiply); (B3) `KT_MAX_DEFERRED` 0 vs 4 at C4, to see how much of the per-row cost is the deferred path; (B4) `kt-cpu-per-step.sh` CPU-seconds per step at C1 vs C4 — if CPU-s per step scales 4× with rows, the tier is compute-bound and cores are the lever; if it stays flat while wall grows, it is serialisation/handshake; (B5) one long-output cell (C4, 1,024 tokens) to see whether the 11 % expert-reuse gain grows as streams diverge less or more.
- **Part B results (2026-09-23, 325 W, patched image, spec off, distinct prompts, `--cuda-graph-bs-decode 1 2 3 4` on every cell; evidence [`evidence/e26-concurrency/`](../evidence/e26-concurrency/)):** the live flag is `--cuda-graph-bs-decode` (`--cuda-graph-bs` is a deprecated alias in this SGLang; `max_bs` is derived as max(bs), so the script's `--cuda-graph-max-bs-decode 4` is harmless alongside it). Launch log confirms `bs=[1, 2, 3, 4]` captured, `KT_BUF promote temp buffer bs=4 into capture_buffers (capture_bs=[1, 2, 3, 4])`, 6.2 GB free before capture (`launch-b1.txt`).
  - **Gate:** `verify-batch-consistency.py --same-prompt` at C2/C3/C4, 128 tok: PASS ×3, gibberish 0.02, min pairwise row prefix 603 / 310 / 155 chars (`verify-b1-same-c*.json`). The C4 155 is below the 225 seen at R3 (96 tok) — still late, fluent divergence (no bug-C signature: rows diverging at char 16 or losing the prompt) but **watch it**; if it drops under 100 on a later gate, rerun at 96 tokens against R3 before trusting any C4 number.
  - **B1 (`KT_CPUINFER=16`, D=4, 256 tok, 3 runs after 1 warmup):** C1 **39.07 summed / 33.94 wall**, TTFT 1.03 s; C2 45.60 / 40.82, 1.26 s; **C3 49.00 / 44.97, 1.47 s**; C4 51.34 / 47.53, 1.68 s (`decode-b1-c*.json`). H1 confirmed and exceeded: C3 38.3 → 49.0 (+28 %), above the 42–44 guess and at the ≈ 49 ceiling the 8 + 17.7 × rows model predicts — the old C3 was entirely a padding-to-bs=4 penalty. C1/C2/C4 are within run noise of the R3b baseline (39.2 / 44.9 / 50.8), so the extra graph costs nothing there. Per-stream: 39.1 / 22.8 / 16.3 / 12.8 tok/s.
  - **B4 (`kt-cpu-per-step.sh`, 200 tok, C1–C4):** step wall 30.0 / 50.5 / 71.0 / 85.5 ms (+20.5, +20.5, +14.5 ms per added row; model 17.7); kt CPU-s/step 0.464 / 0.792 / 1.122 / 1.358; **"kt threads busy" 15.6–15.8 at every C** (`kt-cpu-per-step-b1/`). The busy figure is the tell: kt-kernel workers spin 50 ms before sleeping and no per-layer wait is that long, so all 16 workers burn CPU for the whole step whether they compute or wait — **CPU-seconds ≈ 16 × step time and cannot separate compute from serialisation.** B4's question (is the tier compute-bound at C4?) is therefore *not answered* by this instrument; it needs either the kt worker-side timers or a perf-counter view (instructions retired / DRAM reads per step), added as B4′ below. The accompanying bench rows (C1 39.20 / 33.91, C2 46.12 / 39.76, C3 48.79 / 42.33, C4 51.86 / 46.84) reproduce B1.
  - **B5 (C4, 1,024 tok, 2 runs, seed 9191):** 51.43 summed / **50.31 wall**, TTFT 1.80 s (`decode-b5-c4-1024.json`). Per-row cost is flat with output length — no growing expert-reuse benefit as streams lengthen; the wall/summed gap closes only because TTFT is amortised over 4× the tokens.
  - **B4′ (added):** measure CPU-tier *work* rather than CPU-seconds — `perf stat -e instructions,cycles,mem_loads` on the TP0 process per 200-token run at C1 vs C4 (needs `perf` + `kernel.perf_event_paranoid` allowance — owner), or DRAM GB/s from `hsmp-clocks.py --watch 1` during C1 vs C4 decode (controller counter; no privileges). If DRAM GB/s at C4 ≈ C1 while step time is 2.9×, the per-row cost is compute/handshake, not bandwidth.
  - **B2 (`KT_CPUINFER` at C4, D=4, with `hsmp-clocks.py --watch 1` during the C1 and C4 cells):** 14 threads — C1 38.64 / 33.20 wall (TTFT 1.12), C4 49.43 / 45.31 (1.89), DRAM busy-median 98 GB/s at C1 (p10–p90 77–108), 106 at C4 (95–110); 12 threads — C1 38.31 / 32.14 (1.30), C4 45.91 / 41.92 (2.17), DRAM 93.5 (47–102) / 99 (78–107) (`decode-b2-*.json`, `hsmp-b2-*.json`). 16 threads = B1. Fewer workers cost more at C4 (−12 %) than at C1 (−5 %) and pull proportionally less bandwidth; 14 vs 16 is flat in both. Same-prompt gate PASS at C4 on both launches.
  - **B3 (`KT_MAX_DEFERRED` 0 vs 4, 16 threads):** D=0 — C1 31.52 / 27.97 (TTFT 1.07), C4 45.67 / 42.55 (1.74); DRAM busy-median **64.5 GB/s at C1** (58–88), 96 at C4 (59–120) (`decode-b3-d0-*.json`, `hsmp-b3-d0-*.json`). Deferral is worth +21 % wall at C1 and +12 % at C4 — and the counter shows *how*: the same bytes move at 64.5 GB/s average when the CPU reads cannot overlap the GPU phase and at 98 GB/s when they can. Deferral hides the tier; it does not shrink it (confirms LEARNING §11). Gate PASS at C4; NLL at bs=1 1.48644 vs E21 D=0 1.48741 (ΔNLL −0.00097, z +0.27, `nll-b3-d0-bs1234.json`).
  - **B4′ result — the question the summary "step ≈ 8 + 17.7 × rows" raised is answered: the per-row cost is bytes.** At the default the controller moves ≈ 98 GB/s across the whole C1 step (≈ 2.5–2.9 GB/step; FEASIBILITY's 101 CPU experts × 17.9 MiB ≈ 1.9 GB of weights plus Engram/activations/pinned copies) and ≈ 106 GB/s at C4 (≈ 9 GB/step, ≈ 3.1× — near-zero expert reuse across 4 rows, as 2.75 CPU experts per token per layer out of 176 predicts). Against the 125 GB/s two-CCD ceiling (E25/E27) the tier is reading ≥ 78 % of the wall time at bs=1: **bandwidth-bound, not core-bound.** Bound for this placement: 2.5 GB/step at 125 GB/s = 20 ms = ≈ 50 tok/s summed C1 if overlap were perfect; realistic further overlap gains (D=5, scheduling) are single digits. So: H2 (more threads help more at C4) — *inverted*: fewer threads hurt more at C4, but 16 → 24 already lost (Part A), so the core count is not the lever; H3 (kt loops per token re-reading weights) — consistent with the byte scaling, still to be confirmed by reading `cpu_backend/moe*` (open). Caveats: socket-wide counter (GPU DMA, Engram page cache included), 1 s samples over 12–33 steps, so ± 15 % on bytes and the duty figure is a lower bound.
  - Consequences: (1) `serve-hybrid.sh` gains `CUDA_GRAPH_BS="1 2 3 4"` (default, ~~provisional — E24 soak pending~~ qualified by the E27 served soak 2026-09-23; `CUDA_GRAPH_BS=""` restores the derived list); (2) B4's `kt-cpu-per-step.sh` is retired as a compute-vs-wait instrument (TROUBLESHOOTING entry) — use `hsmp-clocks.py --watch 1` alongside any decode cell; (3) the FEASIBILITY / HETEROGENEOUS-ROUTING / MIGRATION-ROADMAP model: step ≈ 8 ms + 17.7 ms × rows *because each row streams ≈ 1.9 GB of CPU-expert weights through a ≈ 100–125 GB/s pipe*; "more cores" (a 96-core CPU) helps only through its 12 CCDs = 12 GMI links (≈ 6× reachable bandwidth), not its cores; (4) the four-agent router rule: 1 → 39.1, 2 → 22.8 each, 3 → 16.3 each, 4 → 12.8 each (summed); (5) single-user lever ranking unchanged in order, sharper in reason — placement (fewer bytes) > D=5 (more overlap, ≤ single digits) > kernel work (only if it reduces bytes or raises achieved bandwidth); FCLK is closed (E27).
- Measure: summed and wall tok/s, per-stream tok/s, TTFT, server `gen throughput` medians per `running=N`, kt CPU-s/step, host DRAM GB/s during C4 (`bench-dram-suite.py --monitor` or `perf stat` if allowed), GPU W/util per card, `verify-batch-consistency` pass at every cell.
- Quality gates for any new default (e.g. capturing bs=3): `verify-batch-consistency.py --same-prompt` at C2, C3, C4; `nll-score.py` at bs=1 unchanged (bit-identical expected); the E24 20K-token mixed soak with zero NaN / Xid.
- Pass: a per-row cost model with measured coefficients replaces "near-linear batching" in FEASIBILITY, HETEROGENEOUS-ROUTING (router / four-agent advice, third-GPU C4 projection) and MIGRATION-ROADMAP; C3 no longer trails C2; the kt C++ reading names the loop structure at M > 1 and either an upstream kernel that batches rows or a concrete kernel task for the migration roadmap.
- Decision fed: `KT_CPUINFER` and `KT_MAX_DEFERRED` defaults in `serve-hybrid.sh` (C1-driven); the captured-bs list in `serve-hybrid.sh`; whether "more cores" (96-core CPU) is a real lever for concurrency (it is only if CPU-s/step scales with rows); the four-agent router rule (today: one agent gets 39 tok/s, four get 12.7 each); kernel-track priority for a row-batched CPU MoE kernel.
- Learning pointers: LEARNING.md §10 E23/E26 rows, §11 "a too-good scaling result is a bug signal".

## E27 — BIOS Tier 3: which fabric-side clock caps the CPU at 125 GB/s — FCLK (CCD↔IOD GMI) or UCLK/MEMCLK (controller)? (owner-run cold boots; no GPU serve until a candidate passes the host gate; T1.11 Tier 3, BIOS guide §6)

- Status: **DONE — slot 3 fully qualified 2026-09-23 (Boot 12 served soak passed; result paragraph below).** *Earlier:* REOPENED and PASSING (Boot 7, T3-F1v). *Soak launch note (Boot 12):* the first soak launch with `KT_NAN_PROBE_MIN_TOKENS=1` died at decode-graph capture — the probe's host sync is illegal under capture (overlay fix `cad73914`, TROUBLESHOOTING entry); not a BIOS effect. Second launch clean, graphs captured, `verify-batch-consistency` C4 PASS (min prefix 502). After the Auto-voltage arms closed (below), the owner raised VDDG CCD/IOD to 1050 mV and VSOC to 1.20 V and FCLK 2100 booted clean: 0 MCE / EDAC 0 over ≈ 45 min incl. a full serve; per-CCD read +6.0 %, socket +5.5 %, kt one-layer −3.1…−4.3 %, **C1 37.19 wall (+3.1 %), C3 +3.5 %, C4 +3.8 %, NLL identical, prefill unchanged** — H-GMI confirmed from both sides. Voltage floor found the same day (Boots 8–9): clean and identical bandwidth at VSOC 1.10 / VDDG 1000 and at **VSOC 1.05 / VDDG 950 = floor**, idle power back to 64–68 W (Auto 57–65), 30-min `stressapptest` clean. FCLK 2200 reached GRUB but the kernel would not boot at both allowed voltage rungs (Boots 10–11) → fabric ceiling = 2100 within the allowed voltage. Boot 12 = slot 3's second clean cold boot → **slot 3 (FCLK 2100 / VSOC 1.05 / VDDG 950) is the standing BIOS profile**; owed: served 20K soak + NLL at the next GPU window. Result paragraphs below. *Earlier status:* T3-0 done (Boot 3); T3-F1 FAILED at Auto voltage (Boots 4–5) — corrected-MCE storm on the GMI link at FCLK 2100 / Auto voltage, FCLK arm closed (T3-F2 struck); **T3-M1 run (Boot 6) — clean boot, FCLK held at 2000 by HSMP, per-CCD read 62.7 → 62.7 (0 %), kt one-layer −2.3 % (noise), latency-bound rows −7 to −10 %: NOT adopted, T3-M2 struck, MEMCLK arm closed.** Owner reverts to slot 2 (EXPO 6400, FCLK Auto). Both result paragraphs below the arms. Standing configuration = slot 2. Tier 3 at Auto voltage is exhausted (a voltage-assisted FCLK retry, T3-F1v, remains an owner option — see the T3-F1 result); the remaining levers past 125 GB/s are placement (T3.6g2), hardware (H1 more CCDs, third GPU) and the native roadmap. Result paragraph below the arms. Overclocking approved in principle by the owner (2026-09-23); each boot's exact values are still set by the owner. Protocol and sources: [`docs/research/BIOS-TIER3-OVERCLOCK-RESEARCH.md`](../docs/research/BIOS-TIER3-OVERCLOCK-RESEARCH.md) §2 (the research thread's document, Zen 5 / WRX90 sources where they exist, Zen 4 and single-system reports labelled as such).
- Hypothesis (H-GMI): the 62–63 GB/s per-CCD read ceiling is one narrow GMI link at 32 B/clk × FCLK; at the measured FCLK 2000 MHz that is 64 GB/s per CCD, 128 GB/s for the socket, matching E25/Boot 3 (63.1 / 125–126) to 2 %. Then FCLK +5 % → per-CCD ≈ 66 GB/s, socket ≈ 131, kt one-layer −4 to −5 %, C1 ≈ +2–2.5 % (CPU tier ≈ half the step). Competing hypothesis (H-UMC): the controller clock (UCLK, Auto-derived from MEMCLK, not readable) binds instead — then FCLK changes nothing and MEMCLK 6600/6800 at fixed FCLK moves the ceiling. Boot 2 (JEDEC 4800, −11 %) is consistent with either because its FCLK was never read.
- **T3-0 result (Boot 3, EXPO 6400 restored, no BIOS change):** `scripts/hsmp-clocks.py` (kernel `hsmp_acpi`, `/dev/hsmp`, unprivileged) reads **FCLK 2000 MHz, MCLK 3200 MHz** — 52/52 samples, idle and under the host suite (APBDIS=1 pins it). The controller's DDR-bandwidth counter peaks at **174 GB/s of 410 theoretical** in the copy phase while cores read ≤ 125 GB/s, so the DIMM/controller side has ≥ 40 % headroom over what the cores can pull: the ceiling is upstream of DRAM. Host suite = Boot 1 within spread (per-CCD 62.2–63.1, socket 125–126, copy 115, triad 123, expert-chunk 121.7). UCLK not observable (HSMP 7 has no UCLK message). Evidence `evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot3-expo-restored/`.
- Arms (one cold boot each, BIOS guide §5 gate: EDAC CE/UE = 0, no MCE/Xid, training clean, 30-min `stressapptest`, host suite + kt one-layer; serve + NLL ≤ 0.002 + needle + 20K soak only for a candidate worth keeping; stop and revert on any error; manual voltage is a separate approval-gated tier per the owner's directive — *the earlier "never add voltage to rescue a clock" wording here was the protocol authors' rule, not the owner's; corrected 2026-09-23*):
  - **T3-F1** FCLK = 2100 MHz, MEMCLK 6400 (EXPO), UCLK mode Auto, all voltages Auto. Pass if HSMP reads 2100 and per-CCD read ≥ 65.5 GB/s (≥ +4 %, outside the ±1 % Boot 1/Boot 3 spread); falsifies H-GMI if the ceiling stays 62–63 with the clock confirmed. **→ FAILED 2026-09-23 (Boots 4–5): never reached the measurement — the OS boot flooded with corrected MCEs from MC bank 16 (Global Memory Interconnect PCS Unit); see result below.** **→ T3-F1v (Boot 7, owner-chosen VDDG 1050 mV / VSOC 1.20 V): PASSED — per-CCD 66.4 (+6.0 %), C1 37.19 wall (+3.1 %), 0 MCE; floor VSOC 1.05 / VDDG 950 (Boot 9); FCLK 2200 failed at both allowed rungs (Boots 10–11) → ceiling 2100; slot 3 standing after Boot 12.**
  - ~~**T3-F2** FCLK = 2200 MHz, otherwise as F1; only after F1 passes. Expect ≈ 69–70 per CCD if H-GMI holds; a non-monotonic result (F2 < F1) is a rejection, not a prompt to tune.~~ **Struck 2026-09-23: unreachable — F1 failed and the protocol forbids adding voltage to rescue a clock.**
  - **T3-U1** only if a `UCLK DIV1 MODE` item exists on BIOS 1317 for this CPU *and* forcing it changes anything measurable — a Shimada Peak owner's AMD correspondence (community-posted, indirect) says selectable 1:1 is not supported; skip if absent or ineffective.
  - **T3-M1** MEMCLK 6600 with FCLK fixed at the established value, profile timings/voltages, only if the FCLK arm does not move the ceiling. **→ RUN 2026-09-23 (Boot 6): boots clean, FCLK confirmed 2000, ceiling unchanged (62.7 per CCD), kt flat, latency rows −7 to −10 % — NOT adopted; see result below.** ~~**T3-M2** MEMCLK 6800, only after M1 fully passes.~~ **Struck 2026-09-23: M1 showed no memory clock moves a GMI-bound ceiling while FCLK stays 2000; 6800 would only deepen the latency loss.**
- Measure per boot: `hsmp-clocks.py --watch 1` idle and during the suite (FCLK, MCLK, controller GB/s, socket W); `bench-dram-suite.py --only scaling,patterns,expert --iters 6`; `test-kt-one-layer.py --bench-cold` layer 5, T=1,2,3,4,8; EDAC counters before/after stress; `k10temp`/DIMM temperatures; for a keeper, `bench-decode.py` C1–C4 with distinct prompts and the quality gates.
- **T3-F1 result (2026-09-23, Boots 4–5) — FAILED at the boot gate.** Owner set Infinity Fabric Frequency and Dividers = 2100 MHz from the slot-2 profile (EXPO 6400 / 1.40 V, UCLK Auto, every voltage Auto) and cold-booted twice. POST and DRAM training passed both times; the kernel logged corrected machine checks from 1.0–2.7 s after boot at ≈ 500 events/s (127,156 and 70,871 `Hardware Error` journal lines in 32 s and 23 s; `mce_gen_pool_process hogged` workqueue warnings). Every event carries the same decode: **MC bank 16 = Global Memory Interconnect PCS Unit, Ext. Error Code 6, status `0x9820000000060150` (corrected, MiscV, SyndV), IPID `0x0000024113309200`, syndrome `0x0000812d4a000000`, FRU `Perr S0:T000:B10`, `cache level: RESV, tx: INSN, mem-tx: IRD`**. DRAM EDAC CE/UE = 0 in both boots — the DIMMs and controller were clean, the CCD↔IOD link that FCLK clocks was not. HSMP could not confirm 2100 (no shell was reached), so the clock is "not confirmed by HSMP; the error bank is the FCLK domain, so the setting evidently took effect". Owner reverted to profile slot 2 (FCLK Auto); the reverted boot reads FCLK 2000 / MCLK 3200 (52/52 samples), EDAC 0/0, 0 MCE lines. **Interpretation:** stop criterion hit (any MCE, corrected or not, ends the tier); T3-F2 struck; rescuing 2100 with VDDG/VDDP/VSOC is forbidden by the protocol and not proposed — it would need a separate explicit approval naming the voltages, for a ≈ +2 % C1 bound on a fabric without ECC. H-GMI is neither confirmed nor falsified (no bandwidth measured at 2100); the result is *consistent* with the GMI link being the tightly-margined path, which is what the H-GMI ceiling story also says. Evidence: [`evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot4-t3f1-fclk2100/`](../evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot4-t3f1-fclk2100/) (`mce-summary.txt` with per-boot `uniq -c` field counts, `hsmp-after-revert.{json,txt}`, `edac-after-revert.txt`; PPIN and hostname redacted). Forensics recipe that was enough: `journalctl --list-boots`, then `journalctl -k -b -1 --no-pager | grep -c 'Hardware Error'` and the decoded `Bank`/`Ext. Error Code` lines. Lever set after this: T3-M1/M2 (owner discretion), hardware (H1 more CCDs, third GPU), placement (T3.6g).
- **T3-M1 result (2026-09-23, Boot 6) — clean, null on the ceiling, negative on latency; NOT adopted.** Owner set DDR5-6600 from the slot-2 profile (EXPO timings/1.40 V otherwise, FCLK Auto, UCLK Auto, all voltages Auto); POST and training passed first time. `hsmp-clocks.py`: **FCLK 2000 MHz, MCLK 3300 MHz** in 86/86 samples idle and through the suite — the fabric clock was held while the memory clock rose 3 %, which is the single-variable test the arm was designed to be. EDAC ce/ue 0/0 before and after; zero `Hardware Error` lines. **Suite (two runs, vs Boot 3 at 6400):** per-CCD read 62.7 → 62.7 and 62.6 GB/s (0 %); socket read 125.7 → 125.6 / 124.4; expert-chunk 121.7 → 121.8; expert-rows 122–125 → 120–125 (−0.3 to −1.7 %); write 78.4 → 78.0, write-nt 96.9 → 97.1, copy-nt 136.2 → 136.8.
