Controller counter peak 159 GB/s (Boot 3: 174) while the cores read 125 — DRAM headroom again. **kt one-layer** (layer 5, 208 GPU experts, 3 CPU slots/token, cold, 40 iters): T=1 0.603 → 0.589 ms (−2.3 %), T=2 1.109 → 1.136 (+2.4 %), T=3 1.603 → 1.599, T=4 2.091 → 2.097, T=8 3.965 → 4.034 (+1.7 %) — all inside the run spread E25 established (0.590 ↔ 0.603 between identical boots). **Reproducible regressions (both runs, non-overlapping six-iteration ranges):** 1-thread read 56.7 → 50.9 / 52.0 GB/s, split ×2 read 108.4 → 97.2 / 99.1, 16-thread copy 115.2 → 104.6 / 105.0, triad 122.9 → 114.4 / 114.6, 8-thread copy-nt 69.7 → 67.8. **Interpretation:** the memory clock cannot move a ceiling that sits on the CCD↔IOD link — H-GMI predicted a null here and got one, and Boot 2's −11 % at JEDEC 4800 is therefore best read as Auto lowering the *fabric* clock at 4800 (its FCLK was never read, so this is the best-supported reading, not a measurement; H-UMC is not strictly excluded). The latency loss with bandwidth flat is what a looser controller path gives — Auto timings above the EXPO profile and/or a UCLK 1:2 divider engaging above 6400 (the Zen 4 rule's prediction) — and neither UCLK nor the effective timings are observable from Linux, so the cause is inferred. **Gate:** fails adoption criterion 1 (≥ +4 % per-CCD; 0 %) outright; C1 serve and the 30-min stress were not run (a candidate that cannot move the host comparators cannot pass ≥ +2 % C1, and the DIMMs are above rating). **Owner reverts to slot 2.** T3-M2 struck. **Tier 3 at Auto voltage closed:** FCLK has no margin at +5 % (Boots 4–5), MEMCLK has no effect (Boot 6). Memory voltage/timings cannot help (no mechanism — the wall is the fabric link, and latency does not move the hybrid, Boot 1); SoC-side voltage for FCLK (**T3-F1v**: 2100 MHz, VDDG CCD/IOD ≈ 950 mV or VSOC +50 mV, approval-gated, payoff bound ≈ +2–2.5 % C1) is the one voltage experiment with a mechanism, offered to the owner and not recommended by the agent. Evidence: [`evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot6-t3m1-memclk6600/`](../evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot6-t3m1-memclk6600/) (`suite-cpu.{json,txt}`, `suite-cpu-repeat.{json,txt}`, `hsmp-idle.{json,txt}`, `hsmp-under-suite.txt`, `layer5-gpu208-cpuslots3.{json,txt}`). Lever set after this: placement (T3.6g2, chunk 2048 → more GPU experts), hardware (H1 more CCDs, third GPU), native roadmap.
- **T3-F1v result (2026-09-23, Boot 7) — FCLK 2100 holds with SoC voltage; H-GMI CONFIRMED; all gates pass on boot 1 of 2.** Owner's change set from slot 2 (memory back at EXPO 6400): FCLK 2100 MHz, VDDG CCD 1050 mV, VDDG IOD 1050 mV (Auto ≈ 903/863, 903/830), VSOC 1.20 V (Auto 0.924), UCLK Auto. HSMP FCLK 2100 / MCLK 3200 in every sample (idle; 61/61 under the suite; 120/120 under C1 decode). **Health:** 0 `Hardware Error` lines, EDAC ce/ue 0/0 after ≈ 45 min spanning suite, kt one-layer, model load, C1/C3/C4, NLL, 32K prefill (Boots 4–5 at Auto voltage: ≈ 500 corrected GMI PCS MCEs/s from t+1 s). **Host suite vs Boot 3:** per-CCD read 62.7 → **66.4 GB/s (+6.0 %)**; socket read 125.7 → **132.0 (+5.5 %)** (split ×4 131.9 — four threads still saturate); write 78.4 → 81.5; write-nt 96.9 → 102.5; copy-nt 136.2 → 140.4; expert-chunk 121.7 → 127.2 (+4.6 %); expert-rows 122–126 → 125–131; single-thread read 56.7 → 57.4 (latency-bound, barely moves — the gain is link width × clock, as H-GMI says); controller counter peak 176 GB/s. Prediction was 66 / 131. **kt one-layer** (layer 5, 208 GPU experts, 3 CPU slots, cold, 40 iters): T=1 0.603 → 0.582 ms (−3.5 %), T=2 1.109 → 1.072 (−3.3 %), T=3 1.603 → 1.554 (−3.1 %), T=4 2.091 → 2.002 (−4.3 %), T=8 3.965 → 3.836 (−3.3 %); mins moved the same way (0.461 → 0.422 at T=1). **Serve** (defaults 226/158, 0.96, bs 1 2 3 4, D=4, spec off; GPUs at the 300 W default — irrelevant, peak draw 160 W decode / 148 W prefill): batch-consistency PASS; **C1 42.61 summed / 37.19 wall tok/s vs E28 41.23 / 36.08 (+3.3 % / +3.1 %)**, TTFT 0.907 s; **C3 54.92 / 50.44 vs 53.04 / 48.72 (+3.5 %)**; **C4 58.19 / 53.87 vs 56.18 / 51.91 (+3.6 % / +3.8 %)**; NLL vs E22 stream **+0.00182, z −0.48, 143/148 — identical to E28's 226 result**; 32K prefill 846 tok/s / 37.9 s (E28 845 / 38.1). HSMP DRAM busy-median during C1 ≈ 107 GB/s (E28 at 226: 90) — the CPU tier is pulling more bytes per second, which is the mechanism. **Cost:** socket power idle 57–65 → 87 W; suite peak 152 → 191 W (Tctl 53 → 62 °C); C1 decode median ≈ 206 → 277 W, peak 270 → 323 W (+60–70 W) — V² on the IOD/GMI rails plus more work; ≈ +65 W for +1.1 tok/s. **Interpretation:** the per-CCD GMI link is the CPU ceiling and FCLK is its knob — +5 % clock gave +6 % link bandwidth, −3.5 % CPU-tier time, +3 % decode at every concurrency (the CPU tier is the same fraction of the step at bs 1–4). The Boot 4–5 failure was voltage margin, not a clock limit. **Adoption (E27 pass criteria: two clean cold boots, ≥ +4 % per-CCD ✓, ≥ +2 % C1 ✓, zero CE/UE/MCE ✓ so far):** owed — (1) voltage-floor step, VSOC 1.10 / VDDG 1.00 (clean → optionally 1.05 / 0.95 once; MCE → back to 1.20 / 1.05, floor found); performance is voltage-independent once the link is clean, only the power tax and margin move; (2) second clean cold boot at the final values; (3) 20K mixed soak + 30-min memory stress at the final values. Owner saves the set as profile slot 3; slot 2 stays the fallback. When adopted, repo projections move from `--dram-gbps 125` to 131–132. Evidence: [`evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot7-t3f1v-fclk2100-vddg1050-vsoc1200/`](../evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot7-t3f1v-fclk2100-vddg1050-vsoc1200/).
- **T3-F1v voltage floor (2026-09-23, Boots 8–9) — clean at VSOC 1.10 / VDDG 1000 and at VSOC 1.05 / VDDG 950; bandwidth identical to Boot 7 at every voltage; floor fixed at Boot 9 by rule.** Both boots POSTed first time, HSMP FCLK 2100 / MCLK 3200 in every sample (Boot 9: idle, 85/85 under the suite, every 5 s sample under the 30-min stress), 0 `Hardware Error`, EDAC 0/0. Suite medians Boot 7 / 8 / 9 (GB/s): ccd0 ×8 66.0 / 66.0 / 65.2; ccd1 ×8 66.4 / 66.3 / 66.3; socket ×16 132.0 / 131.3 / 130.9; write-nt 102.5 / 102.6 / 102.4; copy-nt 140.5 / 140.4 / 140.8; triad 125.5 / 125.7 / 125.4; expert-chunk 127.2 / 129.4 / 127.0. kt one-layer T=1 0.582 / 0.581 / 0.583 ms, T=8 3.836 / 3.803 / 3.849. Power: idle socket 87–92 / 67–72 / **64–68 W** (Auto 57–65); suite peak 191 / 172 / **165 W** (Auto 152). Boot 9 `stressapptest -M 200000 -s 1800 -m 16 -W` (200 GiB, byte-verified random copies): **PASS — 249 TB copied and byte-verified in 1800 s at 138 GB/s, 0 hardware incidents, 0 errors; kernel `Hardware Error` count 0 and EDAC ce/ue 0/0 afterwards; FCLK 2100 in 367/367 HSMP samples during the run; socket power median 163 W, peak 178 W.** **20-min `stress-ng --cpu 8 --cpu-method all --matrix 8 --matrix-method all --vm 4 --vm-bytes 32G --verify`** (all-core FPU/AVX + memory, self-verifying): PASS 20/20 stressors, 0 failed; FCLK 2100 in 256/256 samples; 0 `Hardware Error`, EDAC 0/0; socket median 234 W, peak 279 W, Tctl peak ≈ 82 °C, no throttle events (`stress-ng-20min.txt`, `hsmp-under-stress-ng.txt`). **Reading:** the fabric link's bandwidth is a function of FCLK alone once it trains clean; SoC voltage sets the margin and the power tax. Boot 9 costs ≈ +7 W idle / +13 W peak over Auto for +6 % per-CCD bandwidth and +3 % decode — the Boot 7 "+65 W for 1.1 tok/s" figure was the voltage, not the clock. **No step below Boot 9** (ladder rule: find a floor with margin, not the minimum that boots). Adoption: Boot 9 → profile slot 3; owed the second clean cold boot and the served 20K soak at these voltages; the Boot 7 serve results (C1 37.19 wall, C3 50.44, C4 53.87, NLL identical) stand for the profile because the CPU tier measures identically. Evidence: [`evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot8-t3f1v-floor-vddg1000-vsoc1100/`](../evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot8-t3f1v-floor-vddg1000-vsoc1100/), [`evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot9-t3f1v-floor-vddg950-vsoc1050/`](../evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot9-t3f1v-floor-vddg950-vsoc1050/).
- **T3-F2v FCLK 2200 (Boots 10–11) FAILED; Boot 12 second cold boot CLEAN → slot 3 adopted (2026-09-23).** 2200 at VSOC 1.05 / VDDG 950 and at 1.10 / 1000 both passed POST/training, reached GRUB, and the kernel would not boot (no journal entries) — a margin failure one notch past the Boots 4–5 MCE storm, which the two allowed voltage rungs did not close. Ladder rule stops there — 2100 is this 9955WX's fabric ceiling within +0.18 V VSOC / +140 mV VDDG of Auto. Boot 12 (slot 3 reloaded): HSMP 2100 idle + 60/60; 0 MCE, EDAC 0/0; suite identical to Boots 7–9 (ccd ×8 66.0 / 66.2, socket 130.8, expert-chunk 130.0 GB/s); kt one-layer rerun T=1 0.575 / T=4 2.005 / T=8 3.941 ms (first run: one-off 6.0 ms T=1 median with normal min and normal T≥2 — transient, both files kept). **E27 pass criteria met:** two clean cold boots ✓, ≥ +4 % per-CCD ✓ (+6 %), ≥ +2 % C1 ✓ (+3.1 %, Boot 7 serve), zero CE/UE/MCE ✓, 30-min memory stress ✓ (stressapptest) + 20-min compute stress ✓ (stress-ng). ~~Owed: served 20K soak + NLL at these voltages (E24-style) at the next GPU window; then move repo projections to `--dram-gbps 131`.~~ Both done (next paragraph). Evidence: [`evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot12-slot3-2nd-cold-boot/`](../evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot12-slot3-2nd-cold-boot/).
- **Boot 12 served soak (2026-09-23) — PASS; slot 3 fully qualified; the capture-list and 226/158 defaults lose their "provisional" tag.** Server at the launcher defaults (226/158, 0.96, bs 1 2 3 4, D=4, spec off, streamed prefill; GPUs at the 300 W default) with `KT_NAN_PROBE_MIN_TOKENS=1`; `verify-batch-consistency` C4 PASS (min prefix 502). Eight `bench-decode.py` blocks, 12 runs × 512 tokens each, distinct prompts (`--seed 4242`, fixture offsets 0/4/2/8/6/12/0/9), 44 min, **141,312 decoded tokens**, every block health 200: C4 **58.09 / 58.06 / 58.97 summed, 55.79 / 55.80 / 57.39 wall**; C3 **56.00 / 55.63, 54.38 / 54.14**; C2 **52.12 / 50.27, 49.80 / 47.73**; C1 **42.02 summed / 40.08 wall**, TTFT 0.61 s (Boot 7's C1 fixture gave 42.61 / 37.19 with a 0.91 s TTFT — same summed rate, the wall difference is the prompt length in TTFT). Then NLL vs E22 stream **+0.00182, z −0.48, 143/148 of 4,795** (identical to Boot 7 and E28) and a 16K prefill at **859.6 tok/s** (15,978 tok in 18.6 s). Health: 0 `nan` / `probability tensor` / `Xid` / `Traceback` lines in the server log, 0 kernel `Hardware Error`/Xid, EDAC ce/ue 0/0, HSMP **FCLK 2100 in 781/781 samples** over 65 min, socket ≈ 103–108 W idle-after; GPUs peak 169 W (GPU0) / 183 W (GPU1), 76 / 74 °C, utilization median 23 % / 94 % (rank 0 waits on the host, rank 1 spins in NCCL — LEARNING). Teardown left no phantom-busy GPU0 (0 %, 2 MiB). **C2 finally measured at the current defaults, 50–52 summed / 48–50 wall — monotone between C1 and C3, so the "bs=2 anomaly" was the unpatched-kt C3/C4 artifact making C2 look low, not a C2 problem (research notes §5 item closed).** Consequences: (1) `serve-hybrid.sh` comments and E26/E28 status drop "provisional"; (2) E27 closed — all pass criteria met including the soak; (3) `--dram-gbps` default 125 → 131 (commit `3384f18`); (4) the E24 NaN root cause is unchanged — this soak was D=4 / bs ≤ 4 and never touched the D≥5 / 1,024-token conditions where the asserts fired, so it qualifies the defaults, not the defect. Evidence: [`evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot12-slot3-2nd-cold-boot/soak/`](../evidence/bios-trials/2026-09-23-boot12-slot3-2nd-cold-boot/soak/) (`c*-off*.json`, `nll.json`, `prefill-16k.json`, `verify-c4.json`, `launch.txt`, `gpu-during-soak.csv`, `hsmp-during-soak.txt`, `soak.out`).
- Pass (experiment): each arm's result identifies which clock binds and by how much per MHz; **Pass (adoption):** a clock that passes the whole gate on two separate cold boots, ≥ +4 % per-CCD read and ≥ +2 % C1 wall, zero CE/UE/MCE, and no voltage above Auto. Not adopted as a standing default merely because it boots.
- Expected payoff bound: 17 of ≈ 35 ms per bs=1 step is CPU-tier (E21/E26 figures; the research doc's 28.6 tok/s baseline is that rounded step, not the qualified 34.0 wall / 39.2 summed C1) → +5 % bandwidth ≈ +2.4 % decode, +10 % ≈ +4.6 %, +15 % ≈ +6.8 %. Small, so clock-only at Auto voltage; no timing/voltage campaign.
- Decision fed: BIOS guide §6 Tier 3 standing configuration; LEARNING §8 GMI row and §11 "faster DIMMs would not help" (already amended: the DIMMs are not the limit, the fabric-side clock is — and it is a knob); HARDWARE "CPU-reachable bandwidth" tier; whether the 96-core / more-CCD upgrade path (H1) is the only way past 125 GB/s. **Fed 2026-09-23:** standing configuration = slot 2 (EXPO 6400, FCLK Auto 2000, Boot 1 policy); the fabric clock is a knob with zero margin on this sample and the memory clock is not a knob at all for the CPU tier — H1 (more CCDs) and placement are the only ways past 125 GB/s.
- Learning pointers: LEARNING.md §10 E25 rows (Boot 2 and Boot 3 surprises), §8 host hardware table.

## E28 — Expert placement: how many of the 384 experts per layer fit on the GPUs once the KV pool is sized for the served config, and what does each one buy? (GPU; T3.6g)

- Status: **done 2026-09-23.** 208 → 222 → 226 GPU experts per layer; **226 / 158 is the new launcher default** (~~provisional until the E24 20K soak runs on the new defaults~~ **qualified 2026-09-23 by the E27 served soak: 141K tokens, 0 NaN, NLL identical**). Result table in BENCHMARKS "E28"; evidence [`evidence/t36g-placement/`](../evidence/t36g-placement/).
- Question: E26 A4 found the KV pool sized at 1,933,056 tokens (≈ 3.3 GB/rank) on a server that admits 4 × 32K = 131K, and E26 B4′ showed the CPU tier is bandwidth-bound — every expert moved off it removes bytes from the only saturated pipe. How many experts does the reclaimed VRAM hold at 32K × 4 slots with `CHUNKED_PREFILL_SIZE=4096`, and does the byte model (E26: step ≈ 8 + 17.7 × rows ms, ≈ 1.9 GB of expert weights per row-step) predict the gain?
- Hypotheses: (H1) capping the pool at slots × context + one chunk of headroom (147,456) returns ≈ 3 GB/rank with no functional change (the pool was never more than ≈ 10 % used). (H2) 14 experts (222) fit and give ≈ +4 % C1 wall (−8 % bytes, ≈ 60 % of which shows at C1 because ≈ 15 ms of the step is GPU work). (H3) at C4 the gain equals the byte reduction, because the pipe is saturated. (H4) the ceiling at chunk 4096 is 226–228 — set by the dynamic prefill/decode allocations, not by KV.
- Protocol: per step — launch with `KT_NUM_GPU_EXPERTS=N MEM_FRACTION_STATIC=0.96 EXTRA_SERVER_ARGS="--max-total-tokens 147456"` (now the launcher defaults), read `Load weight end` / `Memory pool end` / `Capture … avail mem` from the launch log and persist it; `verify-batch-consistency.py --same-prompt --concurrency 4`; `bench-decode.py` C1 and C4 (C3 at the final step) with `hsmp-clocks.py --watch 1` beside them; `bench-prefill.py --tokens 32768 --runs 2` plus `nvidia-smi` after it; `nll-score.py --compare` against the E22 streamed-prefill file (the E21 files predate the streamed-prefill offset and must not be the comparator); and — added during the run because a single 32K prefill at C1 is not the worst case the config admits — **`scripts/stress-kv-full.py`: four concurrent ≈ 30K prompts with 256 output tokens, `nvidia-smi` sampled every second, report the peak per GPU.** Stop at the first failed capture, OOM, or worst-case free below ≈ 0.5 GB; keep the last count that passes everything.
- Results (325 W, patched image, spec off, `KT_CPUINFER=16`, D=4, capture list `1 2 3 4`, 256 tok, distinct prompts, seed 4242, 3 runs after 1 warmup):
  - **G1, 222 / 162:** weights 87.73 GB/rank (+4.76 for 14 experts → 0.34 GB per expert per rank ≈ 8.5 MiB per expert-layer per rank, ×2 ranks ≈ FEASIBILITY's 17.9 MiB); pool 0.60 GB (was 3.33); avail before capture 5.28 GB; after capture 92,358 / 92,415 MiB. Gate PASS (310). **C1 40.53 summed / 35.39 wall (+3.7 % / +4.3 %), TTFT 0.95 s; C4 54.08 / 50.12 (+5.3 % / +5.4 %).** DRAM busy-median 93.5 GB/s at C1 (p10–p90 54–104), 108 at C4 (91–113). 4K prefill 869 tok/s / 4.60 s; 32K 873 tok/s / 36.7 s, then 95,056 / 95,297 MiB used (≈ 2.6 GB free). Worst case 4 × 30K: min free 2,937 / 2,878 MiB, all finished. NLL 1.49165: vs E22 stream +0.0055 (z +1.5; 160 worse / 140 better >0.5 nats); vs E26 D=0 +0.0052 (z +1.4); vs E21 D=4 +0.0103 (z −2.59, wrong comparator — contains the E22 offset).
  - **G2, 226 / 158:** weights 89.01 GB/rank; avail before capture 4.00 GB; after capture 93,668 / 93,727 MiB. Gate PASS (310). **C1 41.23 / 36.08 (+5.5 % / +6.3 %), TTFT 0.91 s; C3 53.04 / 48.72 (+8.2 % / +8.3 %); C4 56.18 / 51.91 (+9.4 % / +9.2 %).** Per stream 41.2 / 17.7 / 14.0. DRAM 90 GB/s at C1 (37–106), 108 at C4 (85–112). 32K 845 tok/s / 38.1 s, then 96,286 / 96,345 MiB (≈ 1.5 GB free). **Worst case 4 × 30K: min free 779 MiB on GPU0 / 1,226 on GPU1**, all four finished (finish=length, 146 s wall, TTFTs 39 / 70 / 100 / 128 s — chunked prefill serialises the prompts), zero OOM; TP1 logged three Triton `device-loaded after serving started (free device mem: 0.97 GiB)` warnings (none at 222). NLL 1.48795: vs E22 stream **+0.0018 (z −0.48; 143 / 148)**.
  - Health across three launches: zero Xid, EDAC 0/0, no NaN in any log.
- Reading: H1 confirmed (pool 3.33 → 0.60 GB, no behavioural change). H2 confirmed exactly (+4.3 % wall at 222). H3 confirmed (C4 +9.2 % for −10.2 % bytes). H4: 226 is the ceiling — the four experts from 222 to 226 cost 1.28 GB of weights but 2.16 GB of worst-case peak on GPU0 (fragmentation + late Triton loads with less slack), so 228 would leave ≈ 0.1 GB and is not attempted. The ≈ 3.4 GB between after-capture and worst-case peak is prefill-chunk activations at 30K context plus bs=4 decode workspaces; that is the next placement budget and its price is prefill chunk size (T3.6g2), not KV. The quality readings at 222 and 226 (+0.0055 and +0.0018 against the same baseline) bracket the noise floor of the 4,795-token corpus for a tier swap: the per-token |Δlogp| ≈ 0.128 is the E22 offset (CPU kt numerics → GPU MXFP8-activation numerics) and is symmetric in both cases.
- Consequences: `serve-hybrid.sh` defaults `KT_NUM_GPU_EXPERTS=226`, `MEM_FRACTION_STATIC=0.96`, new `MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS` derived as `MAX_RUNNING_REQUESTS × CONTEXT_LENGTH + 16384` (`MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS=""` restores fraction-sized pools — needed before any 262K qualification); `CHUNKED_PREFILL_SIZE=8192` documented as incompatible with 226; `scripts/stress-kv-full.py` **followed by `nll-score.py` on the same server (E29 amendment: the `return_logprob` transient is the largest single allocation; a config that survives the stress run but dies on the logprob request has not passed)** is the fit gate for any future VRAM spend (placement, chunk, drafts). `verify-batch-consistency` + NLL + 32K + worst case pass; **E24 soak on the new defaults pending** (same status as the capture list). Third-GPU and router projections (HETEROGENEOUS-ROUTING) start from 226 / 158 and C1 36.1 wall.
- Not done / open: C2 at 226 (E26's 45.6 / 40.8 stands as the 208 figure); ~~the E24 soak~~ (E27 Boot 12 soak); ~~T3.6g2 (chunk 2048 → 4–8 more experts, projected +1.5–3 % C1 wall at a slower prefill — decide by C1 wall and TTFT together)~~ **run as E29 (2026-09-24): +1.6 % C1 wall for −39 % prefill and a failed NLL gate — rejected.**
- Learning pointers: LEARNING.md §10 E28 surprise row, §11 rows "placement pays what the byte model says" and "a 32K prefill at C1 is not the worst case".

## E29 — Placement round 2: prefill chunk 2048 → 230 GPU experts (T3.6g2)

**Status:** done 2026-09-24 (measured). **Rejected as default.** Evidence
`evidence/t36g2-placement-r2/`. Follow-up to E28; all runs with the user's
340 GB `hf download` writing to the separate `/mnt/data` NVMe (E28 measured
that at ≈ 0.6 % C1, so it does not change any reading below).

**Question.** E28 kept 226 GPU experts because the fit gate said the next
tier (230) has no VRAM headroom at `CHUNKED_PREFILL_SIZE=4096`. The largest
prefill transient scales with the chunk; does halving it to 2048 buy the
≈ 1.4 GB/rank that 230 needs, and is +4 experts worth whatever the smaller
chunk costs in prefill throughput?

**Hypotheses (before running).**
- H1 — chunk 2048 frees ≥ 1.5 GB/rank of worst-case headroom at 226
  (the 4×30K `stress-kv-full` peak drops by about that).
- H2 — 230 experts then fits the same worst case with ≥ 0.75 GB/rank free.
- H3 — decode gains follow the byte model: −2.5 % CPU expert bytes ⇒
  ≈ +1.5–2.5 % C1 wall, more at C3/C4.
- H4 — chunk 2048 costs prefill ≈ 10–20 % (smaller GEMMs, twice the
  per-chunk fixed cost: Engram gather + expert dispatch + CPU sync).
- H5 — NLL stays inside the ±0.002 gate (E28: the 226-tier swap read
  +0.0018).

**Protocol.** Eight launches (A–G below; two failed by design), D=4, spec
off, capture `1 2 3 4`, `KT_NAN_PROBE_MIN_TOKENS=1`, 300 W (never binds).
Fit gate per config = `stress-kv-full --concurrency 4 --prompt-tokens 30000
--max-tokens 256` **followed by** `nll-score.py` on the same server (the
E28 protocol ran NLL first; see "Reading" for why the order matters).
Decode: `bench-decode.py --max-tokens 512 --runs 12 --distinct-prompts
--seed 4242`, offsets C1 6 / C3 2 / C4 0. Prefill: `bench-prefill.py
--tokens 16384|32768 --runs 2 --seed 777`. Controls are the E27 soak / E28
D=4 readings at 226 / 4096 / 0.96.

| Launch | Config | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| A | chunk 2048 / 226 / 0.96 | H1, H4, chunk-only decode + NLL effect |
| B | chunk 2048 / 230 / 0.96 → **0.975** | H2, H3 (0.96 refused: "minimum viable 0.9633") |
| C | chunk 4096 / 226 / 0.96 (current default) | re-measure default worst case + NLL-after-stress |
| D | B + `SGLANG_LOGPROB_CHUNK_SIZE=512` | make the NLL request survive at 230 |
| E | A, fresh server | NLL of chunk 2048 alone, plus repeat on same server |
| F | B, fresh server, default logprob chunk | is the B OOM allocator-history dependent? |
| G | C + `SGLANG_LOGPROB_CHUNK_SIZE=512`, fresh | is the logprob chunk numerically neutral? |

**Results.**

VRAM (MiB used per rank; 97,887 total) and fit gate:

| Launch | After capture | Worst-case peak (4×30K) | Min free | Stress wall / TTFTs (s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A 2048/226 | 93,646 / 93,703 | 94,964 / 95,425 | **2,923 / 2,462** | 269; 86/142/198/252 |
| B 2048/230 (0.975) | 95,106 / 95,165 | 96,634 / 96,819 | 1,253 / 1,068 | 258; 82/135/189/241 |
| C 4096/226 (default) | 93,646 / 93,703 | 96,538 / 96,597 | 1,349 / 1,290 | 165; 58/88/119/147 |
| D 2048/230 + lp512 | 95,106 / 95,165 | 96,862 / 97,049 | 1,025 / 838 | 259 |

H1 confirmed: chunk 2048 at 226 frees ≈ 1.6 / 1.2 GB versus the same-night
default run C. Note C's own minimum (1,349 MiB) is ≈ 0.5 GB better than the
E28 reading (779 MiB) for the identical config — run-to-run allocator
variance is of that order, so the fit gate should be read with a ±0.5 GB
error bar. B at 230 needed `MEM_FRACTION_STATIC=0.975` (weights 90.41
GB/rank; +1.40 GB for the 4 experts; KV pool shrinks to 0.60 GB) and then
passed the stress run with ≥ 1.0 GB free (H2 confirmed, barely).

Decode (median summed / wall tok/s, TTFT s):

| Config | C1 | C3 | C4 |
|---|---|---|---|
| control 226 / 4096 (E28 D=4 / E27 soak) | 41.76 / 39.83 / 0.617 | 56.00 / 54.38 | 58.09 / 55.79 |
| A 226 / 2048 | 42.10 / 40.16 / 0.609 | — | — |
| B 230 / 2048 | **42.84 / 40.81 / 0.599** | 57.56 / 55.94 / 0.82 | 60.00 / 57.64 / 1.45 |

Chunk size is neutral on decode (A vs control +0.8 % wall, noise). The 4
extra experts give +1.6 % C1 wall vs A, +2.9 % C3, +3.3 % C4 — H3 confirmed,
the byte model still predicts tier swaps to within a point.
`verify-batch-consistency --same-prompt --concurrency 4` PASS at 230.

Prefill (tok/s, TTFT s):

| Config | 16K | 32K |
|---|---|---|
| control 226 / 4096 (E27 soak 16K; E28 32K) | 860 / 18.6 | 845 / 38.1 |
| A 226 / 2048 | 513.9 / 31.2 | 518.3 / 61.8 |
| D 230 / 2048 | 530.9 / 30.2 | — |

**H4 badly under-predicted: chunk 2048 costs −39 % prefill throughput**, not
10–20 %. The per-chunk fixed cost (rank-0 CPU expert sync, Engram gather,
Python launch) is larger than modelled; smaller GEMMs are the minor term.

NLL (`nll-score.py` vs E22 `nll-stream.json`, 4,795 tokens, gate |Δ| ≤ 0.002):

| Launch | When | mean NLL | Δ vs E22 | z (paired) | better/worse tokens |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E 2048/226 | fresh | 1.48647 | +0.00033 | −0.09 | 121/149 |
| E repeat | same server | 1.48647 | **bit-identical to E** | | |
| C 4096/226 | after stress | 1.48795 | +0.00182 | −0.48 | 143/148 |
| G 4096/226 + lp512 | fresh | 1.48795 | **Δ 0.0 vs C on every token** | | |
| D 2048/230 + lp512 | after stress | **1.49491** | **+0.00877 — FAIL** | −2.23 | 141/125 |
| B 2048/230, default logprob chunk | after stress | **server killed: CUDA OOM** in `input_logprob_processor._forward_by_chunk` (2048 × 129,280 × fp32 = 1.06 GB + 0.53 GB; 0.28–0.76 GB free) | | | |
| F 2048/230, default logprob chunk | fresh | **same OOM** → not allocator-history dependent | | | |

Attribution: chunk 2048 alone = +0.0003; logprob chunk 512 = exactly 0.0;
so the +0.0088 is experts 226–229 changing tier. C's reading is bit-identical
to the E28 G2 reading for the same config, and E's repeat is bit-identical
to E: **NLL is deterministic per config**, so the tier-swap deltas seen so
far (222: +0.0055; 226: +0.0018; 230: +0.0088; all vs the E22 reference
placement) are real distribution shifts, not measurement noise. H5 refuted
for 230.

GPU: peaks 167 / 179 W and 74 / 71 °C during the 2048-chunk runs (A, B);
141–146 W / 69 °C at the default chunk (C). 0 NaN / Xid / Traceback in
every launch log except the two logprob OOMs; EDAC 0/0 throughout.

**Reading.**
- Break-even (derivation, not measured): the decode gain of B over A is
  1/40.16 − 1/40.81 ≈ 0.0004 s per output token. A 16K prompt pays
  +12.6 s of TTFT ⇒ ≈ 31K output tokens to break even; a 2K prompt pays
  +1.5 s ⇒ ≈ 3.8K tokens. Only prompts under ≈ 1K tokens come out ahead,
  and C1 wall on a 300-token prompt is what the +2.5 % measures.
- The largest single VRAM transient the server produces is not the 4×30K
  decode; it is a `return_logprob` request materialising
  `logprob_chunk × vocab` fp32 logits (2048 × 129,280 × 4 B = 1.06 GB, plus
  a bf16 copy). E28's assumption that a CUDA OOM fails the request and not
  the server was wrong: the scheduler exception calls `kill_process_tree`.
- `SGLANG_LOGPROB_CHUNK_SIZE=512` (overlay `environ.py`, used by
  `layers/logprob_processor.py`) is numerically bit-identical and does not
  materially change NLL end-to-end latency (fresh 25.3 s vs 28.9 s; warm
  10.6 s vs 7.3 s). It cuts the transient 4×.

**Consequences.**
- **Reject `CHUNKED_PREFILL_SIZE=2048` + 230 as the default:** −39 %
  prefill and a failed NLL gate for +1.6 % C1 wall, and it needs a fourth
  knob (`0.975` + `lp512`) just to keep a logprob request from killing the
  server. Defaults stay **226 / 4096 / 0.96** (`serve-hybrid.sh`).
- **Launcher default `SGLANG_LOGPROB_CHUNK_SIZE=512`** (this commit):
  bit-identical output, free ≈ 0.8 GB of worst-case margin for any
  `return_logprob` caller, no measured latency cost. Overridable by env.
- E28 protocol amendment: the fit gate is `stress-kv-full` **then**
  `nll-score.py` on the same server; a config that passes the stress run
  but dies on the logprob request has not passed.
- The NLL gate's ±0.002 threshold was calibrated against measurement noise
  that turns out to be zero; a tier swap of 4 experts moves it by 0.0003–
  0.0088. TASKS gets a low-priority row to re-derive the threshold from a
  held-out corpus rather than from repeat variance (T3.6h).
- Chunk 2048 is still a legitimate knob for a deployment whose prompts are
  all short (< 1K tokens) and whose VRAM is under pressure — documented in
  the launcher comment, not enabled.

## E30 — Option B2 in a real forward: can the GPUs read DRAM-resident experts beside the CPU tier and turn E4b's additive bandwidth into decode throughput? (GPU; T3.18)

**Status:** done 2026-09-24 (measured). **Stop criterion met — B2 rejected as a
lever; code kept off by default.** Evidence `evidence/t318-b2-prototype/`
(README has the tables), overlay `7e41317e`. All runs at the qualified
defaults (226 / 158, D=4, spec off, chunk 4096, capture 1 2 3 4).

**Question.** E4b measured CPU DRAM reads and GPU DMA reads of the same DIMMs
as additive (234 GB/s); FEASIBILITY turned that into a "78 tok/s B2 ceiling"
and the migration roadmap made a rank-0 B2 prototype Stage 2 item 0. Does a
subset *S* of the CPU-resident experts computed by the GPUs *out of host
memory*, in parallel with kt-kernel computing the rest, shorten the decode
step?

**Hypotheses (before running).**
- H1 — the SM120 CUTLASS MXFP4 grouped GEMM runs correctly on weights whose
  storage is pinned host memory (UVA pointers) and reaches ≥ 70 % of the
  memcpy PCIe rate; no custom kernel or staging copy needed.
- H2 — it can be captured in the decode CUDA graph and replayed bit-equal.
- H3 — with |S| ≈ 40 (25 % of CPU bytes off the fabric) C1 wall improves
  ≥ +10 % (the go bar), following E28's "gain = CPU byte reduction".
- H4 — host DRAM contention from kt's own reads does not slow the UVA GEMM
  (the links, not the DIMMs, are the limit).

**Protocol.** (1) `scripts/bench-b2-uva-moe.py` on an idle GPU: raw PCIe
(memcpy, UVA copy kernel, UVA `index_select`), grouped GEMM on device vs UVA
weights at M = 1 / 4 with 0–6 valid B2 slots, graph replay, the same under a
130 GB/s host-read hog, and B2 on a side stream beside a 226-expert device
tier. (2) Overlay: pinned-host copy of the last |S| CPU experts per layer per
rank (built once from the E22 export path), `remap_topk_ids_for_b2`, the
GEMM via `gm.runner.run`, kt handed −1 for S. (3) Serve |S| = 40 with the GEMM
on the main stream; `verify-batch-consistency --same-prompt -c 4`, C1 (6 × 512
tokens, distinct prompts, seed 4242, offset 6), HSMP DRAM counter, GPU-only
`/start_profile`. (4) One change at a time: fork the GEMM onto a side stream
after `submit()`, join after `sync()`, gate to bs ≤ 1; re-serve 40, then 60.

**Result.**
1. H1 holds: UVA GEMM bit-equal to device weights; 6 hits at M=1 in 1.35 ms =
   39 GiB/s effective (memcpy 51–53 GiB/s) → **≈ 0.09 ms floor + 0.21 ms per
   expert hit** (8.96 MiB TP slice per rank). torch `index_select` over UVA is
   6–13 GiB/s — never gather through it.
2. H2 holds: graph replay 1.28 ms, bit-equal; forked beside the device tier
   the graphed time is max(tier, B2) (0.349 serial → 0.241 forked at h=1).
3. H4 holds: under the 130 GB/s host hog eager launches slow to ≈ 1 ms (host
   launch latency) but graph replay is unchanged (1.30 vs 1.28 ms).
4. **H3 fails.** Main stream, no token gate, |S| = 40: C1 **33.1 / 28.5 wall,
   TTFT 2.5 s** (baseline 41.9 / 39.9 / 0.61). Profile: M=1 GPU busy 25–30 of
   31–36 ms per step (baseline 15.4), 4 grouped GEMMs per layer, B2 ≈ 10–13
   ms/step serial on the critical path; short-prefill layers (M = 9–13) hit
   most of the 40 experts. Forked + gated to bs = 1: |S| = 40 → **42.71 /
   40.71 (+2 %)**, TTFT 0.617; C4 57.2 / 55.0 (unchanged, B2 off at bs=4);
   |S| = 60 → **40.65 / 38.73 (−3 %)**. Union GPU busy 17.3 → 19.8 ms/step;
   B2 GEMMs 4.9 → 7.3 ms/step, ≈ 70 % hidden at 40, the longest pole at 60.
   Same-prompt bs=1 (B2) vs bs=4 (no B2) agree at both sizes; 0 NaN, EDAC 0/0.
5. RAM: 14.8 GiB/rank pinned at 40 (available 62 GiB), 21.0 at 60
   (available 31); kt keeps its own copy of S, so ≥ 80 does not fit.

**Reading.** Per expert B2 is 1.4× slower than the path it replaces (0.21 vs
kt's 0.146 ms for a full 17.9 MiB expert at 125 GB/s; two 8.96 MiB slices over
two links = 85 GB/s, 106 GiB/s raw would still be 0.165 ms). A balanced split
(|S| ≈ 45–50 by the per-layer model, matching the measured peak between 40 and
60) saves at most ≈ 0.11 ms/layer ≈ 4 ms/step of *CPU-path* time — and D=4
(E21) already hides most of that path, while B2's own GEMM added ≈ 2 ms/step
of *un-hidden* GPU time. E28's "gain = byte reduction" held because moving an
expert to VRAM removes CPU bytes at ≈ zero added cost; B2 removes them at 1.4×
their cost. **Additive DRAM bandwidth (E4b) is real; additive throughput
needs the second reader to be at least as fast per unit of work as the first,
and one x16 link per rank is not.** The FEASIBILITY 78 tok/s ceiling is struck.
What remains un-hidden in the CPU path is its fixed per-layer part (E21's
≈ 0.45 ms, 40 submit/sync boundaries) — T3.19(a)'s per-layer timeline is the
instrument for that, and the third GPU (T4.1b) removes bytes the E28 way.

**Kept.** `KT_B2_UVA_EXPERTS` / `KT_B2_MAX_TOKENS` (default 0 / 1) as a
documented experiment; `_B2HostPool` + direct-UVA compute as a proven way to
run a grouped GEMM on pinned host weights on SM120 without a custom kernel;
the side-stream `fork`/`join` pattern for T3.20's Engram staging. T3.16i's
review pass decides whether the ≈ 300 overlay lines stay.

## E31 — CPU-tier route lookahead: how predictable are layer L+1's CPU experts from layer L, and how much CPU-pool idle is there to fill? (GPU, opt-in log; T3.25 follow-up, Stage 2 candidate)

**Status:** registered 2026-10-07, **pending**. Low-coupling, opt-in instrumentation in the
style of `KT_TIMELINE`; runs on any G1 gate serve. Build nothing until the probe says so.

**Question.** mlx-stream fills 81 % of its per-layer SSD reads before the layer's routes are
known by scoring layer L's router before attention (prefill) and layer L+1's router on layer L's
MoE input (decode), after Eliseev & Mazur arXiv:2312.17238. The analogue here is to hand
kt-kernel the predicted CPU experts of layer L+1 during layer L. T3.19a says the CPU tier is
throughput-bound and D=4 already queues four layers of work, so an early start only pays where
the pool is idle: the three segment starts after the Engram breaks and the D=0 last layer.
Two unknowns decide it: the prediction's top-6 hit rate on *this* model, and the pool's idle
time per step.

**Hypotheses.** (1) Hit rate of layer L+1's top-6 scored on layer L's MoE output (post-combine
residual) is ≥ 85 % per layer at bs 1; scored on the pre-attention residual it is lower. (2)
CPU-pool idle per step is ≤ 2.5 ms at bs 1 (the Engram gaps plus layer 39) and near zero at
bs 4. (3) Therefore the ceiling is ≈ 8 % C1 wall at bs 1 and ≈ 0 at C4; a miss costs one
expert of CPU compute (0.146 ms at the kt read rate, E30), so the build pays only above ~90 %.

**Protocol.** Opt-in `KT_ROUTE_PROBE=1`: in `kt_ep_wrapper`, for each MoE layer record the
real top-6 ids and the ids that layer L+1's gate (weights already resident) produces on the
current layer's input and output hidden states; sample every 8th step; write JSONL beside the
timeline. Pool idle: from the T3.19a timeline, per layer, `sync` wait = 0 and no deferred backlog
→ idle interval; sum per step. Traffic: C1 and C4 standard recipes. Report per-layer hit rate for
the CPU-resident subset only (the 158 ids), overall and at segment starts.

**Go / stop.** Go to a dispatch build (behind the T3.19b `CpuExpertProvider` seam) only if
CPU-subset top-6 hit rate ≥ 90 % at bs 1 *and* measured pool idle ≥ 1.5 ms/step; else record
the hit rates as a routing-locality fact (they also inform placement, E28) and close.

## E32 — HumanEval pass@1 greedy on the served hybrid: a public quality number beside the NLL gate (GPU, ~20 min; T3.25 follow-up)

**Status:** registered 2026-10-07, **done 2026-10-09** on the 262K × 4 chunk-4096 serve (`evidence/e32-humaneval/`): **161/164 = 0.9817** pass@1 greedy (mlx-stream 3.0 bpw: 158/164 = 0.9634); failed ids 132, 140, 145; of mlx-stream's six failures we pass 3/6, excluding those six 158/158; decode median 42.23 tok/s, TTFT median 1.049 s (max 2.01), median completion 166 tokens, finish {'stop': 164}, 962 s wall. Hypothesis (1) ≥ 158: met; (2) decode ≥ 38 / TTFT < 1 s: decode met, TTFT not. Scorer `scripts/bench-humaneval.py` (generate through chat, temperature 0, thinking off, max_tokens 4096; score in a `--network none` throwaway container, 20 s per problem). Hypothesis (2)'s TTFT bar (< 1 s) is missed by 5 % — chat-template prompts of ~200 tokens through the chunk-4096 serve; the C1 review fixture on the same serve read 0.618 s.

**Question.** The frozen-corpus NLL gate is a relative A/B instrument and says nothing to an
outsider. The one other published V4.1-Flash recipe prints HumanEval pass@1 0.9634 (158/164,
greedy, lossy 3.0 bpw experts). What does the exact-weight hybrid score on the same test, and
what does the run's decode/TTFT distribution look like on 164 short-prompt requests?

**Hypotheses.** (1) ≥ 158/164 (exact experts should not score below a lossy requantization of
the same checkpoint; a lower score would point at a serving bug, not at the weights). (2)
Per-request decode median ≥ 38 tok/s, TTFT median < 1 s (prompts ≈ 200 tokens).

**Protocol.** `human-eval` problems through `/v1/chat/completions`, temperature 0, max_tokens
4096, one sample per problem, stop on the model's own stop; score offline by running each
problem's tests on the last fenced code block, inside a throwaway container with no network.
Record per-request tokens, TTFT, decode tok/s, finish reason; the six problems mlx-stream fails
(32, 83, 132, 140, 145, 163) are reported separately for the comparison. Script
`scripts/bench-humaneval.py` (to write; reuses `bench-decode.py`'s client). Evidence
`evidence/e32-humaneval/`.

**Go / stop.** Informational; no serving change depends on it. A score below 155 triggers a
correctness investigation before anything else.

## E33 — Context ladder 32K → 64K → 128K → 262K → 512K → 1M: qualify rung by rung, stop at the first failure (GPU; T3.6e–g)

**Status:** registered 2026-10-07 (owner's framing), **done 2026-10-07 — stopped at 128K.**
32K / 4 slots = the T3.19b gate serve ([`evidence/t319/b/`](../evidence/t319/b/README.md));
**64K / 1 slot passes every gate** (stress 61.9K prompt TTFT 84.3 s, needles 6/6 at 55.2K,
NLL 0 of 4,795 moved, C1 42.25 / 40.35 wall / 0.592 s, min free 807 / 732 MiB, 0 NaN / Xid,
EDAC 0/0); **128K / 1 slot and 64K / 4 slots fail gate (b)** with the same `torch.OutOfMemoryError`
in the DSV4 indexer's dense scoring (`_low_ratio_index_topk_dense` → `select_candidate_blocks`
→ `F.pad`): the indexer builds fp32 `[4096, prefix]` logits per prefill chunk (64 MiB per 4K of
prefix; 1 GiB at 64K, 2 GiB at 128K) plus a ≤ 1 GiB padded copy bounded by the upstream
`_TORCH_INDEXER_SCORE_BUDGET_BYTES = 1 << 30`, and the 226 / 158 placement leaves ≈ 4.0 GB after
capture. The KV pool itself is not the binder (≈ 0.1–0.2 GB per 65K tokens). Hypothesis (2) held
("indexer workspace that grows with context"); hypothesis (1) held (KV bytes do not bind).
262K / 512K / 1M were not launched: they fail the same gate before KV bytes matter. **Qualified
limit: 64K single slot; deployment profile stays 32K / 4 slots**, which itself runs ≈ 1 GB from
the same edge (E28 worst case 779 MiB). Candidate fixes are E34. Evidence
[`evidence/e33-context-ladder/`](../evidence/e33-context-ladder/README.md). Ran on the
qualified hybrid profile (226 / 158, D=4, spec off, capture 1 2 3 4, chunk 4096) after T3.19b
and before T3.19c; no overlay change (HEAD `9fc14817`).

**Question.** The hybrid is qualified at 32K with 4 slots. Where is its real context limit on
two cards, and what does each rung cost in prefill tok/s, TTFT and C1 decode? The published
mlx-stream recipe shows a 1K–1M ladder; ours should either match the shape or state the rung
at which it stops and why (T3.25).

**Hypotheses (projected, unverified).** (1) KV bytes alone do not bind: the T3.6g pool of
147,456 tokens (32K × 4 slots + 16K) is ≈ 0.6 GB/rank, so 1M × 1 slot is ≈ 4.1 GB/rank, under
the ≈ 6.2 GB the 226-expert placement leaves free before capture. (2) The first binder is more
likely one of: the token-pool / slot arithmetic (the pool is re-derived per rung from slots × R),
FlashInfer / indexer workspace that grows with context, chunked-prefill time at 4096 per chunk
(1M = 245 chunks, each paying the streamed-prefill CPU tier), or a placement trade (fewer GPU
experts to hold the pool, which costs C1). (3) At the largest passing rung, 4 slots will not fit
without reducing GPU experts; the single-slot rung is the honest "limit", the 4-slot rung the
honest "deployment".

**Protocol.** Per rung R ∈ {64K, 128K, 262K, 512K, 1M}, one serve with `CONTEXT_LENGTH=R
MAX_RUNNING_REQUESTS=1` (the launcher derives the pool as slots × R + 16K; do **not** clear
`MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS`, which hands the pool back to the memory fraction and re-creates the T3.6g
15× oversizing) (then, for the max passing rung only,
`MAX_RUNNING_REQUESTS=4`). Gates in order, same server: (a) launch and capture succeed on the
same expert placement (226 / 158) — if the pool does not fit, record the placement SGLang
demands and treat that as a cost, not a failure; (b) `stress-kv-full.py --concurrency <slots>
--prompt-tokens <R − 2K>` peak VRAM per GPU with no OOM; (c) `verify.py --context R` needles
6/6; (d) `nll-score.py --compare evidence/t316-review-intake/nll-t316c.json` **0 tokens moved**
(the 4K corpus must be bit-identical to 32K — a context change must not touch short-prompt
arithmetic); (e) 0 NaN / Xid / Traceback / `KTContract` / `Sealed` lines, EDAC 0/0; (f) record
prefill tok/s and TTFT at R − 2K from (c), and C1 `bench-decode.py` 512 tokens / 6 runs at the
standard short fixture for the decode row. Evidence `evidence/e33-context-ladder/<R>/` with the
redacted launch log and the `resolved:` line.

**Stop rules.** Stop the ladder at the first rung that fails (a)–(e); record the failing gate,
the message, and the last passing rung as the qualified limit in BENCHMARKS and the T3.6e–g row.
A rung that passes only with fewer GPU experts is recorded as "passes at N/M" with its C1 cost,
and the ladder continues from that placement. Do not retune D, chunk size or capture list inside
the ladder; a candidate fix for a failed rung is a separate experiment.

**Go / stop for the roadmap.** The ladder is informational for G1 (no code change rides on it);
its outputs are the `BENCHMARKS.md` context ladder table, the T3.6e–g closure (262K qualified or
the measured limit), and the TTFT / prefill rows the mlx-stream comparison (T3.25) is missing.

## E34 — Indexer score memory: make the DSV4 sparse-attention prefill transient independent of prefix length (GPU; T3.26)

**Status:** registered 2026-10-07 from the E33 failure. **Part a done 2026-10-07** (overlay
`1953a92e`, budget 256 MiB, two serves): **64K / 4 slots passes every gate** (4 × 62K stress all
finished, needles 6/6, NLL 0 moved, C1 42.20 / 40.26 wall, min free 641 / 582 MiB, health 0) and
**128K / 1 slot still fails gate (b)**, now at ≈ 110K prefix instead of ≈ 65K, on the `[4096, lc]`
bool mask of `scores.masked_fill_(j >= lens)` (448 MiB) over the 1.75 GiB logits. The pad copy is
no longer the binder; the logits and their mask are — exactly what part b removes. Hypothesis (a)
half held (64K / 4 slots yes, 128K / 1 slot no; margin arithmetic was ≈ 1 GB optimistic because
the broadcast mask and the other slots' working set were not counted). 256 MiB stays as the
overlay default (no measured cost); ~~64K / 4 slots is a qualified rung, not the deployment default~~
**64K × 4 slots is the deployment default since 2026-10-07** (owner decision: "if 64K × 4 is
qualified it becomes the new default"): the E27-shape served soak passed on top of the E33 gates —
same-prompt C4 consistency PASS (min prefix 310), 8 decode blocks (C1 41.9 / 39.9 wall, C2 50.5–52.1 /
47.9–49.7, C3 55.8–56.1 / 54.3–54.5, C4 57.8–59.0 / 55.5–57.4) all health 0 with the NaN probe on,
NLL 0 of 4,795 moved, 16K prefill 842 tok/s / 19.0 s, 4 × 62K stress all finished (295 s, peak
97,150 / 97,237 MiB, min free 737 / 650 MiB), needles 6/6 at 55.2K (TTFT 57.5 s), 0 Xid, EDAC 0/0.
`serve-hybrid.sh` default `CONTEXT_LENGTH` 32768 → 65536. Worst-case margin is ≈ 0.65 GB against
0.8–1.3 GB at 32K — thinner, documented, and the reason E34b stays next.
Evidence [`evidence/e34-indexer-memory/a/`](../evidence/e34-indexer-memory/a/README.md) and
`soak-64k-4slot/` there.
**Part b done 2026-10-08** (overlay `c639cbee`): `_low_ratio_index_topk_dense` now scores the
chunk's query rows in tiles of `_TORCH_INDEXER_SCORE_BUDGET_BYTES // (width × 4 B)` rows
(`_dense_index_topk_rows`), publishing / consuming the candidate masks per request segment inside
each tile. **Rows, not key blocks:** every kernel on the path (`fp8_fp4_mqa_logits`,
`select_candidate_blocks`, `topk_transform_ragged_v2`, `_mask_topk_scores`) is per query row, so
row tiling is bit-exact by construction with no running-top-k merge and no tie-break rule — the
registration's key-block design was not needed. GPU bit-equality test
`scripts/tests/test_indexer_row_tiling.py` (two requests, tiles straddling the boundary, 1-row
tiles, plain / source / consumer): 5/5; a mask-slicing mutant fails 3/5. Finding while testing:
the fused ragged top-k returns identical sets per row in a launch-shape-dependent order, which
the caller's ascending sort normalises. **128K / 1 slot passes every gate** (129K-token prompt
TTFT 154 s, needles 6/6 at 110,666 tokens, NLL 0 of 4,795 moved, C1 42.00 / 40.07 wall, min free
1,335 / 1,260 MiB, health 0, EDAC 0/0). **64K × 4 (deployment) re-measured:** min free 737 / 650 →
**1,537 / 1,478 MiB** (+0.8 GB, projected +0.9), **prefill 842 → 1,048 tok/s at 16K and 845 → 1,042 at
32K (+24 %)**, C1 42.14 / 40.20 wall (unchanged), NLL 0 moved, needles 6/6. Hypothesis (b) held
on memory and was wrong on cost in the good direction: no second pass exists (rows, not blocks),
and prefill is faster, projected to be the caching allocator no longer retrying 1 GiB + 256 MiB
allocations from a 3.4 GB pool (E34a's 128K traceback shows those retry sizes; untraced).
Evidence [`evidence/e34-indexer-memory/b/`](../evidence/e34-indexer-memory/b/README.md).
**Ladder resumed at 262K / 1 slot (2026-10-08): fail, gate (b), new binder.** CUDA OOM at
184K prefix (chunk 46 of 64) allocating 720 MiB for `torch.cat(parts)` of the candidate-source
layer's published mask — a `[chunk_rows, prefix]` bool per request (1 B × prefix per row: 1 GiB at
262K, 4 GiB at 1M) that the tiling builds in parts and copies once more at publish. 643 / 248 MiB
free; 0 NaN / Xid. The score matrix is no longer the binder; the candidate mask is. Per-chunk
prefill throughput was flat 929–963 tok/s from 40K to 184K prefix (the "prefill degrades with
prefix" reading in E33 was end-to-end TTFT, i.e. tokenization + scheduling gaps, not the indexer).
Part c is not needed.

**Part b-ii done 2026-10-08** (overlay `d6811728`): `select_candidate_block_keep` /
`expand_candidate_blocks` in `dsv4/indexer.py`; both prefill paths publish `[rows, ceil(lc / 8)]`
and each consumer tile expands only its own rows (asserting the block count matches its lc);
decode's `select_candidate_blocks` is the composition, unchanged. Test extended (partial last
block, block-level random masks, expansion vs index-gather reference, old == keep -> expand): 6/6;
a one-block-shift mutant fails 2/6 (the random-mask run catches it; the source-published run
cannot, since `topk_blocks` = 2048 keeps every block at 6K). **262K / 1 slot passes every gate:**
253,490-token prompt TTFT 285.9 s, **min free 1,807 / 1,734 MiB** (E34b: OOM at 184K), per-chunk
prefill 953-962 tok/s flat to 253K, needles **6/6 at 221,756 tokens** (TTFT 218 s), NLL 0 of
4,795 moved, C1 42.27 / 40.34 wall, prefill 974 / 946 tok/s at 16K / 32K, decode at 253K context
42.4 tok/s, health 0, EDAC 0/0. **64K x 4 re-measured: unchanged** (min free 1,541 / 1,484 MiB,
C1 40.23 wall, prefill 1,040 / 1,040 after re-running on an idle server - the first pair taken
right after the 4-stream stress read 965 / 946 and is kept as the confound), NLL 0 moved.
Hypothesis held. Next binder for 512K / 1M: not yet seen; projected the KV pool / attention
working set (compressed KV ~ 0.1-0.2 GB per 65K, so 512K / 1 slot is plausible on 1.7 GB spare,
1M is not at 226 / 158). Evidence
[`evidence/e34-indexer-memory/b-ii/`](../evidence/e34-indexer-memory/b-ii/README.md).
~~The ladder's next rung is 512K / 1 slot, after part d.~~ **512K / 1 slot done 2026-10-08 (two serves, `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/b-ii/512k/`): passes every gate at `MEM_FRACTION_STATIC=0.962`.** At the default 0.96 the KV-pool sizer caps the pool at 505,856 tokens (`available_bytes=1.00 GB` after weights minus the 4 % dynamic slack, 1,630 B per full token + 0.23 GB SWA) and the server returns HTTP 400 for inputs over 505,850 — a silent cap, not an OOM; at that ceiling a 502,649-token prompt passed (TTFT 561 s, min free 783 / 708 MiB, needles 6/6 at 425.6K, NLL 0 moved, C1 39.99 wall). 0.962 lets the launcher's 540,672 cap take over (+57 MB of pool, free after capture 3.20 / 3.12 GB): **522,237-token prompt TTFT 596 s, min free 713 / 638 MiB, needles 6/6 at 443,617, NLL 0 moved, C1 42.05 / 40.10 wall, prefill 943 / 943 idle, health 0, EDAC 0/0.** Two new measurements: prefill now slopes with prefix (988 tok/s at 150–200K → 896 past 500K, −9 %; the tiled indexer's work is still O(prefix)), and ≈ 3.5–4 KB/token of prefix-proportional memory grows outside the static pool (min free 1,807 → 783 → 713 MiB at 253K / 503K / 522K) — unidentified, registered as **E34e** (candidates: DSA indexer key cache outside the `full_token` pool, radix / block-table metadata). **1M projection (not run):** pool needs fraction ≈ 0.971 (−1.0 GB dynamic) while the long-prompt transient projects to ≈ 4.5 GB vs ≈ 2.2 GB available → ≈ 2.3 GB short ≈ 7 GPU experts (219 / 165) or bounding E34e. The ladder stops at the measured 512K rung; 1M is an owner decision, not a serve.
~~**Part b-ii (registered 2026-10-08, next):**~~ publish the candidate mask at block granularity.
`select_candidate_blocks` decides per `candidate_block_size` = 8 positions and
`repeat_interleave(8)`s the result, so the published `[rows, prefix]` bool is 8× redundant.
Publish `[rows, ceil(prefix / 8)]` and expand per tile at the consumers
(`_dense_index_topk_rows` consume branch, `_low_ratio_index_topk_torch` `consume[b][rows]`; the
late-layer-tail row slice and the decode path's `[bs, lmax]` tensor are unaffected or separate).
Bit-exact by construction; resident mask 128 MiB at 262K, 512 MiB at 1M, no publish-time
duplicate. Hypothesis: 262K / 1 slot passes gate (b) with ≥ 1 GB min free; 512K / 1M then meet
the next binder (projected: the consumers' per-tile expansion is ≤ tile-sized, so the candidate
is the KV pool itself or the attention working set). Test: extend
`test_indexer_row_tiling.py` so the published block mask expands to the E34b full mask bit-for-bit
and the consumer selection is unchanged. Gates and evidence as part b (`b-ii/<rung>/`).
Preallocating the full mask in place was considered and rejected: it removes only the duplicate
(720 MiB here) and leaves a 1 GiB resident mask at 262K.

~~**Part d (registered 2026-10-08, owner request, after b-ii; next):**~~ **Part d done 2026-10-08, negative (two serves, `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/d/`).** prefill chunk size. E22a measured
streamed prefill at 3.9× (4K chunks) and 6.5× (8K chunks) over CPU prefill but 16K chunks were
blocked by VRAM; E34b freed ≈ 0.8 GB at the deployment profile. One serve each at
`CHUNKED_PREFILL_SIZE=8192` then 16384 on 64K × 4: 16K / 32K prefill tok/s, C1 / C4 decode
(unchanged expected — chunk size is prefill-only), 4 × 62K stress min free (the cost: the
`[chunk, ·]` transients and the streamed expert staging scale with chunk), NLL 0 moved, needles.
Go bar: ≥ 10 % prefill gain at ≤ 0.5 GB of margin; the winner becomes the `serve-hybrid.sh`
default. Owner's framing: the box serves long coding / code-review sessions, so TTFT on the first
turn and on cache misses is what chunk size buys.
**Result:** `CHUNKED_PREFILL_SIZE=8192` at 226 / 158 (`ALLOW_UNTESTED_FIT=1` to bypass the launcher guard, which predates E34b) is ready with 3.25 / 3.19 GB free after capture (−0.23 GB vs 4096) but the first 16K prefill **OOMs on a 980 MiB FlashInfer CUTLASS fused-MoE workspace** (`FusedMoeRunner::getWorkspaceInfo` via `fused_experts_none_to_flashinfer_mxfp4`; 535 / 476 MiB free, 913 MiB reserved-but-fragmented). The workspace scales with chunk tokens — not the indexer, not KV. Retried with `PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF=expandable_segments:True` to defragment: the server dies at CUDA-graph capture in the custom all-reduce (`get_graph_buffer_ipc_meta` → `RuntimeError: invalid argument`; VMM segments cannot be CUDA-IPC exported), so expandable segments are incompatible with this stack while the custom all-reduce is on, and the NCCL fallback taxes every decode step. 16384 not run (strictly more). **4096 stays the default; the launcher guard was right.** Registered **E34d-ii** (owner decision, not run): the only way 8192 fits is a placement trade, ~ 7 fewer GPU experts (**219 / 165**, 0.34 GB each per E28) for a projected −3-4 % C1 wall and +30-60 % prefill (E22 measured +64 % at 208 / 176; discounted because the tiled indexer already removed part of what the larger chunk amortised). Changes the deployment default → needs owner go. **E34d-ii measured 2026-10-08** (one measurement serve + one NLL-only control, `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/d-ii/`): 219 / 165 + chunk 8192 at 64K × 4 — free after capture 5.56 / 5.51 GB, **prefill 1,438 / 1,526 tok/s at 16K / 32K (+38 % / +47 %)**, C1 41.39 / **39.45 wall (−1.9 %)**, C4 56.32 / 54.16 wall (−2.4 to −5.6 % vs the E34a soak range), 4 × 62K stress min free 2,339 / 1,302 MiB in 175.5 s (vs 290.6 s), needles 6/6, health 0, EDAC 0/0 — **but NLL fails the gate: +0.00674 nats/tok (z −1.89; 146 worse / 121 better)**. Control serve at the same 219 / 165 placement with chunk 4096: **+0.00134 (z −0.35)** — the placement passes, the chunk does not. Paired per-token comparison of the two 219 serves: the first 4,096 tokens (one prefill batch in *both* modes, only the row count differs: 4,096 vs 4,795) are +0.0080 at z +2.22, median Δlogp 0, no 512-token pattern; the 699-token tail (CPU kt path at 4096, streamed at 8192) is −0.0098 at z −1.06. **Chunk 8192 had never been NLL-scored**: E22e's "NLL-neutral" came from the chunk-4096 serve (`e22-layerwise-prefill/nll-stream.json`), and every NLL run in the recipe used chunk 4096. **Not adopted**; default stays 226 / 158 + chunk 4096. Registered **E34d-iii** (chunk-8192 NLL shift diagnosis; owner decision on when): (1) a second chunk-8192 NLL on a fresh serve (one-serve sample so far); (2) `nll-score.py --max-chars` to score a ≤ 4,096-token prefix on a chunk-8192 serve vs a chunk-4096 control — common-prefix Δ ≈ 0 at M ≤ 4,096 and back at M = 4,795 would make it row-count-specific (streamed-prefill buffers / fused-MoE tactic at M > 4096, a latent overlay bug the deployed config never exercises); (3) if the shift survives, bisect `KT_GPU_PREFILL_MIN_TOKENS` (CPU path for the whole chunk) to separate the streamed experts from attention / indexer. Feeds T3.16e (correctness debt) and the T3.19 shape assertions. **E34d-iii done 2026-10-08** (one serve, 21 NLL runs, `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/d-iii/`): the replicate is **bit-identical** to d-ii (deterministic); a `--max-chars` sweep shows tokens 0–2,999 are bit-identical for every M from 4,460 to 4,733 (Δ +0.0005 vs the chunk-4096 control) and bit-identical, at +0.0081, for every M from **4,737** to 4,796 — a step at 4,736 = 37 × 128 rows in some batch-wide kernel schedule (GEMM / fused-MoE tile or split-K; unidentified), **not** an M > 4096 path (the SM120 paged-indexer 4,096-row chunking is one but cannot touch rows 0–2,999). Two more texts scored just under and just over the step on the same serve: **−0.0029 (z −1.0) and +0.0053 (z +1.7)**; pooled with the fixture, +0.0031 ± 0.0017 over 14,087 tokens. Verdict: **the +0.0067 is a 2σ draw from M-regime-dependent numerics, not a defect; chunk 8192 is neither quality-rejected nor quality-proven.** Mechanism: block-scaled MXFP8 / MXFP4 activations turn any accumulation-order change into ~0.1 nats of per-token jitter (same as streamed-vs-CPU 0.125, D=4 reruns 0.13), zero-mean to first order, so one 4.8K-token fixture resolves the mean only to ±0.0035 — **the +0.002 gate is below the fixture's resolution for any config that changes a kernel's M** and is a valid "0 moved" identity check only for same-shape configs. Registered **T3.28** (shape-aware NLL gate: pooled multi-text corpus ≥ 15K tokens with standard error for shape-changing configs; "0 moved" for same-shape). E34d-ii's adoption question reopens on that gate (owner decision). **T3.28 done 2026-10-08 (five serves, `evidence/t328/`): on the 65,653-token pooled corpus 219 / 165 + chunk 8192 reads +0.00075 ± 0.00098 nats/token vs 226 / 158 + 4096 (cluster-robust se 0.00165; per-text −0.0069 … +0.0067) → passes ≤ +0.002; adopted as the `serve-hybrid.sh` default (plain-default serve bit-identical to the candidate; prefill 1,504 tok/s at 16K, C1 41.34 / 39.39 wall, needles 6/6). Re-scores on the corpus: streamed vs CPU-path prefill −0.00025 ± 0.00104 (E22 neutral); D=0 vs D=4 at the default is bit-identical because every ≥1,024-token chunk is streamed and deferral lives only in kt-kernel's CPU `submit_forward` — the gate is blind to D unless `KT_GPU_PREFILL_MIN_TOKENS=0`; on that path +0.00066 ± 0.00103 nats/tok for D=4 over D=0 (z 0.64; cluster-robust se 0.00225, z 0.29; per-text −0.0116 … +0.0074; 65,217 tokens changed) — within the gate: deferral costs ≤ +0.005 at 95 % (cluster-robust). Ladder rungs above 64K stay on 226 / 4096 (launcher guard) until T3.29.** **E34d-iv registered 2026-10-08 (owner: 8192 was never meant as a ceiling; find the optimum, higher or lower).** Chunk sweep on the new default: 12288 and 16384 at 219 / 165 (`ALLOW_UNTESTED_FIT=1`), then one more doubling only if 16384 fits and still gains; if a size OOMs on the fused-MoE workspace (980 MiB at 8192, scales with chunk tokens), retry it once with the fewest experts that fit (E28: 0.34 GB per expert per rank) and charge the decode cost. Per size: free after capture, 16K / 32K prefill + TTFT, C1 wall, 4 × 62K stress min free, pooled-corpus NLL vs `evidence/t328/default-219-8192/corpus/` (shape-changing → ≤ +0.002 pooled, both se reported), needles 6/6, health 0. Winner = best 16K-prefill gain per 1 % of C1 wall given up, with the stress margin ≥ 1 GB on both ranks; projected (not measured) +10–20 % prefill for ~2 % decode at 16384, and nothing below 8192 (chunk 2048 was rejected, 4096 is the measured floor). Runs next, before T3.19c; one serve per size, ~25 min each. **E34d-iv done 2026-10-08 (three serves, `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/d-iv/`): chunk 16384 OOMs at 219 / 165 (workspace 1.86 GiB vs 631 MiB free) and at 212 / 172 (1.87 GiB vs 1.44 GiB free — the other transients take ~6 GB first; 16K-prefill peak ~8 GB). At 205 / 179 it fits (9.9 GB free): prefill 1,838 / 1,701 tok/s at 16K / 32K (+22 % / +11 %), C1 39.56 / 37.66 wall (−4.4 %). Ratio 5 vs 8192's 45 → **8192 stays**; 12288 not run (projected same ratio, < 1 GB margin at 212). Registered **E34d-v** (conditional): make the streamed-prefill transient sub-linear in the chunk — row-tile the `cutlass_fused_moe` call (workspace is per-call, 0.98 GiB per 8192 rows) and identify the remaining ~3 GB of chunk-proportional transient at 16384 from a prefill timeline (the T3.19a instrumentation covers decode only); go only if a tiled 16384 chunk keeps ≥ +15 % at 16K with the fused-MoE tiles NLL-gated on the pooled corpus (row tiling changes each expert's GEMM M, so not bit-exact). Entry: after T3.21 (the large-M prefill GEMM path is being rewritten there anyway); stop if the prefill timeline shows the 16384 gain is in the GEMMs themselves rather than per-chunk fixed cost, because then T3.21 collects it directly.
~~**Part b next.**~~ Three parts, each its own serve, in order a → b → c; stop after the first part
that qualifies the target rung.

**Question.** E33 stops at 128K / 1 slot and 64K / 4 slots because the DSV4 indexer scores each
4096-token prefill chunk against the whole prefix in fp32 (`[chunk, lc]`, 64 MiB per 4K of prefix)
and then pads a copy of it (≤ 1 GiB, `_TORCH_INDEXER_SCORE_BUDGET_BYTES`). Which of a budget
constant, a tiled scorer, or a placement trade buys 128K / 1 slot and 64K / 4 slots at the least
prefill and decode cost, and does the fix also give the 32K / 4-slot deployment profile back the
≈ 1 GB of margin it is missing?

**Hypotheses (projected).** (a) Lowering the budget constant to 256 MiB bounds only the padded
copy, so it recovers ≈ 0.75 GB and is enough for 64K / 4 slots and probably 128K / 1 slot, but
cannot reach 262K (the 4 GiB logits remain). (b) Tiling the scoring over key blocks with a running
top-k makes the transient O(chunk × block) and removes the context dependence entirely; cost is a
second pass over the scores, expected ≤ 5 % of prefill at 32K. (c) Fewer GPU experts (0.34 GB per
expert per rank, E28; 262K needs ≈ 12 fewer, 1M ≈ 47 fewer) pays the E28 byte model in C1/C4
and is the fallback only.

**Protocol.** Part a: overlay one-line change, serve at 64K / 4 slots then 128K / 1 slot, E33
gates (a)–(f), plus the 32K / 4-slot worst case (4 × 30K `stress-kv-full.py`) for the margin
reading. Part b: overlay change to `_low_ratio_index_topk_dense` / `select_candidate_blocks` with a
CPU-side test that the tiled top-k equals the dense top-k on random `[chunk, lc]` inputs and on
the E33 64K prefix shapes (bit-equal block masks), then the same serves; also the ladder from
128K upward (262K / 1 slot, then 4 slots, then 512K) until a new gate binds. Part c only if a and
b fail or regress prefill > 10 %. Evidence `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/<part>/<rung>/`.

**Stop rules.** Any gate (c)–(e) failure stops the part (a mask mismatch in the tiled top-k is a
correctness bug, not a tuning question). A > 10 % prefill regression at 32K rejects that part.
NLL must stay 0 moved on the 4K corpus for every part (the indexer change must not touch
short-prompt arithmetic; at 4K the dense and tiled paths must select the same blocks).

**Go / stop for the roadmap.** Part b is the upstream-PR candidate (OPEN-SOURCE-PLAN §3): the
dense scorer is upstream code and the memory bound is a model-independent transient at any
context ≥ 64K on a 96 GB card. Rides as T3.26 after T3.19c (runner-owned state), because the
scorer lives in the attention backend, not the CPU-expert seam, and does not depend on it.

**Part d-v — chunk-independent prefill transients (registered 2026-10-08 in the E34d-iv change log, protocol written 2026-10-08 after T3.29; behind T3.21, conditional).** *Question.* The `cutlass_fused_moe` workspace that FlashInfer sizes from the chunk's token count (980 MiB at 8192, 1.86 GiB at 16384; `FusedMoeRunner::getWorkspaceInfo` via `fused_experts_none_to_flashinfer_mxfp4`) and the other `[chunk, …]` transients together peak at ~3.2 GB at chunk 8192 and ~8 GB at 16384 (E34d, E34d-iv). Which of them dominates, and can the peak be made independent of the chunk so that 16384's +22 % 16K prefill (E34d-iv at 205 / 179) and 262K × 4 (T3.29, OOM at 3.88 GB after capture) become reachable without giving back experts? *Hypotheses (projected).* (a) Allocate the fused-MoE workspace once at the largest chunk and hand it to every call (FlashInfer accepts a caller workspace in its newer `cutlass_fused_moe` signature — to verify against the pinned version) — removes the per-call 1 GiB request but not the other transients. (b) Row-tile the fused-MoE call over the chunk in fixed 2048- or 4096-row tiles, the move E34b made for the indexer; the MoE kernel is per row so the tiling is bit-exact, and the workspace shrinks to the tile's size; costs one extra launch per tile. (c) `expandable_segments` — already ruled out (E34d: VMM segments cannot be CUDA-IPC exported by the custom all-reduce at capture). *Protocol.* First a prefill memory timeline: T3.19a's recorder extended to log `torch.cuda.memory_allocated()` / `memory_reserved()` before and after each prefill phase (indexer, attention, fused-MoE, CPU-tier export) on one 16K prompt at chunk 8192 and 16384 — this names the dominant transient and is the entry gate. Then (a) or (b) as one overlay change, GPU test that the tiled output is bit-equal to the untiled one on random inputs at both ends of the row bucket, and the E34d gates: 16K / 32K prefill tok/s, NLL 0 moved on the T3.28 corpus (same shape) or ≤ +0.002 pooled (shape change), min free during the 64K × 4 stress. *Go.* Peak prefill transient ≤ 2 GB at chunk 16384 (from ~8), 16K prefill ≥ E34d-iv's 1,838 tok/s at 219 / 165 with C1 wall unchanged; then re-run the T3.29 262K × 4 rung. *Stop.* The timeline shows the workspace is < 30 % of the peak and the rest is attention/indexer (then this is a T3.26 follow-up, not a MoE change); or the tiled kernel is slower than the untiled by > 5 % at 16K. Evidence `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/d-v/`. **Owner decision 2026-10-09 for 262K × 4: (a) this Part's transient removal first; (b) chunk 4096 for that rung and (c) 212 / 172 are registered fallbacks, not defaults.** *Note under (a) from the RonanLabsAI sidecar assessment (T3.32 follow-up, owner-approved 2026-10-09):* if the timeline names the kt CPU-tier export staging among the unnamed 320 / 400 MiB requests, compact it to tokens with ≥ 1 CPU route (not chunk × top-k) and fuse route-split + quantize/pack and wait + scatter-add — their v2 protocol shows runtime row counts stay graph-safe behind fixed maxima; keep D=4 overlap, do not copy their timeout-drops-contribution behaviour. *Progress under (a) 2026-10-09 (d-v (ii)):* of the four transients the d-v (i) 262K × 4 run named by size (320 / 400 / 640 / 234 MiB), the 640 (`hc_combine` fp32 input) and the 234 (kt scale conversion) are gone — ≈ 0.87 GiB of the ≈ 3 GB that run needed against 2.9 GB after bs-4 capture; the 64K × 4 stress margin moved +0.67 GB. 320 / 400 still unnamed. Next datapoint: the 262K × 4 retry on `1eeb48cd`; if it OOMs, extend the timeline to name the two, with `memory_stats()['num_alloc_retries']` logged per chunk (it also decides whether the +25 % 16K prefill on 64K × 4 is allocator relief).

**Part e — the prefix-dependent VRAM growth outside the static pool (registered 2026-10-08 from the 512K rung; protocol written 2026-10-08 after T3.29; owner of the 1M question).** *Question.* On every rung above 262K some allocation grows with the prefix outside the KV pool: min free 1,807 → 783 → 713 MiB at 253K / 503K / 522K prefix at 226 / 4096 (≈ 3.5 KB/token on a two-point slope), and on T3.29's 512K × 1 run free VRAM is flat from ~251K to ~440K of prefix and then falls again — a staircase, not a line. What is it, and does removing it put 1M × 1 within the 5.3 GB left after capture at the 512K pool size (1M at 226 / 158 projects ≈ 2.3 GB short; at 219 / 165 the gap is ≈ 1.5 GB by the E28 byte model)? *Hypotheses (projected).* (a) The block-granular candidate mask `[rows, ceil(prefix / 8)]` (E34b-ii) and its per-consumer expansions still scale with the prefix; at 1M and 8192 rows that is 1 GiB of bool per published mask. (b) The indexer's per-tile score buffers are cached by the allocator at the largest tile seen, so reserved memory steps up each time a tile grows past a previous maximum (the staircase). (c) Request-level metadata SGLang keeps per token (logprob slices, `req_to_token` rows, DSA block tables) sized from the running prefix. *Protocol.* One 512K × 1 serve with `stress-kv-full.py` sampling free VRAM every ~16K of prefix (the `vram_samples` in `evidence/t329/512k-1slot/stress-kv.json` are the first such trace; resample at a fixed interval) plus `torch.cuda.memory_snapshot()` dumped at 128K, 256K, 384K and 500K prefix via a debug endpoint or signal; diff the snapshots by allocation site. The site that grows is the answer; the fix follows its owner (a → T3.26 mask representation, b → cap the tile at a fixed size, c → upstream SGLang). *Go.* Growth identified and bounded to ≤ 256 MiB from 128K to 512K; then the ladder reopens at 1M × 1 on the T3.29 gates with `MEM_FRACTION_STATIC=0.962` and the 219 / 8192 default (expected to need no expert change). *Stop.* The growth is in the KV pool's own bookkeeping (then 1M is a placement decision, as recorded) or the fix regresses prefill > 5 % at 32K. Evidence `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/e/`. **Result 2026-10-09 (via e-ii, one 512K × 1 serve, `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/e-ii/`): GO on the letter — 128K → 508K reserved +174 MiB, allocated +189 MiB; the staircase is the first large prefill's transient high-water (+2.6 GiB at 128K, 99 % `inactive` cached blocks, largest the 980 MiB fused-MoE workspace) and the only prefix-scaling live tensor is hypothesis (a): the published candidate mask `[last-chunk rows, ceil(prefix / 8)]` bool at `deepseek_v4_backend.py:3392` (274.9 MiB at 384K, worst case 1.0 GiB at 1M), retained on the backend between requests. (b) and (c) falsified (inactive stable from 128K on; every other site ≤ 4 MiB). The 1M × 1 rung reopens but is projected ~0.5–1.0 GB short at 219 / 8192 / 0.962: its 1,064,960-token pool leaves ≈ 4.1 GB after capture and the prefill high-water is ≈ 4.2 GB at 508K plus up to 0.5 GiB more mask at 1M — the binder is the constant transient peak, not the growth. Served after e-i (packed mask) lands, or at 212 / 172 for that rung by owner decision.**

**Part e-i — bit-packed candidate mask / block-ID lists (registered 2026-10-08 from T3.31 idea 1; rides with T3.26, behind Part e's trace).** *Question.* Does the DSA candidate representation drive the Part e growth? Verified on this box 2026-10-08: `select_candidate_block_keep` (`indexer.py:1132`) returns a `torch.bool` mask (one byte per block) and a second helper expands it to a *per-position* `[rows, width]` bool mask — the opposite of packed. *Protocol.* Behind a flag, two representations: packed `uint32/uint64` bits consumed directly by candidate selection, and compact block-ID lists when selected-block density makes IDs smaller than a bitset; no persistent unpacked per-position copy (a temporary unpacked tile is allowed only if bounded independently of prefix length). Count live copies with the Part e snapshot first. *Projected* (no run): at 8192 rows × 1M prefix the byte-per-block mask is ~0.95 GiB per live copy, packed ~0.12 GiB, so ~0.83 GiB per copy; two or three concurrent copies would cover the projected 1M × 1 shortfall. *Go.* Min free VRAM during a context-minus-2K prompt at 512K and 1M rises by ≥ 0.5 GiB; 0 NLL movement on the 4K corpus; 16K/32K prefill within 5 %; 0 NaN/Xid. *Stop.* Only one bounded copy exists (then Part e's growth is elsewhere), or the consumer immediately expands the packed form to an equally large prefix-dependent tensor. Evidence `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/e-i/`. Source: [BINDER-RESEARCH-2026-10 §1](../docs/research/BINDER-RESEARCH-2026-10.md). **Done 2026-10-09 (overlay `b6dc5173`, one 512K × 1 serve at 0.962, `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/e-i/`):** block-ID lists, not a packed bitset — `select_candidate_block_ids` / `expand_candidate_ids` publish `[rows, min(2048, blocks)]` int32, -1 padded (8 KiB per row whatever the prefix; smaller than a bitset above 512K, never larger than the byte mask, bit-exact by construction as the keep mask's own `topk` output); both prefill paths swapped, decode untouched; GPU test 7/7 with a new `test_block_ids` (keep-path equality at five lc × two topk, -1 census, the clamp-to-0 scatter race as the mutant: 3/7 fail). **The tensor is gone (52 → 24 MiB at 384K → 508K, was 275 → 200; allocated −205 / −166 MiB), quality and speed unchanged (NLL 0 of 65,653 moved, prefill 1,627 / 1,660 tok/s −1.4 / −0.6 %, C1 41.48 / 39.57 wall, needles 6/6), long-prompt TTFT −10 % (508K stress 369 → 333 s, needles 267 → 238 s), health 0, EDAC 0/0 — but min free VRAM at 508K is unchanged at 1,091 / 1,018 MiB: the Go gate (+0.5 GiB) is not met.** The mask always fit inside the 4.7–4.9 GiB of inactive blocks the first large prefill's transients had reserved, so it never set the 512K peak (the stop rule's "growth is elsewhere", landing after a free fix); the peak is the chunk-proportional transient set, owner E34d-v (i). What e-i buys is 1M: up to 1.0 GiB live + 1.0 GiB `torch.cat` transient at the first full-width chunk no longer exists, so the 1M × 1 prefill high-water should equal 512K's (≈ 4.2 GB) against ≈ 4.1 GB after capture — a coin flip (~0.1 GB), not e-ii's 0.5–1.0 GB shortfall; one serve, harmless OOM as the failure mode.

**Part e-ii — allocation trace and fixed DSA arena (registered 2026-10-08 from T3.31 ideas 3 and 4; this is Part e's protocol made concrete).** *Question.* Is the Part e staircase allocator high-water retention from grow-on-demand tensors (SGLang's `SparsePrefillWorkspace` replaces its cached tensor when a larger request arrives, briefly holding old plus new; top-k state, indexer scores, gather indices and candidate publication follow the same pattern) or genuine persistent per-token state? Also audit any tensor whose shape multiplies query rows by prefix blocks (vLLM's equivalent is a `[max_batched_tokens, max_model_len/block] int32` table, ~488 MiB per rank at 8192 rows × 1M / 64). *Protocol.* Trace every allocation at those sites (requested and capacity bytes, pointer, stream, layer, prefix and query rows, whether a superseded generation is still live) across the 512K × 1 serve of Part e; then replace grow-on-demand tensors with one startup-profiled arena partitioned into reusable buffers, tiling work when a request exceeds its partition instead of reallocating. *Projected*: 0.5–1.5 GiB if the staircase is retention. *Go.* Free-VRAM trace flat after startup from 250K to 1M (not just end-state), 0 NLL movement, 16K/32K prefill within 5 %, graph replay, 0 NaN/Xid. *Stop (falsified).* `memory_allocated` grows in lockstep with `memory_reserved` with no superseded buffers — the growth is real state and the fix is a-i or upstream. Evidence `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/e-ii/`. Source: [BINDER-RESEARCH-2026-10 §3–4](../docs/research/BINDER-RESEARCH-2026-10.md). **Done 2026-10-09 (trace stage only; `scripts/trace-prefix-memory.py` over SGLang's `/start_profile` MEM activity, no overlay change):** neither Go nor Stop as written — `memory_allocated` is flat (+189 MiB over 128K → 508K) while `memory_reserved` steps once (+2.6 GiB at the first large prefill) and then stays flat (+174 MiB), so the staircase is allocator caching of reusable transients, not superseded generations held live. ~~replace grow-on-demand tensors with one startup-profiled arena~~ **arena stage struck 2026-10-09:** nothing is retained twice, the cached blocks are reused by every later prefill, and an arena would hold the same bytes; the peak is owned by E34d-v (i) (workspace) and e-i (mask). Result table in Part e.

**Part d-v (i) — caller-owned fused-MoE workspace with an independent row tile (registered 2026-10-08 from T3.31 idea 2; the first concrete Part d-v protocol).** *Finding that makes it cheap.* The pinned image's FlashInfer is 0.6.18 and its `cutlass_fused_moe` already accepts `workspace_buffer=` (with `cutlass_fused_moe_workspace_size`); the overlay's `moe_runner/flashinfer_cutlass.py` never passes it (verified 2026-10-08, no `workspace` in the file), so each chunk's call allocates its own 980 MiB / 1.86 GiB workspace from the caching allocator. No backport. *Protocol.* In `fused_experts_none_to_flashinfer_mxfp4`: an independent MoE row tile (first 8192, then 4096), one persistent 1-D byte tensor per device sized by `cutlass_fused_moe_workspace_size` for that tile and allocated before graph capture, every row-indexed input sliced consistently and each result written into its output slice, growth after capture raised as an error; local standard-dispatch path only, never aliasing the A2A workspace. *Projected*: an 8192 tile caps the 16384-chunk workspace at ~980 MiB instead of 1.86 GiB (−0.88 GiB) and removes the late allocator growth that killed T3.29's 262K × 4 at ~190K prefix; it may keep most of E34d-iv's +22 % 16K prefill at chunk 16384. It does not touch the other transients that take the chunk-16384 peak from 3.2 to 8 GiB. *Go.* 16K/32K prefill at chunk 16384 ≥ chunk 8192's; 0 NLL movement; min free VRAM at context-minus-2K ≥ the chunk-8192 figure; decode within noise; capture/replay at every graph shape; 0 NaN/Xid. *Stop.* Routing, expert sorting, scale generation or fused finalisation has cross-row state that changes results across tile boundaries, or the non-MoE transients still force the same expert give-back. Evidence `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/d-v-i/`. Source: [BINDER-RESEARCH-2026-10 §2](../docs/research/BINDER-RESEARCH-2026-10.md). *Ordering.* Stays behind T3.21 as registered, but now needs only one overlay call-site change plus a tile loop, so it may run in T3.21's second window if the cuTile bench stalls. **Done 2026-10-09 (overlay `2323052b`, three serves, `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/d-v-i/`; pulled ahead of T3.20 after the 1M × 1 fail):** ~~independent row tile~~ **no tiling needed** — the pinned 0.6.18 binding documents `cutlass_fused_moe_workspace_size` as monotonic in rows and validates the caller's buffer (`size >= need`, 128-byte aligned; short = Python `RuntimeError`), so one persistent per-device buffer sized for the chunk ceiling (979.6 MiB at 8192 rows / 219 experts / top_k 6; independent of `inter_size`) serves every call. Allocated at the first MXFP4 call (FlashInfer autotune, before capture), never resized; rows above the ceiling or a second non-fitting shape fall back to per-call allocation with one warning; kill switch `SGLANG_FLASHINFER_MOE_PERSISTENT_WORKSPACE`, override `..._WORKSPACE_MAX_ROWS`. GPU test `scripts/tests/test_moe_workspace.py` 4/4 (size monotonic; binding agreement incl. 128-byte-short rejection; bit-equal with/without buffer at rows 1–2048 plus overflow-with-warning; capture guard); mutant top_k = 1 fails on "workspace_buffer too small". **64K × 4: every gate met** (NLL 0 of 65,653 moved, prefill 1,512 / 1,580, C1 41.50 / 39.59 wall, stress min free 1,119 / 1,062 MiB, needles 6/6, health 0, EDAC 0/0); capture-end avail reads 0.95 GB lower on every profile (the buffer is resident at rest instead of cached after the first prefill — same bytes, paid earlier). **1M × 1 retry: the fragmentation binder is gone** — prefill ran to ≈ 680K prefix (was ~306K) at 1,500 → 1,150 tok/s per chunk, then OOMed on a 332 MiB **int64 index tensor** in `get_index_k_fp4` (`[n, 64]` × 8 B = 512 B per prefix token, plus `[n, 4]` for scales, to gather 68 B per token of keys; ≈ 0.6 GiB of transient per low-ratio layer at 1M), 1.20 GiB still reserved-but-unallocated. That is T3.31 idea 9's gather workspace, now evidenced → **Part d-v (ii)** below. **262K × 4 retry: fails on the first 8192-token chunk** (2.92 / 2.86 GB after bs-4 capture; the allocator retried 320 / 400 / 640 MiB and failed a 234 MiB int32 temporary in `kt_prefill.load`, 818 MiB reserved-but-unallocated): with the workspace resident, the remaining chunk-8192 transient set is ≈ 3 GB against 2.9 — the rung needs Part d-v's timeline (name and shrink the 320 / 400 / 640 MiB transients), chunk 4096 for the rung, or 212 / 172 (owner's named fallback). *Go* met on the letter (prefill ≥ chunk-8192 figures, NLL 0, min free ≥ the 64K × 4 gate, decode within noise, capture at every shape, health 0); *Stop* not triggered (no cross-row state: no tiling was needed at all). Not re-run: 128K–512K × 1 (0.95 GB tighter at rest, prefill peaks unchanged; the 512K rung is re-qualified on the next ladder pass, and should be before any default change).
