**Part d-v (ii) — gather the index-K keys without a prefix-sized index tensor (registered 2026-10-09 from the d-v (i) 1M retry; T3.31 idea 9 reinstated on evidence).** *Question.* `DeepSeekV4IndexerPool.get_index_k_fp4` builds `payload_cols = (off * 64).unsqueeze(-1) + arange(64)` (`[n, 64]` int64) and `scale_cols` (`[n, 4]` int64) to advanced-index the page buffer: 544 B of index per prefix token to read 68 B of keys, per low-ratio layer per chunk — 332 MiB at ≈ 680K (the 1M OOM) and ≈ 0.55 GiB at 1M, on top of the gathered rows. Does indexing a `[pages, page_size, 64]` / `[pages, page_size, 4]` view of the same buffer with two `[n]` index tensors (`page`, `off`; 16 B per token) read the same bytes, and does 1M × 1 then prefill to the end? *Protocol.* One function in `deepseek_v4_memory_pool.py`, `get_index_k_dequant` likewise if it shares the pattern; GPU test in `test_indexer_row_tiling.py` or a sibling: payload and scales bit-equal to the current gather at random slot sets spanning page boundaries (page 0, last page, unsorted, repeated slots) and n ∈ {1, 255, 256, 257, 100,000}; mutant = off-by-one in the page split. Evidence `evidence/e34-indexer-memory/d-v-ii/`. **Done 2026-10-09 (overlay `1eeb48cd` = three bit-exact commits, four serves, ~2 h): Go met — 1M × 1 qualified.** The Stop clause fired twice and was answered in place, because each further site was chunk-proportional and one function: (1) `e5f60f54` gather via `[pages, p, 64]` / `[pages, p, 4]` views with `[n]` page / offset indices (16 B/slot vs 544) — 1M then OOMed at ≈ 298K on (2) a 640 MiB fp32 copy of `pre_hc_head` per chunk (`f18dc961`: pass bf16, the kernel casts on load) — stress passed (TTFT 961 s) and the needle run OOMed at ≈ 860K on (3) a 234 MiB int32 temporary in `kt_prefill.py`'s e8m0 scale conversion, twice per streamed layer per chunk (`1eeb48cd`: in-place `int16 >>= 7` + narrowing `copy_`). T3.31 idea 8 not needed. 1M × 1 is a per-rung override (`CONTEXT_LENGTH=1048576 MAX_RUNNING_REQUESTS=1 MEM_FRACTION_STATIC=0.962`), not a default.

## E35 — DSV4.1 SWA bounded replay: does the overlay's dormant decoder / encoder replay path buy prefill on the hybrid, and at what graph cost? (GPU; T3.33)

**Status:** registered 2026-10-08 from T3.32 ([TENSORFOLD-ASSESSMENT §4](../docs/research/TENSORFOLD-ASSESSMENT-2026-10.md)). The community DeepSeek recipes on TensorFold report "CED bounded replay" roughly halving decoder-side prompt work (jayleaton: full prefill 1,561 → replay 2,028 tok/s at 8K on 2 × GB10 — **inferred**, different engine and quantisation). Our overlay already has the mechanism as two unlaunched flags (`server_args.py:3639–3649`; validation in `arg_groups/deepseek_v4_hook.py:232–347`).

*Question.* On the launcher default (219 / 165, chunk 8192, 64K × 4), does `--enable-decoder-swa-bounded-replay` raise 16K / 32K prefill once the prefill CUDA graph it refuses is accounted for, with NLL bit-identical and the 64K × 4 memory profile intact? Secondary: does the encoder variant (prefix-hit replay) help the multi-turn code-review shape where the prefix cache hits?

*Protocol.* Three serves, each on the default manifest: (A) control — prefill graph disabled only (`--cuda-graph-backend-prefill disabled`), so the graph-off cost is measured on its own; (B) decoder replay + prefill graph disabled; (C) encoder replay on the default graph config, explicit `--max-running-requests 4`. Per serve: `bench-prefill.py` 16K / 32K `--runs 3`, `bench-decode.py --distinct-prompts` C1 and C4, `verify.py` needles at 64K, `stress-kv-full.py` 4 × 62K with min-free sampling, the T3.28 65K-corpus NLL, docker log grep for NaN / Xid. For (C) add a warm-pass prefill (same seeds, radix hit) since that is the path it changes.

*Gates.* NLL 0 moved; needles 6/6; min free ≥ E34a's 641 / 582 MiB at 4 × 62K; C1 wall ≥ 39.0 (T3.19 band); 0 NaN / Xid. *Go.* (B) − (A) ≥ +25 % prefill at 16K **and** (B) ≥ the current default's 1,504 tok/s (T3.28) — i.e. the win survives losing the prefill graph. *Stop.* NLL moves (the flag's "deterministic for a fixed prompt and chunk size" is not "identical to no-replay" — the gate decides), or (B) < default, or 64K × 4 loses min-free, or the decoder path breaks the breakable-decode-graph Engram breaks. If (B) passes, re-run T3.29's 262K × 1 and 512K × 1 rungs before it becomes the default.

Evidence `evidence/e35-bounded-replay/`. Slot: ~~proposed directly after G1 exit (flag flip, ~45 min); owner to confirm~~ run 2026-10-08/09 after G1 exit.

**Result (2026-10-09, four serves, `evidence/e35-bounded-replay/README.md`): STOP on quality; the prefill claim is real.** (A) control = launcher default (the prefill graph is auto-disabled for DSV4 already; A's corpus NLL 0 of 65,653 moved vs T3.28). (B) decoder replay: **16K 1,514 → 2,106 tok/s cold (+39 %), TTFT 10.6 → 7.6 s; 32K 1,620 → 2,175 (+34 %)**; C1 41.46 / 39.55 wall, C4 56.15 / 54.04, needles 6/6 at 55,206 tokens (TTFT 30.8 → 22.6 s), 4 × 62K stress min free 941 / 880 MiB, health 0, EDAC 0/0. Quality, paired teacher-forced next-token Δlogp vs A over 2,558 positions (the corpus gate cannot run: the flag refuses prompt logprobs and the refusal kills the server): **fresh prompts +1.13 nats/tok (se 0.36; greedy token differs at 34 / 158; code texts +2.3…+2.8), extends +0.055 (se 0.008; 1–8-token extends +0.29, 129–256 +0.02)** — deterministic across the two B serves to the fourth decimal. Mechanism, not noise: `late_layer_tail_layout` floors every tail row's SWA window at the tail start because the late layers never write window KV for the rows before it, so the first tail row attends to itself only and the last row inherits truncated-window states through 19 layers; the ~2× speed-up of 19 / 40 layers is the same cut. Three protocol corrections: the "flag flip" needed a +74 / −5 overlay patch (`PrefillPipeline.abandon` / `LayerwisePrefillCoordinator.abandon_forward`: a forward whose token count shrinks at a CPU-path layer closes early instead of raising — `B-crash/`), archived in `patches/` and reverted; the graph-off cost is zero; the encoder variant (C) was not served — same floored replay on every prefix hit, and the SWA pool it would free is already capped at 0.56 GB/GPU. Overlay stays `bc73ef8d`, image unchanged, no registry knob.

## E35-ii — exact bounded replay: tail = the late layers' receptive field (GPU; candidate, not scheduled)

**Status:** registered 2026-10-09 from E35's STOP. The E35 speed-up came from running layers 21–39 on 128 of every 8,192 chunk rows; the quality loss came from flooring the window at the tail start. The last 128 rows of an extend are the only late-layer rows decode can reach (window 128); their values at layer 39 depend on 128 × 19 earlier rows at layer 21. A tail of 128 × (19 + 1) = 2,560 rows with the same floor makes the reachable 128 rows exact at every late layer (equal to no-replay up to kernel batch-shape numerics) while still skipping 69 % of late-layer rows on an 8K chunk.

*Question.* With `tail_len = 2560`, is the teacher-forced Δlogp vs the default within ±0.002 on both shapes, and how much of E35's +39 % survives (expectation: roughly half; nothing on extends ≤ 2,560 tokens, i.e. nothing on chat turns)?

*Prerequisites (each a small overlay change).* (1) `tail_len` knob in `deepseek_v4_backend._build_late_layer_tail_metadata` (today hard-coded `SWA_WINDOW`); (2) the E22 contract for a *streamed* tail: 2,560 ≥ `KT_GPU_PREFILL_MIN_TOKENS` takes the streamed path at a different token count — a second pipeline case beyond E35's `abandon_forward` (which covers the CPU-path tail only; reapply `patches/overlay-kt-prefill-abandon-forward.patch` as the base); (3) move `_check_late_layer_tail_readers`' prompt-logprob refusal to the tokenizer manager (HTTP 400) — E35 showed the model-level `ValueError` kills the server; (4) promote `.amp/in/e35-tf-score.py` into `scripts/` as the gate (the corpus NLL cannot run under replay).

*Protocol.* One serve on the launcher default + `--enable-decoder-swa-bounded-replay` + the knob; the E35 battery (prefill 16K / 32K cold + warm, C1, C4, needles 64K, 4 × 62K stress, teacher-forced scorer vs `evidence/e35-bounded-replay/A/tf-score.json`). *Gates.* Scorer pooled ΔNLL ≤ +0.002 on fresh *and* extends (resolution ~±0.005 at 95 % with 2,558 positions — report the CI), needles 6/6, C1 wall ≥ 39.0, min free ≥ 641 / 582 MiB. *Go.* ≥ +15 % 16K prefill at the gate. *Stop.* Any shape outside the gate, or < +10 %.

Slot: candidate behind T3.27 (autotune) — E35-ii is worth ~+15–20 % of first-turn prefill only; owner decides whether the four overlay changes are worth it against T3.20 / T3.27.


## E36 — Engram row I/O ahead of use: early-issued deduplicated prefetch vs the lookup-time `MADV_WILLNEED` path (GPU; T3.20 part b)

**Status:** ~~registered 2026-10-08 from T3.32 ([TENSORFOLD-ASSESSMENT §2/§4](../docs/research/TENSORFOLD-ASSESSMENT-2026-10.md)); runs inside T3.20, after its break-time measurement.~~ **Done 2026-10-09 — go met** (T3.20 round 1; result below).

*Question.* `_FileTable.gather()` (overlay `engram.py:752`) advises the rows with `MADV_WILLNEED` from a thread pool only when the layer's Engram break runs, so each break pays one NVMe queue-depth-N round trip on a cold row (~1 ms for 48 rows, ~0.5 s for a 4K chunk, E18). Both Engram layers' row ids are a pure function of input ids (`EngramHasher`), known before layer 0. Does issuing the reads for layers 1 and 14 at step start — deduplicated across the batch and across the two TP ranks that map the same file — remove the cold-row latency from the step, and does bypassing the page cache (O_DIRECT + io_uring into a pinned staging buffer, as the TensorFold DeepSeek forks do) beat `MADV_WILLNEED` on this box, or does it just compete with kt-kernel for the 125 GB/s fabric (E25)?

*Protocol.* (1) Measure first on the current path: per-break wall (T3.19a timeline), `perf stat` page faults, NVMe latency and read amplification (`iostat -x` on the 9100 PRO), page-cache churn (`/proc/meminfo` Cached / Dirty deltas), DRAM-fabric counter during the kt CPU tier — on C1 and C4, cold (after `drop_caches`, owner-run) and warm. (2) Early-issue variant: compute both layers' ids in the step prologue, dedupe, `MADV_WILLNEED` immediately; same measurements. (3) O_DIRECT + io_uring variant into a fixed pinned buffer (this is the fixed-address buffer T3.20 part (a) wants anyway); same measurements plus the fabric counter during overlap.

*Gates.* NLL bit-identical (rows are rows); needles 6/6 at 64K; C1 wall and C4 per-stream ≥ T3.19 band. *Go.* Cold-step p99 ITL falls by the measured break latency, or long-prefill (64K / 262K) wall falls ≥ 5 %, with the fabric counter during the CPU tier unchanged. *Stop.* Warm-path gain < 1 % (the page cache was already doing the work — then only the cold case matters and it is bounded by (2)), or the O_DIRECT path raises CPU-tier time at C4 (bandwidth theft).

Evidence `evidence/t320-engram-prefetch/io/`. Ordering: inside T3.20 as registered; no change to the active sequence.

**Result (2026-10-09, overlay `ff516964`, `evidence/t320-engram-prefetch/`; README has the full table).** (1) *Measure first* became an idle-box microbench of the issue mechanism (`scripts/bench-engram-advise.py`, `io/README.md`): the served 16-thread pool was 2.4–3× slower than one thread cold and 30× slower warm (CPython's `mmap.madvise` holds the GIL); WILLNEED costs ≈ 0.24 / 0.5 µs per page warm / cold in the kernel whatever the issue path (ctypes, `process_madvise` ×1024 identical; the latter EPERM under Docker's seccomp); one 8192-token chunk ≈ 786K pages per layer = 0.5–0.6 s cold / 0.2 s warm; page faults alone 2–6× worse cold (E18 stands). The `perf stat` / `iostat` / fabric-counter rows were not taken: the microbench already located the cost (kernel readahead per page), and the served A/B below measures the only thing that matters, the break. (2) *Early issue* was built as `SGLANG_DSV41_ENGRAM_FILE_PREFETCH=ahead` (layer 14's rows advised and gathered on one worker from layer 1's break, one D2H for both layers' ids) with `sync` (serial, in the break) and `pool` (E18) as comparators. One serve each, same hour: decode Engram break bs 1 **2.20 → 1.80 → 1.66 ms** (layer 14's 0.94 → 0.45), bs 4 3.19 → 2.15; C1 wall 38.87 → 39.55 (+1.7 %); C4 +0.4 %; **prefill 16K / 32K cold 1,592 / 1,596 → 1,844 / 1,915 (+16 / +20 %), warm +26 / +24 %, TTFT −14 / −17 %** at the same ≈ 1.5 % residency; NLL 0 of 65,653 moved; needles 6/6; same-prompt PASS. (3) *O_DIRECT + io_uring* struck: the cost is kernel-side per page, not the syscall, and a pinned staging copy of two 94 GB tables has no home (the server leaves ≈ 60 GB). **Go met** on the long-prefill clause (≥ 5 % wall) with the quality gates bit-identical; the fabric counter was not sampled (C4 wall unchanged is the proxy). What the break still costs is layer 1's own fetch (≈ 0.8 ms of cold NVMe latency over the ≈ 0.45 ms break floor) — T3.20 round 2's target. **Round 2 (2026-10-09, `ad973440`, `ahead-r2b/`):** the issue moved to one post-hasher break on one shared worker; break 1.66 → 1.25 ms (−43 % vs pool), C1 wall 39.55 → 39.96, prefill held (1,811 / 1,910 cold), NLL 0 moved; see the README's round-2 table. E36 unchanged (go).

## E37 — kt CPU tier under page size, thread placement and compiler flags: is there anything left on the host side of the per-row expert stream? (GPU one-layer + four serves; T3.36, T3.40, T1.6)

- Status: **DONE 2026-10-11 — null on every axis; two placements are large losses; nothing adopted.** `evidence/t336/README.md`, `evidence/t340/`.
- Hypotheses: (H-TLB) 4 KiB pages on the 136 GiB expert arena cost TLB walks the DRAM stream does not hide → 2 MiB pages buy a few %; (H-CCD) worker placement (one CCD vs both, physical cores vs SMT) changes the achieved fabric bandwidth; (H-CG) `-march=native` (znver5 scheduling) lets the compiler do better than the wheel's explicit AVX-512 flag set on the hand-written intrinsics. The sidecar assessment's `numactl membind` is Grace evidence; this box has one NUMA node, so NUMA policy reduces to CCD placement.
- Method: `test-kt-one-layer.py --bench-cold` (layer 5, 219 / 165, 3 CPU slots per token, T = 1…256, 40 iters, 2 reps) under 6 settings — baseline, `GLIBC_TUNABLES=glibc.malloc.hugetlb=1` (madvises THP on malloc'd chunks: 2.79 of 3.5 GiB arena on huge pages, verified from `smaps_rollup`), `--cpuset-cpus 0-15` (physical cores only), both, CCD0-only with 8 workers, CCD0 + THP — plus the recipe vs `-march=native` images; then four serves at the 262K × 4 defaults in one hour (`a` baseline, `b` THP, `c` native, `a2` baseline as order control) with decode C1 / C4, prefill 16K / 32K, `kt-cpu-per-step.sh`, the NLL corpus and an offline per-token identity check (`evidence/t336/analysis/identity.py`).
- Result: THP ≤ 1 % at every T; **physical-cores-only is 10× worse at T=1 (6–7 ms vs 0.58)** and +30 % at T=256 — kt's busy-spinning workers starve the dispatch thread when it has no SMT sibling to float to (T3.39 H2's mechanism); **one CCD / 8 workers is exactly 2× slower at every T** (per-CCD fabric, E25 confirmed from the other side); native identical within 1 %. Served, against `a2`: THP C1 +0.5 %, C4 +1.8 %, prefill 0 / +1 %; native C1 −0.9 %, C4 +0.6 %, prefill −1.6 / +0.3 %; kt CPU-s per step equal to three digits; NLL 0 of 65,653 moved for both. The four serves' C1 band is 59.7–61.3 (±1.3 %), so neither variable leaves the noise. Two facts for the record: since K2 the served arena is `memfd` shmem and `shmem_enabled = never`, so THP on the served arena is root-only (`shmem_enabled`, or `MFD_HUGETLB` + `nr_hugepages`) — not requested, because the one-layer test where THP *did* cover the arena is null; and kt-kernel's documented `CPUINFER_NATIVE` is unread at the pinned commit, `CPUINFER_CPU_INSTRUCT=NATIVE` is the real switch (now build ARG `KT_CPU_INSTRUCT`). Side finding: the first serve after an idle box read prefill 15–26 % above the next three (same prompts, same hour) — box state; the order control caught it.
- Pass: ≥ +3 % C1 wall for any setting (T3.36); NLL bit-identical then any speed (T3.40). **Neither met; T3.36, T3.40 and T1.6 closed.**
- Decision fed: launcher stays unpinned and on 4 KiB pages; the image keeps the wheel's `AVX512` flag set; LEARNING §11 rows (SMT starvation, per-CCD 2×, THP null, native null).

## E38 — Where does the bs=1 greedy drift come from, and can it be switched off? (offline probes + three serves; T3.15b)

- Status: **DONE 2026-10-11 — two sources, both GPU reduction order, both lever-able; bit-reproducible greedy decode at zero measured cost. Defaults unchanged pending T3.15c.** `evidence/t315b/README.md`. (E39 qualified the levers twice and found a third, kernel-side residual past ~14K tokens: T3.15d.)
- Question: every quality gate is teacher-forced and bit-stable across serves, but bs=1 greedy continuations of one prompt diverge after 320–390 characters (E21 result 7, T3.15 at D=0 too). Which tier is non-reproducible, and is it the deferral, the CPU tier, NCCL, the MoE runner or attention?
- Method: (1) offline, inside the image: kt-kernel layer-5 CPU tier replayed 20× at T=1…64 with 16 and 8 threads (`scripts/test-kt-determinism.py`); the overlay JIT `topk_v2` indexer top-k 30× per length (`evidence/t315b/offline/topk-v2-probe.py`); the SM120 sparse decode MLA on a fixed index list and on the same list permuted (`mla-probe.py`). (2) serves on the launcher defaults, `scripts/greedy-drift.py` (5 repeats per prompt, radix cache flushed before each, temperature 0, per-token logprobs): A defaults; B `SGLANG_FLASHINFER_MOE_FUSED_FINALIZE=0`; C B + overlay `e07379c6` `SGLANG_DSV41_SORT_DECODE_TOPK=1` (descending sort of each decode row's sparse slots, padding last). C also ran C1 / C4 / prefill 16K / 32K / needles 64K / corpus NLL.
- Result: kt tier **bit-identical** on every repeat and 8 threads == 16 threads; NCCL excluded (a+b commutative at TP2); `--enable-deterministic-inference` refuses to start (`dsv4` not an allowed backend) and is ignored by the backend anyway. `topk_v2` returns the same 512-slot SET past 512 tokens but in a run-dependent ORDER (29/29), and the sparse decode kernel's split-K reduction changes ~2,300 of 32,768 outputs under a permutation. Serve A: short prompts (23–28 tokens) diverge at token 16 / 32 / 41 / 52 (max |Δlogp| on the shared prefix 0.27–0.62), also with the prefix cache kept (4 / 17 / 27 / 34); a 3,715-token prompt at 57. Serve B: **all four short prompts bit-identical ×5**; the long prompt still diverges at 63 (the top-k order). Serve C: **bit-identical at 23, 28, 3,715 and 14,775 prompt tokens.** B and C emit the same tokens on short prompts (the sort is a no-op under 512) and A differs from B/C inside A's own repeat spread. Cost: C1 61.0 (day band 59.7–61.3), C4 123.5 (118.7–121.4), prefill 16K 3,183–3,192 / 32K 3,406–3,489 (band 3,150–3,683 / 3,400–4,292), needles 6/6 at 63,563 tokens TTFT 14.5 s, 0 health hits. Corpus NLL vs the 219/8192 anchor: pooled Δ **−0.00033 ± 0.00101** (z +0.33) but **65,226 of 65,653 tokens changed** — `FUSED_FINALIZE=0` is a different (unfused) reduction in prefill too, not the fused one made deterministic.
- Pass: name the source(s) with a toggle that removes the drift. **Met.**
- Decision fed: T3.15c registered (qualification serve on the defaults with both levers, corpus replicate for cross-serve bit-stability, then the owner flips the default); LEARNING §11 rows; BENCHMARKS quality-gate note (greedy diffs are usable evidence only with both levers on); patch 0053 in `patches/sglang/`. Nothing in the served defaults changed.


## E39 — Do the two determinism levers hold up on the full release gate set, and what is left once they are on? (two serves + one offline probe; T3.15c)

- Status: **DONE 2026-10-11 — every gate passes twice at zero cost; one residual named and reproduced offline (the top-k kernel's tie-break), split out as T3.15d. Flipped to the launcher default 2026-10-11 on owner approval: registry knobs, one re-measure on the plain launcher passes every gate with a corpus bit-identical to serve 2 (`evidence/t315c/default-flip/`).** `evidence/t315c/README.md`.
- Question: E38 showed the two levers remove the drift on one serve with light traffic. Do they survive the release smoke on the launcher shape (262K × 4 stress, needles at 255K, corpus, C1 / C4 / prefill / power), is the corpus NLL bit-stable across serves on the unfused path, and does bit-identity hold after real allocator churn?
- Method: serve 1 on overlay `e07379c6` (patch 0053, sorts physical KV slots) → `release-smoke.sh --context 262144 --slots 4 --full`, then `greedy-drift.py` (23–28, 3,715 and 14,775-token prompts, 5 repeats each). The 14,775-token prompt jittered after the smoke, so the sort was moved ahead of the slot map (overlay `743f8fa9` = patch 0054: select logical positions with `page_table=None`, sort, then `filter_topk_pages`) and serve 2 repeated the smoke with drift probes before and after it. Then offline, `offline/topk-v2-ties-probe.py`: `topk_transform_paged_v2` 30× on synthetic scores with the top-512 boundary inside an exact tie (relu-style zeros), with > 2,048 distinct values in one fp16 bin, and a tie-free control.
- Result: smoke **PASS × 2** — C1 58.1 / 57.5, C4 121.9 / 121.1, prefill 16K 3,327 / 3,996 (TTFT 4.8 / 4.0 s), 32K 3,415 / 4,215, needles 6/6 at 255,388 tokens, stress 4 × 260,096 in 328 / 286 s, corpus Δ −0.00033 ± 0.00101 (z +0.33) and **0 of 65,653 values moved** serve C → 1 → 2, power 424 W busy / 84 °C at C1, health 0, Xid 0. Drift: short and 3,715-token prompts **bit-identical ×5 and across all three serves**; 14,775 tokens **tokens identical in all 20 runs**, logprobs identical in 14, the other 6 (2/5, 1/5, 2/5 per probe) differ from decode token 109 on (max |Δlogp| 0.012–0.055) into one of three alternative vectors — serve C's 5/5 was the modal outcome five times. Patch 0054 changed nothing about it. Offline: with the boundary inside a tie the SET moves 28–29/29 at every length from 1,000 to 262,144 (588 tied zeros already do it); > 2,048 scores in one fp16 bin is inexact (and moving at 2,049 and 65,536); the cluster path returns an inexact, moving top-512 for 262,144 distinct scores; the tie-free control is stable and exact. Cause in `topk_impl.cuh`: `radix_tie_select` assigns final-round ties by `atomicAdd` arrival order and the collect pass caps the threshold bin at `kMaxNumTie = 2048`; upstream `d62b9c24` is identical, open as sgl-project/sglang #35257.
- Pass: every release gate on the launcher shape with both levers, corpus bit-stable across serves, no measured cost. **Met.** Bit-identity at every length: not met past ~14K tokens — the remaining source is in the kernel, outside the levers.
- Decision fed: recommend flipping both levers to the launcher default (owner decision; approved and done 2026-10-11 — `SGLANG_FLASHINFER_MOE_FUSED_FINALIZE` default `0`, `SGLANG_DSV41_SORT_DECODE_TOPK` default `1` in `serve_config.py`, new headline + identity anchor `evidence/t315c/default-flip/`; the post-stress prefill gap seen there is T3.47); T3.15d registered (deterministic `(value, idx)` final-round rank + a further radix pass instead of the cap, cluster path too; JIT header, probe-driven; posting to #35257 is an owner call); patch 0054 kept (allocator-independent order is the right contract even though it did not move the residual); LEARNING §11 row; TROUBLESHOOTING residual note; BENCHMARKS quality-gate note. Host runtime recorded from here on: docker 29.9.0 / containerd v2.4.1.

## E40 — Where does the Engram page cache go after a serve, and what does the first request pay? (GPU-free replay + three serves; T3.10 / T3.47)

- Status: **DONE 2026-10-11 — the serve-to-serve prefill spread (T3.47) and the first-request anomaly have two separate causes, both fixed in the recipe without a serve knob; the Engram A/B of E11 is rejected with evidence; headline prefill 16K 3,311 → 4,248 tok/s and the 4 × 260K stress 320 → 257 s on the same shape (`evidence/t310/`).**
- Question: the Engram tables (188.8 GiB, E18 `file` layout) live in the page cache the kt CPU experts (136 GiB shm) leave on a 246 GiB host. How much of a real workload's rows ever fit, does a denser table (E11's FP4 / q3_K) help, why does the headline prefill move 20–35 % between serves (T3.47), and why is a serve's first request 19 s slower than its second?
- Method: (1) `scripts/engram-working-set.py` replays the serve's `compute_engram_hash_ids` (token map, multipliers, primes; layers 1 and 14; 24 rows per token per layer; 264 B rows, 16 weight or 512 scale rows per 4 KiB page) over the NLL corpus (66K tokens) and the release-smoke traffic (262K × 4, 1.48 M tokens) and reports distinct rows, touched pages and a request-level LRU hit curve. (2) `scripts/bench-engram-warmth.py`: the same prompt three times (radix flushed between), `fincore` of the Engram shards before and after each pass, at 16K and 32K on two real texts and one synthetic. (3) `posix_fadvise(DONTNEED)` on the 46 non-Engram shards from the host after ready, then the warmth bench and the full release smoke. (4) The container log around the first request (`triton_load_watch`).
- Result: **(a) Working set.** Real text: 65 % distinct rows, ~1.02 rows per touched weight page, **~190 KiB of page cache per first-touched token** (12 GiB for 66K tokens); 2-gram columns 44 % distinct, 4-gram 82 %. Release-smoke traffic: 9.9 M pages = 37.8 GiB per layer, **75.6 GiB both**, 54 KiB/token (the stress alone 32.4 GiB per layer); request-level LRU 38 % at 16 GiB → 55 % at 86 GiB. So a re-quantized table cannot help: the cost is one page per row touched, not the row's bytes. **(b) The page cache was pinned.** Right after a serve the Engram shards had ~0 resident; during the first warmth run they plateaued at 9.4–10 GiB with evictions (`loaded −91,241 pages` on a 32K prompt) because ~73–75 GiB of non-Engram shard pages from weight load (read by both TP ranks, never read again) sat in front of them beside the 136 GiB shm. `fadvise(DONTNEED)` freed 73 GiB in 2.8 s (no root); after it Engram residency grew monotonically — 27 GiB after two warm runs, 80 GiB after the stress — and the smoke read **prefill 16K 4,401 (TTFT 3.64 s) vs 3,311, 32K 4,278 vs 3,480, stress 262 s vs 320 s, prefill power 245–250 W per GPU vs ~200–235**, every gate PASS, corpus bit-identical. Shipped as the `wait-ready.py` default (`--keep-shard-cache`); the plain-launcher re-measure `default-wait-ready/` (16K 4,248 / 32K 4,232, stress 257 s, C1 57.4, C4 124.3, needles 6/6, 12/12 gates) is the new headline row. SGLang's own `--weight-loader-drop-cache-after-load` would drop each shard as its rank finishes it, while the other rank and the kt loader may still read it; the host-side drop after ready avoids that re-read and is what was measured. **(c) Cold vs warm Engram** costs +1.7–3.1 s per request (+24–66 % of the warm TTFT at 16K–32K; 16K real text 6.9–7.9 s cold vs 4.7–4.8 s warm) and is flat in prompt length — the first chunk's NVMe fetch, which the chunk-ahead prefetch cannot hide (T3.49). **(d) The first request** of a serve was 23.8 and 25.1 s on two serves (+19–20 s over cold-Engram) with RssShmem already 142.7 GB per rank, so not shm faults: the log shows `_hc_mix_reduce_sinkhorn_kernel` taking 13.75 / 14.15 s to compile after serving started, the `FlashInfer MoE: dedicated runner for 165 experts` built on that request, and a dozen Triton device-loads at 0.25–0.7 GiB free — all triggered by the first chunk-sized prefill, which the server's 256-token warmup never reaches, on a `/root/.cache/sglang` (Triton 30 MB, CUDA JIT 393 MB, DeepGEMM, FlashInfer autotune) that died with the container. Fixed by `KERNEL_CACHE_DIR` (host dir mounted at `/root/.cache/sglang`, registry knob, empty = ephemeral) plus a chunk-plus-tail prime prefill in `wait-ready.py` (`--no-prime`); `prime-serve-a/` (empty cache) and `prime-serve-b/` (warm cache) in `evidence/t310/`: prime 22.3 s vs 7.3 s, launch→ready 5m04 vs 3m36, ready probe 13.9 s vs 0.23 s, and the first user request (16K real text, cold Engram) 7.8 s on serve A — the +19 s is gone; `_w8a8_block_fp8_matmul` still device-loads new tile configs for other prompt lengths (sub-second, no OOM at 0.16 GiB free), so the prime removes the compile, not every lazy load.
- Pass: a measured cause for T3.47 with a fix that holds on the full gate set — **met** (two serves); E11's A/B answered — **met, negative**; first-request anomaly named — **met**.
- Decision fed: T3.10 and T3.47 closed; GitHub #2 closed; T3.48 (user-space row cache) and T3.49 (admission-time chunk-0 prefetch) registered low; BENCHMARKS §memory corrected (the "pages stay in the 94 GB page cache" sentence was false); headline row `evidence/t310/default-wait-ready/`; TROUBLESHOOTING entries for slow prefill at ~200 W and for the first-request stall; `release-smoke.sh` identity anchor unchanged (corpus bit-identical).

## E41 — Does an exact, deterministic decode top-k remove the 14,775-token greedy residual, and what does it cost? (offline probes + one serve; T3.15d)

- Status: **DONE 2026-10-11 — the kernel is exact and deterministic at zero serve cost (merged as patch 0055, new headline row), and the residual is NOT the top-k: it survives unchanged, and the indexer logits are bit-identical too.** `evidence/t315d/README.md`.
- Question: E39 reproduced a tie-dependent, truncated top-k offline and attributed the 14,775-token residual (6 of 20 runs differ in logprobs from decode token 109, tokens identical) to it. Does fixing the kernel remove the residual, and does an exact kernel cost decode throughput?
- Method: rewrite `topk_impl.cuh` (overlay JIT header, no sgl-kernel rebuild): 64-bit key `exact_bin << 32 | ~idx` so the final radix round orders ties by `(value desc, idx asc)`; per-round byte refinement instead of the 2,048-candidate truncation in both the register / streaming (`refine_and_collect`) and persistent-cluster (`cluster_refine`) paths; constant-bit detection; collect fused into the last pass; `radix_tie_select` two-pass; histogram adds warp-aggregated with shfl + `__all_sync` (v3; v4's `__match_any_sync` ~2× slower on dense-bin rows, plain atomics ~15 % slower on uniform rows). Offline on GPU 0 inside the image: `topk-v2-exact-probe.py` (stock vs v1–v4, 30 repeats per case, exact multiset check, timings) and `topk-v2-ties-probe.py` (E39's 38 tie cases). Then one plain-launcher serve on the worktree (served tree = `a8804318`): release smoke 262K × 4 `--full`, `greedy-drift.py --long-tokens 3000 12000` fresh and after traffic, corpus vs `t315c/default-flip`. Then `indexer-logits-probe.py`: `deep_gemm.fp8_fp4_paged_mqa_logits` replayed 30× at L = 700 … 262,147 on fixed inputs.
- Result: **kernel**: all four variants exact and stable on every case (38/38 "differs 0/29"; stock wrong in every tied case); v3 timings stock | v3 µs — zero-tie 262K 64 | 199, 65K 25 | 60, 15K 14 | 22, tie × 2049 262K 43 | 118, dense bin 262K 64 | 200, randn 262K 41 | 40 (tie-free rows cost nothing; the worst case is ≈ 0.14 ms of a 17 ms step). **Serve A** 12/12 PASS: C1 60.13 / C4 124.79, prefill 16K 4,361 (TTFT 3.68 s) / 32K 4,513 (7.10 s), stress 273.8 s, needles 6/6 at 255,388 (TTFT 59.5 s), corpus bit-identical to the anchor (0 of 65,653 moved), health 0 — every number at or above the previous headline (57.4 / 124.3 / 4,248 / 4,232 / 257 s; Engram residency at smoke start 32.8 % vs 42.5 %). **Residual**: fresh serve 3/5 bit-identical at 14,775 tokens, 2 runs differ from decode token 109 (max |Δlogp| 0.0507, tokens identical); after traffic 5/5 — the same picture as E39's 14/20. **Indexer logits**: bit-identical 30× at every length, exact ties at the k-th score 0.
- Pass: 0/29 set moves and an exact multiset on every tie case — **met**; `--long-tokens 12000` bit-identical × 5 after traffic — met after traffic, **not met fresh** (3/5), so the residual's source is elsewhere; C1 / C4 unchanged — **met**.
- Decision fed: patch 0055 merged (correctness fix, the qualified serve runs it), headline row → `evidence/t315d/serve-a`; E39's attribution corrected in LEARNING / TROUBLESHOOTING; excluded as the residual's source: kt tier, `topk_v2`, sparse MLA on a fixed list, indexer logits, MoE finalize, KV allocator; T3.15e registered (capturer-based step-109 set comparison); posting the reproduction + fix to sgl-project/sglang #35257 stays an owner call.

## E42 — Which step-109 state differs on the 14,775-token greedy residual, and why? (three recorder serves + offline replays; T3.15e)

- Status: **DONE 2026-10-11 — the selected SET differs: one exact fp32 score tie at the #512 / #513 boundary of layer 36's decode top-k, resolved as 10595 instead of 4971 on one TP rank on ~1–2 of 15 repeats in production only; the same row through the same kernel offline never flips.** `evidence/t315e/README.md`.
- Question: E41 showed the residual (logprobs differ from decode token 109 on a minority of repeats, tokens identical) survives an exact top-k and bit-identical indexer logits. Which decode-path stage differs first — x (upstream MoE / residual), logits (indexer GEMM), sel (top-k), slots (KV allocator), q, or o (sparse attention) — and what makes it differ?
- Method: overlay opt-in recorder (`SGLANG_DSV41_STEP_FINGERPRINT_DIR`, patches 0056–0058, zero cost when unset) writes per-rank order-independent fingerprints (bit-pattern sum / sum of squares) of the six stages for every decode step and layer, graph-capturable; `scripts/fingerprint-diff.py` aligns repeats of the drift prompt and names the first differing (step, layer, stage). Serve 1: 5 fresh + 15 repeats. Serves 2 / 3: `..._TRIGGER_SEQ_LEN` = 7,442 / 14,884 dump the full top-k input row and selected set of every indexer layer at the divergent step (ring of 128). Offline: `trigger-narrow.py` (per-layer set / boundary / tail analysis), `tie-bin-count.py`, `topk-v2-oob-probe.py` (index-range check), `topk-v2-replay-probe.py` (captured row through `topk_transform_paged_v2` 12,800× across tails, windows, output pre-fills, batch sizes and synthetic tails).
- Result: serve 1 `drift-fresh` 5/5 bit-identical, `drift-x15` 1/15 differs (max |Δlogp| 0.0507); `fingerprint-diff`: the differing repeat's first difference is **step 108, layer 36, stage `sel` on rank 1**, and step 108 layer 37 stage `q` on rank 0 (carried over by the layer-36 all-reduce). Serve 2 (7,442 = ratio-2 layers 2 / 8 / 14): all identical. Serve 3 (14,884 = ratio-1 layers 20 … 36): layers 20 / 24 / 28 / 32 identical × 15 × 2 ranks; **layer 36 rank 0 picks 10595 on repeats 1 and 15, 4971 on the other 13; rank 1 picks 4971 × 15**. The row is bit-identical on all 15 repeats and both ranks; positions 4971 and 10595 are an exact fp32 tie at −1.1060476 (#511 −1.1055861, #514 −1.1072798; 16 values in fp16 bin 3014 → the ≤ 32-candidate warp tie path); every candidate index is in range; the tail past L (zeros vs 19 stale values) does not correlate with the flip. Offline replay: 4971 on all 12,800 calls, exact, zero flips.
- Pass: first differing stage named — **met** (`sel`, layer 36, step 108); cause named — **met** (exact tie at the boundary, rank-dependent resolution in production); production-only factor named — **not met** (graph replay with PDL on the persistent-cluster build, concurrent side streams, SM120 plan path remain; the streaming-path code read shows no race).
- Decision fed: T3.15e closed; not a quality gate (tokens identical in E38 / E39 / E41 / E42; bound one tied KV position in one layer at one step) and not a performance item; open upstream-facing finding (candidate follow-up to sgl-project/sglang #35257, owner call); recorder patches kept as opt-in diagnostics (series 58); `greedy-drift.py` retries `/flush_cache` while the previous request retires. Queue continues: T3.9h-b → T3.41.

## E43 — Does n-gram speculation pay on the DSV4 hybrid once it runs, and is its output correct? (five serves; T3.9h-b)

- Status: **DONE 2026-10-12 — it runs (patch 0059), it wins only on prompt-copying work (+60 / +76 % C1 on copy-code / copy-table) and loses everywhere else (−26 % review, −63 % C4), and its decode state is wrong under rejected draft rows; `NGRAM` stays off and is documented as not recommended.** `evidence/t39hb/README.md`.
- Question: E23 R6 crashed (`move_kv_cache` missing on the DSV4 pool). With a chain-only verify (no KV move when the draft is a front chain, C128 slots of rejected rows cleared), does NGRAM reach the go bar (≥ +10 % wall on copy-heavy, ≤ 5 % loss on review), and does its output agree with the model?
- Method: overlay `0887a342` (`ngram_worker.py`: `chainify_drafts()` refuses breadth ≠ 1, skips the KV move, calls `clear_unaccepted_c128_draft_states`). Serve A spec-off and serve B `NGRAM=1 NGRAM_DRAFT_TOKENS=5 NGRAM_BREADTH=1` on the launcher default: `verify.py`, `bench-decode.py` C1 on review / copy-code / copy-table + C4, `greedy-drift.py --long-tokens 12000`, `nll-score.py`. New probe `scripts/spec-consistency.py`: greedy `/generate` with logprobs, then the prompt + output re-scored under teacher forcing; argmax agreement, flips > 0.5 nats, max |decode − forced|; `--flush-cache` resets the prefix cache and the n-gram corpus. Serves C / D: `SGLANG_BCG_DEBUG_EAGER=1` (no-op — registry typed it `flag`; the overlay reads `draft|target`) and `=target` (eager target runner, logged on both ranks). Serve A2: the probe's spec-off baseline.
- Result: **throughput** C1 review 62.84 → 46.58 (min 30.12), copy-code 53.71 → 85.86, copy-table 51.76 → 91.06, C4 122.60 → 45.90 (wall 117.63 → 39.29); accept 1.1–1.8 cold, ≈ 5 warm. **Gates** all pass on both serves: verify ALL PASS (needles 6 / 6 at 255,388), drift bitwise, NLL 1.4875254 bit-identical. **Probe** (cold corpus): copy-table emits token 8061 at output position 20 (a seventh `|---` in a six-column table) at ≈ 0 nats where teacher forcing scores it −20.5 (argmax 22301); review 354 / 384 with 8 flips > 0.5 nats (max 3.1); long12000 368 / 384, 6 flips; copy-code clean. Deterministic across three serves (bit-identical numbers), clean with a warm corpus (384 / 384, max |Δlogp| 0.000), and **unchanged with the target runner eager**. Spec-off baseline: copy-table 384 / 384 (max 0.00015), review 366 / 384 with 1 flip (0.625), long12000 374 / 384 with 1 flip (0.75).
- Pass: copy-heavy ≥ +10 % — **met** (+53–73 % wall); review ≤ 5 % loss — **not met** (−25 %); correctness (probe within the spec-off baseline) — **not met**.
- Decision fed: `NGRAM` stays `0`, knob doc says not recommended with the numbers; patch 0059 stays (inert unless `NGRAM=1`); `SGLANG_BCG_DEBUG_EAGER` re-typed `str`; T3.9h-c (the state fix: c4 indexer compress states / SWA paged ring / seq_lens for rejected rows, diffed against DSpark's verify) registered low, not scheduled; `spec-consistency.py` is the gate any future spec path must pass — NLL, drift and needles cannot see this class of defect. Queue continues: T3.41.

## E44 — Does a decode-shape FP8 GEMV move the served step, and why is C4 bimodal? (one sweep + two serves; T3.41)

- Status: **DONE 2026-10-12 — the GEMV wins 23/28 (shape, M) cells by +3…+51 % per call (+12…+51 % at M = 1) (M=1 16384×1280 26.9 → 14.5 µs, 25600×6144 201 → 99 µs) but the served step moves ≈ +1–2 % at C1 and 0 at C4, inside the serve-to-serve band; `SGLANG_DSV41_FP8_GEMV` ships opt-in (default 0, patch 0060). The control serve found the real C4 lever: floating host threads land on the 16 CPUs kt-kernel's workers own, and C4 then reads 106 instead of 123 tok/s; pinning every non-kt thread to the SMT siblings gives C4 125.1 / 125.3 and C1 60.4 with the slow mode gone — registered as T3.52.** `evidence/t341/`.
- Question: T3.27 window 1 put the seven dense W8A8 block-32 GEMMs at ≈ 20 % of the 17.3 ms C1 step, streaming weights at 428–748 GB/s against a 1,470 GB/s roof. Does an FMA row-dot GEMV at M ≤ 4 (ceiling ≤ +9 % C1, realistically +4–6 %) pay in the served step?
- Method: `kernels/t341/fp8_gemv_block32.py` — Triton `tl.dot`-free GEMV (one CTA per ≤ 32 output columns so the rows share one block-scale column, K in contiguous slices, both scales folded into the activation, M as a constexpr loop, optional SPLIT_K through SGLang's existing reduce), 423 configs × 7 shapes × M = 1–4, L2-cold, in-graph, median of 30 replays, every candidate checked against the FP32 dequantised reference and the max |diff| vs the E20 Triton winner recorded; winners (spread < 1 µs across the top 5) written to a per-(N, K, M) table that the overlay (`d00e8e30`, patch 0060) consults behind `SGLANG_DSV41_FP8_GEMV`. Serve A = launcher default + `SGLANG_DSV41_FP8_GEMV=1` (`evidence/t341/serve-gemv/`): C1 (6 runs) / C2 / C3 / C4 with reruns, NLL vs `t328` and vs the identity anchor, same-prompt C4, and the new decode-numerics gate `spec-consistency.py --flush-cache --compare evidence/t39hb/a2-spec-off/consistency-cold.json`. Serve B = the stock control (`SGLANG_DSV41_FP8_GEMV=0`, `evidence/t341/serve-stock/`): the same decode benches, then the thread-affinity probe (forced collision, then every non-`numa_*` thread pinned to CPUs 16–31 with `taskset` from the host), decode and 16K prefill A/B/A/B with a fresh seed per pass.
- Result: **sweep** 23/28 accepted (3 at M = 4: the FMA kernel loses its edge once the tensor-core tile amortises); per call +12…+51 %, 1.1–1.45 TB/s effective. **Serve A** C1 60.70 / 58.54, C2 86.79, C3 100.56 / 99.06, C4 108.24 / 107.77 (one run 123.6); same-prompt PASS; NLL 0 of 65,653 moved vs the anchor (expected — the GEMV never runs in prefill, so the corpus cannot see it); decode numerics: argmax agreement 1.000 / 1.000 / 0.964 / 0.974 vs the spec-off baseline 1.000 / 1.000 / 0.953 / 0.974, flips > 0.5 nats 0 / 0 / 1 / 2 vs 0 / 0 / 1 / 1, same max |Δlogp| magnitude → clean. **Serve B** C4 106.04 then 123.55, 123.63, 122.54; C1 59.48. So **C4 is bimodal (≈ 106 or ≈ 123) on both serves independent of the flag**; the GEMV's serve-level effect is C1 +1–2 % (60.7 / 58.5 vs 59.5 / 60.4), C4 within noise. **Affinity probe on serve B:** kt-kernel's 16 workers (`numa_0_t_1..15`, `numa_0_m_0`) are pinned by hwloc to logical CPUs 0–15 (one per physical core); the TP0 scheduler, the ~100 % spinning TP1 main thread, the tokenizer and the HTTP thread float on `0xffffffff`. Forcing TP0 main onto cpu5 and TP1 main onto cpu2 → **C4 33.80**. Pinning every non-kt thread to `0xffff0000` (the SMT siblings) → **C4 125.05 / 125.29, C1 60.38 (60.19–60.54)**; 16K prefill with fresh seeds pinned 3,711 / 3,812 vs floating 3,789 / 3,859 (−1 to −2 %, inside the monotone warm-up drift of the four passes; the first "unpinned 4,620" reading reused a seed and was a radix-cache hit — invalid, kept as a note).
- Pass: C1 ≥ +2 % with NLL / numerics clean — **not met** (+1–2 %, inside the band); numerics — **met**; bit-identical — **not applicable by design** (FMA vs MMA order; gated by the decode-numerics probe instead, as E20 was by NLL).
- Decision fed: `SGLANG_DSV41_FP8_GEMV` stays `0` (knob documented with the served numbers; flip only if a pinned paired A/B reads ≥ +2 % C1). The C4 bimodality that confused T3.37's and T3.15c's soaks (110 / 123 spread "following the prompt set") has a mechanism and a zero-code fix: **T3.52** (a `HOST_THREAD_CPUS` launcher knob that starts the server under `taskset` on the kt workers' SMT siblings; qualification serve then an owner default flip). Lesson for every bench: one `bench-prefill.py` seed per serve and size — repeating a seed hits the radix cache.

## E45 — What does the "61/61 exact and stable" probe prove, and is the exact `topk_v2` right on the paths it never ran? (GPU-free review + forced-cluster test runs on GPU 0; T3.51)

- Status: **DONE 2026-10-12 — the probe proved exactness on the register / streaming refinement only (batch 1, k = 512, no page table; the paths SM120 compiles). The cluster refinement (`cluster_refine`, SM90 / SM100) was wrong in two ways and is fixed: patched main forced-cluster 156 failed → 302 passed, default 302 passed, probe 72/72 exact and stable forced (stock forced 13/72). Overlay `deec7a6f` = patch 0061; the #35257 gist v2 is regenerated from it, posting is the owner's.** `evidence/t351/`.
- Question: item (0) of T3.51 — the independent review (`deepseek-v41-derivative-lab` `c3a6413`) asked what the probe behind the draft comment actually covered. Upstream compiles `cluster_refine` on SM90 / SM100 only (`SUPPORT_CLUSTER`, SM120 excluded for "poor cluster performance"), so every probe, test and serve here had exercised `refine_and_collect` alone while the patch rewrote both paths. Can SM120 test the cluster path at all, and does it pass?
- Method: force `#define SUPPORT_CLUSTER (SGL_CUDA_ARCH >= 900)` in `topk_v2.cuh` (device probe: `MAX_C8_OCC2=46`, `MAX_C16_OCC1=10`); run upstream `test_topk_v2.py` on the stock kernel forced (validity of the gate), on the patched kernel forced and default; `topk-v2-exact-probe.py` (72 cases: ReLU zero-tie, shared-value, dense-bin, randn rows at L = 3…262,147, L = 20,000 added for the streaming range) stock vs patched forced; printf instrumentation of the refine rounds (requires disabling the `enable_smem_spilling` pragma) to localise the defect; then the new `test_topk_v2_exact_under_ties` (15 cases on each dispatch template) against stock and fixed trees, and the whole suite on the overlay tree.
- Result: stock forced **288 passed** — the SM120 exclusion is about speed, so forcing is a valid correctness gate. Patched forced **156 failed / 147 passed**: (1) `cluster_refine` folded every warp's key AND / OR with 64-bit `atomicAnd` / `atomicOr` over DSMEM into one word in rank 0's smem and lost updates (AND returned `…ffff0400` where no candidate had bit 10 set), so rounds could not narrow, the collect emptied and `handle_tie` padded 0…511 or dropped / duplicated lowest-index survivors; (2) peers read rank 0's final `count_gt` / `count_eq` after the third barrier while rank 0 could have exited (small-batch INDICES mode — production's path) or begun its next item (persistent kernel) → "unspecified launch failure" in `test_topk_v2_ragged[False-64-persistent-*]`. Fix: per-rank local fold + one published slot per rank, reduced once by rank 0 after the barrier; counts zeroed after the first barrier wait; every cluster kernel ends on a cluster barrier. After the fix: forced **302 passed / 19 skipped**, default **302 passed**, probe **72/72 exact, 0 moves** (stock forced: 59 of 72 wrong or moving); the regression test fails 14/14 on stock. Forced-cluster cost on SM120, stock → patched µs/call: randn 262,147 25.2 → 27.3, 65,537 22.1 → 24.2 (tie-free rows ≈ +2 µs); overflow rows (zero tie, 2,049+ shared, dense bin) 15–25 → 27–102 — against ≈ 200 µs for the streaming refinement at 262,147 (E41), so the cluster path is the cheaper exact one where it compiles. Overlay tree with patch 0061: 288 + 14 passed.
- Pass: the gate (stock forced passes upstream's suite) — **met**; patched passes forced and default — **met after the fix**; regression test catches stock — **met**; production impact on this recipe — **none** (SM120 never ran the cluster path; the served kernel's register / streaming path is unchanged and bit-identical to patch 0055's).
- Decision fed: the #35257 gist v2 (`.amp/in/t351/gist-v2/`, not public) now carries the cluster fix, the regression test, `REPEATS=10`, the per-call vs per-step cost split (8 indexer top-k calls per step per rank), no NLL / "unchanged within noise" / "common case" claims, forced-SM120 as the stated cluster evidence with an explicit request for an H100 / B200 run; the patch applies with `git am` on upstream main `e6072935` to an identical tree; trailers and the internal thread link are scrubbed. Lesson: a path that a GPU does not compile is still testable if forcing the compile keeps the stock kernel green — do that before claiming a rewrite of it is exact. Posting is the owner's; T3.52 is next.
